To demonstrate how multi-field inflation is constrained by swampland conjectures, we decompose the potential into a tangential and a normal component

V^{a}=T^{a}V_{\phi}+N^{a}V_{N}

(86)

•

the tangential is defined as
T^{a}=\dot{\phi}^{a}_{0}/\dot{\phi}_{0}
, with
\dot{\phi}_{0}=\sqrt{G_{ab}\dot{\phi}^{a}_{0}\dot{\phi}^{b}_{0}}
, using the scalar field space metric
G_{ab}

•

V_{\phi}
corresponds to the projection of the gradient along the inflationary trajectory

•

the normal component is defined as
N^{a}=\frac{-D_{t}T^{a}}{\absolutevalue{D_tT}}

•

D_{t}
is the covariant derivative, which is defined as
D_{t}V^{a}=\dot{V}^{a}+\Gamma_{bc}^{a}V^{b}\dot{\phi}^{c}
, using the scalar field space metric
G_{ab}
to define the Christoffel symbols

and focus on the turning rate
\mathfrak{T}

Achúcarro and Palma 2019
:

\mathfrak{T}=\frac{V_{N}}{\dot{\phi}_{0}}.

(87)

The slow-roll parameters for multi-field inflation can be defined as

\displaystyle\epsilon_{V}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\frac{V^{a}V_{a}}{V^{2}}

(88)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\frac{V_{\phi}^{2}+V_{N}^{2}}{V^{2}}

(89)

\displaystyle=\epsilon_{H}\left(1+\frac{\mathfrak{T}^{2}}{9H^{2}}\right)

(90)

\displaystyle\epsilon_{H}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\dot{H}}{H^{2}}

(91)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\frac{V_{\phi}^{2}}{V^{2}}

(92)

where it becomes obvious that we can have situations with
\epsilon_{V}\sim\order{1}
while satisfying the current observational CMBconstraint
\epsilon_{H}<0.0044

Achúcarro and Palma 2019
:

Equation

12
boils down to
\mathfrak{s}_{1}<\sqrt{2\epsilon_{V}}

Garg and Krishnan 2019
, which is satisfied by a high enough turning rate.
If all the fields have masses above the Hubble scale, the slow-roll condition is satisfied if the turning rate is large, i.e.
\mathfrak{T}^{2}/H^{2}\gg 1

Chakraborty et al. 2020
;
Aragam et al. 2022
. This is in contradiction with the common belief that high masses spoil slow-rolling — a theme that runs under the name
\eta
-problem

Chakraborty et al. 2020
. However, settings with a high turning rate seem to be rare from a string theory perspective
Aragam et al. 2022
.
96
96

96

An example of a model with a high turning rate that satisfies the dSCis the realisation of DBIinflation studied by
Solomon and Trodden 2020
.

For
eq.

13
to hold, the turning rate does not have to be particularly large
Bjorkmo 2019
.
The takeaway is that slow-roll inflationary models are not fully ruled out, but require curved, non-geodesic trajectories
Achúcarro and Palma 2019
.

Another Ansatz to satisfy the dSC, the TCC, as well as observational constraints is presented by
Kogai and Tada 2020
: In their model, inflation happens in multiple subsequent phases, driven by multiple fields, including spectator fields. By coupling multiple hill-top potentials à la
V=\sum_{i}V_{i}(\phi_{i})+\sum_{i\neq j}\mathfrak{l}_{ij}V_{i}(\phi_{i})\phi_{j}^{2}/2M_{\textnormal{P}}
, with
\mathfrak{l}_{ij}\sim\order{1}
coupling constants, a dynamical repetition of inflation can be realised, such that
\epsilon_{H}\ll 1
and
n_{\textnormal{s}}-1\simeq-2\epsilon_{H}+2m_{\textnormal{seff}}^{2}/3H^{2}
, where a slightly tachyonic effective spectator mass
m_{\textnormal{seff}}^{2}\sim-0.05H^{2}
makes the model compatible with CMBobservations.

Furthermore, complex fields can be viewed as multi-field models. A complex field can be written as two real fields.
Sadeghi et al. 2022a
assess anisotropic constant roll inflation with a complex quintessence field. The plots they show indicate that the dSCis satisfied for large field ranges (even though a trans-Planckian field excursion violates the DC), yet, for field amplitudes between 0 and 1, the dSCis violated. They didn’t explore if there is an observationally viable phase space window that might be compatible with the swampland conjectures.

Small-field inflation

in the form of multiphase hilltop inflation with PBHproduction satisfies the second dSCcriterion (
eq.

13
) for each phase individually
Tada and Yokoyama 2019
. Furthermore, there are various models of small-field inflation in brane-world scenarios that are compatible with the dSC.

Osses et al. 2021
show that the
brane
can be slow-rolling as

\displaystyle\epsilon_{\textnormal{B}}

\displaystyle=\epsilon_{V}\frac{1+V/\mathcal{T}}{\left(1+V/2\mathcal{T}\right)^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{M_{4}^{2}}{16\pi}\left(\frac{V^{\prime}}{V}\right)^{2}

(93)

\displaystyle\eta_{\textnormal{B}}

\displaystyle=\eta_{V}\frac{1}{1+V/2\mathcal{T}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{M_{4}^{2}}{8\pi}\frac{V^{\prime\prime}}{V},

(94)

where
\mathcal{T}
is the brane tension, and
\epsilon_{V}
and
\eta_{V}
are the standard 4D slow-roll parameters, which can be large in this case (to be compatible with the dSC), whereas the slow-roll parameters for the brane itself are small and suppressed by a high potential. If a high potential is compatible with other swampland criteria or the inflation model at hand with regard to cosmological observations, needs to be investigated on a case-by-case basis.
97
97

97

For instance, if the potential is very high initially, and remains high, is it still in a high false vacuum today? Can this be probed? Does this have testable/observable effects? Or does the slow-roll approximation just break down towards the end of inflation?

Two examples studied by
Osses et al. 2021
are exponential SUSY inflation with

V(\phi)=\Lambda^{4}\left(1-e^{-\phi/\mu}\right),

(95)

where the potential is asymptotically flat for
\phi\rightarrow\infty
, the DCis satisfied, and the dSCis satisfied in a brane-world setting, with

\Lambda
a constant mass scale, and

\mu
the symmetry breaking scale, and
a hilltop potential of the form

V(\phi)=\Lambda^{4}\left[1-\left(\frac{\phi}{\mu}\right)^{n}\right]

(96)

with the vacuum expectation value
\mu
, about which they conclude that observations rule out
n=2
as well as
n=4
for
\mu\lesssim M_{\textnormal{P}}
, that the potential is compatible with the DC, and that in a brane-world scenario the dSCis satisfied because the brane can be slow-rolling while the inflaton potential itself can be steep. Furthermore, they find this model to satisfy the dSCif a suitable GBterm is present
Osses et al. 2021
.

Another small-field, single-field inflationary model is power-law plateau inflation with a potential of the form

V=V_{0}\left(\frac{\phi^{\mathfrak{c}_{1}}}{\phi^{\mathfrak{c}_{1}}+m^{\mathfrak{c}_{1}}}\right)^{\mathfrak{c}_{2}}

(97)

with
m,\phi
a mass scale, and
{\mathfrak{c}_{i}}
real parameters
Dimopoulos and Owen 2016
. The model is compatible with the DC, the dSC, as well as the TCCwhen put in a brane-world
Adhikari et al. 2020
. The presence of extra-dimensions in the brane-world scenario modifies the slow-roll parameters, such that all constraints can be satisfied
Adhikari et al. 2020
.

We make further comments about the feasibility of various other (small-field) inflationary models in brane-world scenarios in other parts of this section.

Warm inflation

converts vacuum energy to radiation through dissipative effects and can be made compatible with the dSC
Brandenberger et al. 2020
;
Berera 1995
;
Bastero-Gil and Berera 2009
;
Motaharfar et al. 2019
;
Das 2019b
;
Bastero-Gil et al. 2019a
;
Kamali et al. 2020
;
Berera and Calderón 2019
;
Kamali 2018
;
Kamali 2019
;
Kamali 2020
;
Das et al. 2020a
;
Yuennan et al. 2024
;
Das and Ramos 2020
;
Rasouli et al. 2019
;
Santos et al. 2022
;
Kamali et al. 2023
;
Berera et al. 2021
;
Kamali et al. 2021a
;
Arya et al. 2024
;
Das 2020
.
98
98

98

Contrary to cold inflation, where Hubble damping dominates, in warm inflation dissipative effects are intrinsic to interactions between the inflaton field and other degrees of freedom
Brandenberger et al. 2020
;
Berera et al. 2009
. This implies that
T>H
is required for inflation to be warm
Das et al. 2020a
.
In warm inflation, the origin of perturbations is thermal, contrary to other inflationary models where quantum fluctuations induce the perturbations
Motaharfar et al. 2019
.

It does not require a reheating phase at the end of inflation
Das 2019a
;
Kamali et al. 2023
.
99
99

99

Kamali 2020
found reheating due to an oscillatory phase around the minimum to be incompatible with
eq.

12
.

The inflaton field’s EoMis given by

\ddot{\phi}+3H\left(1+\Upsilon\right)\dot{\phi}+V^{\prime}=0,

(98)

with
\Upsilon\geq 0
related to the dissipation
Das 2019a
.
100
100

100

\ddot{\phi}
is omitted in the original work by
Berera 1995
; this is justified as slow-rolling is assured by friction due to the thermal bath
Das et al. 2020a
.

The radiation energy density evolves as
Yuennan et al. 2024
:

\dot{\rho_{\textnormal{r}}}+4H\rho_{\textnormal{r}}=3H\Upsilon\dot{\phi}^{2}.

(99)

Furthermore,

r_{\textnormal{ts}}=\frac{H}{T}\frac{16\epsilon_{V}}{\left(1+\Upsilon\right)^{5/2}},

(100)

with
T>H
the temperature of the thermal bath,
101
101

101

The condition that the inflaton background dynamics as well as the primordial powerspectrum are affected by dissipation —
T>H

Bastero-Gil and Berera 2009
— is not a given
Berera et al. 1998
;
Yokoyama and Linde 1999
;
Rasouli et al. 2019
, but is achieved by various models studied in this section.

shows that
r_{\textnormal{ts}}<16\epsilon
can be achieved in warm inflation
Das 2019a
;
Bartrum et al. 2013
.
102
102

102

A warm inflation model studied by
Yuennan et al. 2024
shows
r_{\textnormal{ts}}<${10}^{-2}$
, and for some parameter choices values as small as
r_{\textnormal{ts}}\sim${10}^{-23}$
.
Similarly, a model with strong dissipation by
Kamali et al. 2020
yields
r_{\textnormal{ts}}\sim${10}^{-27}$
for their choices of parameters.

To assess the viability of warm inflation, the weak and strong dissipation regimes are usually treated separately.
On the one hand, work by
Das 2019b
indicates that a weak dissipative regime is compatible with observations
and
the dSC
Das 2019a
.
On the other hand, strong dissipative effects lead to a scale-dependent scalar powerspectrum
Motaharfar et al. 2019
/ non-Gaussianities
Das and Ramos 2020
, and destabilise the inflaton potential
Das et al. 2020a
.
Yet, some models of warm inflation seem to circumvent these constraints and are compatible with observational data and swampland conjectures
Kamali et al. 2020
.

Bertolami and Sá 2022
find that strong dissipative effects are required for single- as well as multi-field warm inflation to be compatible with the dSC, and work by
Bastero-Gil et al. 2019a
highlights that the weak dissipation regime is in tension with the dSCand the DC.
Since
\epsilon_{V}>\mathfrak{s}_{1}^{2}/2
, achieving
\Upsilon=0
requires
\mathfrak{s}_{1}\sim${10}^{\order{\num{e-1}}}$

Das 2019b
, i.e. only a low value of
\mathfrak{s}_{1}
allows for weak dissipation.
Strong dissipation allows the inflaton to approach the slow-roll trajectory quicker, which enforces the attractor-like behaviour of the slow-roll solution
Kamali et al. 2020
.
Moreover, to solve the
\eta
-problem and to satisfy
eq.

13
,
V^{\prime\prime}\ll H^{2}
is required, i.e. Hubble friction has to dominate during inflation
Baumann and McAllister 2015
;
Kamali et al. 2023
. This problem is naturally avoided in the strong dissipation regime of warm inflation
Kamali et al. 2023
;
Berera 2004
;
Das 2019a
.

Mohammadi et al. 2020d
find that compatibility between a model and observational and theoretical constraints depends on the dissipation
function
— even in the strong dissipation regime: They study a model where a tachyon field decays into radiation.
103
103

103

See
footnote

153
for their model.

The dSCis not satisfied if the dissipation coefficient of their model is a power-law function of the tachyon field, neither is the DCin that case. However, if a temperature dependence is introduced in the dissipation coefficient, consistency between the model, observational data, and both conjectures is established.
When dissipation is
strong enough
does not have a clear-cut answer: While
warm inflation with a potential of the form
V(\phi)=V_{0}\left(1-\mathfrak{c}\mathfrak{p}\phi/M_{\textnormal{P}}\right)^{1/\mathfrak{p}}
studied by
Santos et al. 2022
is found to be generally compatible with the dSCfor
\Upsilon\geq 10
, as well as having a window of compatibility for
\Upsilon\gtrsim 1
depending on the value of
\mathfrak{p}
,

Rasouli et al. 2019
find that their model of warm DBIinflation can already be compatible with observations for
\Upsilon\gtrsim 0.1
.
104
104

104

Furthermore, they report that cold DBIinflation is incompatible with Planck data
Rasouli et al. 2019
.

Natural inflation

with a potential of the form

V=V_{0}\left(1-\cos{\frac{\phi}{f}}\right),

(101)

with
f
the axion decay constant, faces the constraints

\displaystyle f

\displaystyle<\frac{M_{\textnormal{P}}}{\mathfrak{s}_{1}}

(102)

\displaystyle f

\displaystyle<\frac{M_{\textnormal{P}}}{\sqrt{\mathfrak{s}_{2}}},

(103)

which are a little weaker than the WGCconstraints
Fukuda et al. 2019
.
Observations favour a trans-Planckian decay constant
Kim et al. 2005
;
Freese et al. 1990
, which violates the DC
Osses et al. 2021
.
105
105

105

Parameswaran et al. 2016
suggest that subleading, non-perturbative corrections can change this verdict.

Some authors deem this model incompatible with the dSCand the DC
Oikonomou et al. 2022
, but others present models with sub-Planckian axion decay constants
Albrecht et al. 2015
;
Holman and Richard 2019
or
non-canonical Lagrangians that are found to be compatible with the dSCand the DC

. Furthermore, a brane-world scenario can offer a viable framework with
\epsilon_{\textnormal{B}}\ll 1
for
V\gg\mathfrak{T}

Osses et al. 2021
.

Minimal gauge inflation

is embedded into a higher-dimensional theory with a non-Abelian SU(2) gauge symmetry with 5-dimensional gauge coupling constant
g_{5}
and compactification radius
r
, such that the inflaton has a potential

V(\phi)=\frac{9}{\left(2\pi\right)^{6}r^{2}}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{n^{5}}\left[1-\cos\left(\sqrt{2\pi r}g_{5}n\phi\right)\right],

(104)

which can be regarded as a higher-dimensional completion of natural inflation
Park 2019
;
Gong and Park 2018
.
To be compatible with the dSC,
\mathfrak{s}_{1}\lesssim 0.15
and
\mathfrak{s}_{2}\lesssim 0.01
are required
Park 2019
, which is in tension with the
\order{1}
prediction.

Chaotic inflation

has a potential of the form

V(\phi)=\frac{1}{2}m^{4-n}\phi^{n}

(105)

and faces the constraints

\displaystyle\frac{V^{\prime}}{V}

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{\phi_{\mu}}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\mathfrak{s}_{1}}{M_{\textnormal{P}}}

(106)

\displaystyle\frac{V^{\prime\prime}}{V}

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{\phi_{\mu}^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{-\mathfrak{s}_{2}}{M_{\textnormal{P}}^{2}}

(107)

with

\phi_{\mu}^{2}=\frac{3M_{\textnormal{P}}^{2}n^{2}-2n\left(n-1\right)M_{\textnormal{P}}^{2}}{1-n_{\textnormal{s}}^{\mu}};

(108)

\mu
a scale and
n_{\textnormal{s}}^{\mu}
the corresponding spectral index
Haque and Maity 2019
.

Haque and Maity 2019
find a tension between their model of chaotic inflation and the dSCfor parameter choices that are compatible with observational data and the DC, since they find
\mathfrak{s}_{1}^{\textnormal{max}}=0.1448<\order{1}
.
Smaller than
\order{1}

are also found in a study by
Matsui and Takahashi 2019

A model of chaotic inflation on a brane, where the constraints are expressed as

\displaystyle\frac{V^{\prime}}{V}

\displaystyle=\frac{n}{\phi}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\mathfrak{s}_{1}}{M_{\textnormal{P}}}

(109)

\displaystyle\frac{V^{\prime\prime}}{V}

\displaystyle=\frac{n\left(n-1\right)}{\phi^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{-\mathfrak{s}_{2}}{M_{\textnormal{P}}^{2}}

(110)

\displaystyle r_{\textnormal{ts}}

\displaystyle=\frac{24n}{N_{e}\left(n+2\right)}

\displaystyle\lesssim 0.1,

(111)

is found to be compatible with the refined dSC, if the conjecture is satisfied for the initial field value
\phi_{\textnormal{i}}
(since the field value is decreasing during inflation)

106

106

V(\phi)=\Lambda^{4}\left[1-\left(\phi/\mu\right)^{2}\right]^{2}

.

Quintessence inflation

with an inflaton potential of the form
V=V_{0}\exp\left(-\mathfrak{p}\phi\right)
is not slow-rolling.

Cicoli et al. 2022a
find that quintessence inflation in the form of axions with a hilltop potential requires very finely tuned initial conditions and a very low energy scale of inflation of
H_{\textnormal{I}}\lesssim$1\text{\,}\mathrm{MeV}$
in order to be a viable model of inflation without violated the dSC.

Eshaghi et al. 2022
;
Eshaghi et al. 2025
study a quintessence inflaton coupled to the Higgs field and find
\mathfrak{s}_{1}\lesssim$8\text{\times}{10}^{-3}$
as an upper bound to be consistent with observations, which violates the dSC.

Nitta and Uzawa 2025
derive a modified dSC, assuming the Strong Energy Condition (SEC)holds and that the scalar field velocity needs to be taken into account:

\displaystyle-\frac{\partial_{\phi}V}{V}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle 2\sqrt{\frac{D-2}{\left(D-d\right)\left(d-2\right)}}-\lambda\frac{\dot{\phi}}{V}

(112)

\displaystyle\lambda

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{M_{\textnormal{P};d}^{d-2}}{4}\sqrt{\frac{D-d}{\left(D-2\right)\left(d-2\right)}}

(113)

\displaystyle\times\left(-5d^{2}+d\left(5D+12\right)-11D-2\right).

Quintessence inflation violates this bound.

\alpha
-attractor models (Starobinsky)

come in two forms
Fukuda et al. 2019
:
T-models of the form

V=V_{0}\tanh^{2n}\left(\frac{\phi}{\sqrt{6\alpha}M_{\textnormal{P}}}\right),

(114)

which are in tension with the dSCunless a suitable GBterm is present
Gashti et al. 2022a
or they take place
in a
f(R)=\mathfrak{p}R
setting with
\mathfrak{p}\leq 0.008

Oikonomou et al. 2022
;
and
E-models of the form

V=V_{0}\left(1-e^{-\sqrt{\frac{2}{3\alpha}}\frac{\phi}{M_{\textnormal{P}}}}\right)^{2n}.

(115)

The Starobinsky model is the special case with
n=1
and
\alpha=1
, ergo
\mathfrak{s}_{1}\lesssim 1.6

Fukuda et al. 2019
.
The single-field Starobinsky model in an unmodified GRsetting is likely part of the swampland
Guleryuz 2023
.
But also in a
f(R)=R+\mathfrak{p}R^{2}
and a
f(R)=R+\mathfrak{c}_{1}R^{2}+\mathfrak{c}_{2}R^{2}\log R
setting, the Starobinsky model is deemed incompatible
Artymowski and Ben-Dayan 2019
.
Observational constraints and the DCrestrict
\mathfrak{s}_{1}\leq 0.025
, which renders the model incompatible with the dSC
Haque and Maity 2019
.
However, in a
f(R)
setting, a window of compatibility with observational constraints, the dSC, and the DCis found by
Oikonomou et al. 2022
;
Oikonomou 2021
, and
in non-critical string cosmology, where new dynamics are introduced by identifying cosmic time with a dynamic Liouville mode, the dSCcontains non-conformal contributions, which renders Starobinsky inflation compatible with this modified dSC
Ellis et al. 2020
.
Both types are found to be compatible with the combined dSCin a scalar–tensor theory setting (while violating the refined dSC, as the
\order{1}
thresholds cannot be met)
Yuennan and Channuie 2022
.

Ekpyrotic scenario
Khoury et al. 2002
;
Khoury et al. 2001

or
slow-contraction scenario
is an alternative to inflation
Cook et al. 2020
,
where a canonically normalised scalar field with a negative exponential potential dilutes anisotropies and spacial curvature, solves the flatness and horizon problems, provides a causal mechanism for structure formation, and is consistent with the TCC
Bernardo and Brandenberger 2021
.
While inflation takes place when
H>0
and
\epsilon>1
, to have accelerated expansion, the ekpyrotic scenario describes a contracting phase with
H<0
and
\epsilon>3
, such that the ekpyrotic field energy density (
\propto a^{-2\epsilon}
) grows faster than the small metric anisotropies (
\propto a^{-6}
)
Lehners 2018
.

Since the ekpyrotic scenario involves a negative potential, the dSCdoes not apply
Ben-Dayan 2019
;
Lehners 2018
, but replacing
V
with
\absolutevalue{V}
in
eqs.

12
and
13
leads to conjectures that show a compatibility with the ekpyrotic scenario
Bernardo and Brandenberger 2021
— something that should not tremendously surprise us, since AdSspace is very popular in string theory settings.
However,

Shiu et al. 2024
present some conjectures against fast-roll and an anti–trans-Planckian censorship conjecture that indicate that string theory might not provide potentials steep enough to realise the ekpyrotic scenario.
107
107

107

Furthermore, constraints based on a generalised distance measure for the appplication of the DCto the ekpyrotic scenario are discussed by
Debusschere et al. 2025
.

Tachyacoustic models

propose a superluminal sound speed in the early universe to solve the horizon problem and maintain an approximately scale-invariant power spectrum
Lin and Kinney 2019
;
Magueijo 2008
;
Bessada et al. 2009
;
Babichev et al. 2007
;
Lin and Kinney 2021
.
Lin and Kinney 2019
show that tachyacoustic Lagrangians can be compatible with the dSC. However, no known string theory offers
c_{\textnormal{s}}>1

Lin and Kinney 2019
;
Kinney and Tzirakis 2008
. Moreover, such models violate the TCC
Lin and Kinney 2021
.

Particle Physics

The SMquark masses are compatible with the dSC, but a large number of additional light quarks is in increasing tension with the conjecture, as metastable states appear
March-Russell and Petrossian-Byrne 2020
. A coupling to quintessence has been investigated and found not to weaken the constraining power of the dSC
March-Russell and Petrossian-Byrne 2020
.

II.4.2
General Remarks

Which observables would indicate an unstable dSspace?

Exactly stable dSspace does not allow for precisely measurable observables
Rudelius 2019
;
Witten 2001
;
Rudelius 2021b
. The (
1\sigma
) signature of a cascade of dSdecays is claimed to be found in the North American Nanohertz Observatory for Gravitational Waves (NANOGrav)
Brazier et al. 2019
data set
Arzoumanian et al. 2020
for the following model
Li et al. 2021
:
The lifetime of inflation is limited by the TCCto
\int\!H\,\mathrm{d}t<\log\left(M_{\textnormal{P}}/H\right)

Bedroya et al. 2020a
.
The lifetime of dSspace during inflation is limited by the TCCto
\Delta t<H^{-1}\log\left(M_{\textnormal{P}}/H\right)

Bedroya and Vafa 2020
;
Bedroya et al. 2020b
.
108
108

108

It was observed that the logarithmic factor enhances
\Delta\phi
, which potentially relaxes the bound on the second derivative (
eq.

13
) around the top of the potential
Seo 2020a
;
Cai and Wang 2021
.

A cascade of first-order phase transitions produces GWsat different frequencies, which are subsequently redshifted by the dSexpansions of the following short-lived phases of inflation of the cascade.
This leads to a stochastic GWbackground with a red-tilt, which is compatible with the NANOGravresults at the
1\sigma
level.

How is the dSCmotivated?

Constructing stable dSsolutions in string theory
109
109

109

The dSCis deeply rooted in string theory and the observation that it is very challenging to construct dSspaces in string theory. Other theories of QGare easily compatible with dSspaces or predict dSspaces right away, e.g. causal set theory
Barrau et al. 2021
;
Bombelli et al. 1987
;
Sorkin 2009
;
Dowker and Zalel 2017
;
Yazdi 2024
.
Asymptotic safety even prefers a cosmological constant with an EoSparameter
w=-1
, or at least leads to flattening potentials when travelling through field space
Pawlowski et al. 2019
;
de Brito et al. 2019
;
Eichhorn et al. 2018a
;
Percacci and Vacca 2015
;
Labus et al. 2016
, which is incompatible with the dSC
Eichhorn and Pauly 2021
. According to the string lamppost principle, everything that happens in QGcan be accommodated in string theory. If this is true, then either asymptotic safety and causal set theory are not valid theories of QG, or it is possible to construct dSvacua in string theory, or the string lamppost principle itself is wrong.

is a hard problem — so hard that scholars started to conjecture that it might be impossible.
110
110

110

Reviewing roughly the first two decades of the 21
st
century,
Danielsson and Riet 2018
discuss various attempts at constructing dSvacua in string theory and their failure, as well as some rough ideas about the fate of DE.

A study on the distribution of stationary points, in particular local minima, of random scalar potentials with a large number of scalar fields by
Low et al. 2021
provides some insight: They find that the number of critical points increases with the number of scalar fields, yet the relative number of local minima decreases exponentially with the number of scalar fields, i.e. it becomes exceedingly rare to find a true minimum with a high number of scalar fields. This hints at why it is so hard to find a stable dSvacuum.
Yet, it doesn’t proof that it is impossible to construct stable dSpoints in string theory.
In fact, there are non-perturbative constructions of dSspace
Dvali et al. 2017
;
Dvali and Gomez 2019
;
Dvali et al. 2019
;
Brahma et al. 2021a
;
Brahma et al. 2021b
;
Bernardo et al. 2021b
;
Hohm and Zwiebach 2019
;
Krishnan 2019
;
Bernardo et al. 2020
;
Bernardo and Franzmann 2020
;
Núñez and Rost 2021
;
Leontaris and Shukla 2023
;
Leedom et al. 2023
;
Bardzell et al. 2022
,
arguments in favour of dSspace in EFTs
Dodelson et al. 2014
;
Moritz et al. 2018
;
Crinò et al. 2021
;
Marolf and Morrison 2010
;
Brahma et al. 2021a
;
Danielsson et al. 2011
;
Caviezel et al. 2009b
;
Silverstein 2008
;
Brahma et al. 2023
;
Bernardo and Brandenberger 2021
;
Landgren 2022
,
and some authors conclude that dSvacua are in the landscape of string theory
Kallosh et al. 2019a
.
Furthermore, a paper proposing that dSvacua are in the swampland
Moritz et al. 2018
is shown to be erroneous
Gautason et al. 2019
and various assumptions are shown not to hold
Kallosh 2019
;
Cicoli et al. 2019
;
Kallosh et al. 2019b
;
Kallosh et al. 2019a
;
Kallosh et al. 2019c
.
However, various attempts to construct stable dSsolutions are flawed:
Several known dSconstructions involving non-geometric fluxes are shown to be unstable, as positive tadpole charges are involved that lead to runaway behaviour
Plauschinn 2021
(cf.
section

II.15
).

Dasgupta et al. 2021a
present a dSsolution in the context of type IIB string theory with
\mathfrak{s}_{1}\gg 1
that is only in the landscape if fluxes are time-independent.

Cordova et al. 2019a
;
Cordova et al. 2019b
present an Ansatz to construct stable dSsolutions using O8-planes, but their solutions were later shown to be unstable
Van Riet and Zoccarato 2023
;
Bena et al. 2021a
;
Cribiori and Junghans 2019
.

Landgren 2022
presents a dSsolution in
\left(2+1\right)
dimensions, yet the low dimensionality is troublesome from a gravity perspective.
111
111

111

See
footnote

7
.

Charting type IIB using machine learning,
Damian and Loaiza-Brito 2022
present 170 dSvacua that are obtained by uplifting metastable AdSvacua, though there are several loopholes mentioned in the paper how the dSvacua could be destabilised.
112
112

112

Alwis 2021
argues that the dSCmight lose predictability in the bulk entirely when radiative corrections uplift an AdSsolution into the dSregime in the IR.
One potential issue with uplifting AdSto dSis addressed by the TPC: The tadpole required to uplift AdSwith a small supersymmetry breaking scale using an antibrane in a long warped throat to dSmight be too large
Bena et al. 2021b
.

In the context of holography,
Grieninger 2020
presents dSsolutions, yet, introducing matter fields makes the family of these solutions disappear.

Moreover, there is evidence that it is not possible to construct stable dSsolutions using a perturbative approach
Danielsson and Riet 2018
;
Emelin 2020
;
Dasgupta et al. 2021b
;
Dasgupta et al. 2019
;
Sethi 2018
;
Kutasov et al. 2015
;
Hertzberg et al. 2008
;
Andriot et al. 2020b
;
Andriot et al. 2022d
;
Maldacena and Nunez 2001
;
Hertzberg et al. 2007
;
Witten 2001
;
Caviezel et al. 2010
;
Giddings and Maharana 2006
;
Alwis 2003
;
Parameswaran and Serra 2024
;
Green et al. 2012
,
as well as indications of a perturbative IRinstability in dSspace
Mottola 1985
;
Tsamis and Woodard 1996
;
Mukhanov et al. 1996
;
Brandenberger 2002
;
Brandenberger et al. 2018
;
Kitamoto et al. 2020
;
Polyakov 2012
;
Polyakov 2008
;
Andriot et al. 2022a
;
Andriot et al. 2022b
;
Bernardo and Brandenberger 2021

and entropic arguments against stable dSvacua
Geng et al. 2019
.
Moreover, the refined dSC(
eq.

13
) is backed by the DCand the CEB, a semi-classical notion of entropy of dSspace
Brahma and Shandera 2019
.

In a 10d setting with 4d dSsubspaces,
Andriot 2019
makes the following observations:
First, no classical dSsolutions with parallel sources were found.
Second, no stable classical dSsolutions with intersecting sources were found.
Third, no classical dSsolutions were found that exhibit the following simultaneously: a large internal volume, a small string coupling, quantized fluxes, and a finite number of orientifolds.
Furthermore, 10-dimensional Minkowski space is supersymmetric with vanishing energy, but dSspace has positive vacuum energy, which indicates the instability of dSspace
Baumann and McAllister 2015
.
The instability of dSvacua with broken supersymmetry, under the conditions that first, the WGCholds; second, the theory has objects of codimension 1 (branes/membranes); third, a three-form chiral multiplet is coupled to supergravity; and fourth, the three-form multiplet is nilpotent and breaks supersymmetry, is shown by
Farakos et al. 2021
.
Charged membranes are also found to be the culprit by
Liu et al. 2024
;
Kaloper and Westphal 2023
:
Charged membranes, if present, change the cosmological constant via their fluxes. Since the membranes have a tension, the WGCapplies, which limits the tension. However, to stop discharge of the membranes, i.e. to form a stable situation, the tension has to go to infinity, which the WGCforbids. Therefore, stable dSvacua are in the swampland.

Are there any counterexamples to the dSC?

There are various counterexamples to
eq.

12

Denef et al. 2018a
;
Conlon 2018
;
Murayama et al. 2018
;
Choi et al. 2018
;
Hamaguchi et al. 2018
;
Roupec and Wrase 2019
;
Garg et al. 2019
;
Andriot 2018
, which
eq.

13
evades
Ooguri et al. 2019
.
113
113

113

A potential counterexample to the refined dSCis presented by
Blåbäck et al. 2018
.

Other counterexamples presented by
Caviezel et al. 2009b
;
Flauger et al. 2009
are dismissed by
Danielsson et al. 2011
.

How does the dSCdepend on the dimensionality?

An insightful link between cosmic expansion and dSspace is presented by
Hebecker and Wrase 2019
:
They conjecture that accelerated cosmic expansion in the asymptotic region of the field space of a
d
-dimensional EFTis only possible if there is a metastable dSvacuum in a higher dimension. They coin this the
no asymptotic acceleration conjecture
. More concretely: If a
d
-dimensional EFTrealises accelerated expansion through a rolling scalar field at an asymptotically large field traversal, then this EFTstems from a compactification of a higher-dimensional theory with positive vacuum energy.
This conjecture raises several open questions. On the one hand, we currently observe accelerated expansion and we assume that our Universe already underwent a period of accelerated expansion in the past (inflation). This would then indicate that these phases of inflation are not taking place in asymptotic field limits respectively are not caused by a rolling scalar field. The former explanation would also be supported by the DC. Furthermore, it indicates that neither inflation nor DEcause eternal accelerated expansion. This is in line with the TCC.
On the other hand, the conjecture could be satisfied if there were a higher-dimensional dSvacuum, i.e. the dSCwould have to be refined to only apply to lower-dimensional EFTsand a valid dSconstruction in a higher-dimensional setting should be found. While we don’t see à priori a good reason to exclude the possibility of inflation being a decompactification, it seems challenging to explain the current phase of accelerated expansion with a decompactification.

Antoniadis et al. 2020
argue that the instability of dSvacua does actually not even appear in 4 spacetime dimensions, only in higher-dimensional theories. In 4d, perturbative effects from localised sources of graviton kinetic terms allow for locally stable dSvacua — this is only the case in 4d, which would explain why our Universe is 4d.
Moreover, to experience a 4d dScosmology, it is not even compulsory to have a (meta-)stable dSvacuum: an alternative can be an unstable AdSvacuum where a brane nucleates such that on the brane a local dSpatch exists
Banerjee et al. 2018
;
Banerjee et al. 2021b
;
Danielsson et al. 2021
;
Banerjee et al. 2021c
;
Banerjee et al. 2020
.

Are dSvacua fully excluded?

The dSCallows for meta-stability.
The conjecture does not exclude dSvacua with a lifetime shorter than the quantum breaking time
Dias et al. 2019a
;
Dvali and Gomez 2014a
;
Vafa 2024a
;
Dvali and Gomez 2019
;
Dvali et al. 2019
;
Dvali et al. 2017
;
Dvali and Gomez 2013a
;
Dvali and Gomez 2014b
.
The quantum breaking time is the timescale

t_{\textnormal{q}}\sim M_{\textnormal{P}}^{2}/H^{3}

(116)

after which a system can no longer be treated classically, regardless of the order of classical non-linearities that are taken into account
Ahmed and Raza 2024
;
Dvali et al. 2019
;
Dvali and Gomez 2019
;
Damian and Loaiza-Brito 2024
;
Dvali et al. 2017
.
114
114

114

Due to non-linearities, a theory will depart from its classical dynamics after a time
t_{\textnormal{cl}}\sim 1/H

Damian and Loaiza-Brito 2024
.
The quantum breaking time is inversely proportional to the number of species in the theory, which links the dSCto the SSC
Dvali et al. 2017
:
t_{\textnormal{q}}\sim M_{\textnormal{P}}^{2}/N_{\textnormal{S}}\Lambda_{\textnormal{cc}}^{3/2}
.
The upper phenomenological bound of
{10}^{32}
species sets an upper bound for the lifetime of our Universe of
{10}^{100}
years.
If there are more than
N_{\textnormal{S}}=M_{\textnormal{P}}^{2}/\Lambda_{\textnormal{cc}}
species, the quantum breaking time becomes shorter than the Hubble time.

dSvacua could be very short-lived due to spontaneous decompactifications
Giddings 2003
;
Giddings and Myers 2004
and compactifications
Dine et al. 2004
;
Draper et al. 2021a
;
Draper et al. 2021b

Draper et al. 2022
.
Furthermore, dSvacua can decay into bubbles of nothing
Draper et al. 2021a
;
Draper et al. 2021b
;
Dine et al. 2004
, or experience a bounce, where the direction of time reverses without encountering a singularity
Kiritsis and Tsouros 2023
;
Emparan and Garriga 2003
;
Bramberger and Lehners 2019
.
For some proposed dSsolutions, the predicted lifetime could be at odds with the quantum breaking time
Arkani-Hamed et al. 2007a
;
Dvali et al. 2019
;
Westphal 2008
;
Dvali et al. 2017
respectively the TCC
Brahma and Hossain 2021
.
For instance, the life-time of a KKLTdSsaddle point is

\tau\sim\frac{1}{H\log\left(M_{\textnormal{P}}/H\right)}<\frac{1}{H}<\frac{\log\left(M_{\textnormal{P}}/H\right)}{H}

(117)

i.e. the log-corrections are in the opposite direction as in the TCCbound and shorten the lifetime
Blumenhagen et al. 2020a
.

A finite dSlifetime is obtained if dSspace is realised as a coherent quantum state of gravitons that decohere after the quantum breaking time
Dvali et al. 2017
:
Relatively recently,
Brahma et al. 2023
;
Brahma et al. 2021a
;
Bernardo et al. 2021c
;
Brahma et al. 2021b
;
Alexander et al. 2024
report to have successfully constructed non-perturbatively stable 4d dSsolutions within a controlled temporal domain by viewing dSspace as a Glauber–Sudarshan state with a shifted interacting vacuum over a supersymmetric Minkowski background. Instead of a fundamental vacuum, dSspacetime would be an emerging, meta-stable coherent state. Therefore, this is not a counterexample to the dSCper se.

Does the dSCalso apply to theories of modified gravity?

We pointed out in
section

II.4.1
that some forms of inflation are incompatible with the dSCin a GRcontext, but compatible with the dSCin a theory of modified gravity. A valid question is if the dSCis even valid in theories of modified gravity, and what implications the dSCmight have for such theories.
It has been found that the dSCis applicable to theories of modified gravity and can constrain them:

•

f(R,T)
gravity provides a viable setting:
a D-brane model with
V=V_{0}\left(1-\left(m/\phi\right)^{4}\right)
,
an E-model (see
eq.

115
),
a T-model (
eq.

114
), and
a model of modular inflation with
V=V_{0}\left(1-\mathfrak{p}\exp(-\mathfrak{c}\phi)\right)

are deemed compatible with the dSC
Oikonomou et al. 2023
.
Furthermore, holographic DEas inflaton with the parametrisation
f(R,T)=R+8\pi G\mathfrak{c}T
has a window of compatibility for
\mathfrak{c}\gtrsim${10}^{\order{\num{e2}}}$

Taghavi et al. 2023
.
115
115

115

The DCrequires
\mathfrak{c}\gtrsim 300

Taghavi et al. 2023
.

•

f(R)
theories are restricted but allowed by the dSC
Mata-Pacheco 2024
;
García-Compeán et al. 2023
. This is not too surprising, since a
f(R)
model in the Jordan frame correpsonds to GRplus scalar fields in the Einstein frame
Benetti et al. 2019
.

–

\partial_{R}f>0
is required to have a well-defined scalar field instead of ghosts
Mata-Pacheco 2024
;
De Felice and Tsujikawa 2010
.

–

\partial_{R}^{2}f>0
is required to have a positive mass term for curvature fluctuations
Mata-Pacheco 2024
;
De Felice and Tsujikawa 2010
.

–

Equation

12
translates to

\displaystyle\absolutevalue{2f(R)-R\partial_Rf(R)}>\sqrt{3/2}\mathfrak{s}_{1}\left(R\partial_{R}f(R)-f(R)\right)

for
f(R)
theories
Artymowski and Ben-Dayan 2019
. While in GRa potential
V\propto\exp(-\lambda\phi)
can always satisfy the dSCat the cost of the DC, in an
f(R)
theory with
R\partial_{R}f>2f
, i.e.
\partial_{\phi}V<0
, the model is constraint already by the dSCalone
Artymowski and Ben-Dayan 2019
.
116
116

116

Bajardi et al. 2022
present the same bound yet with a different numerical pre-factor of
\order{1}
(in a somewhat convoluted form with higher-order derivatives that cancel each other if
\partial_{R}f
is invertible
Benetti et al. 2019
).

–

Various models do not meet observational expectations:

*

While
f(R)
theories per se are not ruled out by the dSC, the constraints that are put on such theories do not automatically solve the Hubble tension either
Elizalde and Khurshudyan 2022
.

*

Artymowski and Ben-Dayan 2019
found no patches of the parameter space that are compatible with the dSCthat allow for suitable models of inflation or DE. If the dSCcould be relaxed to
\mathfrak{s}_{1}\sim\order{0.1}
, suitable models of inflation existed
Sadeghi et al. 2019
;
Sadeghi et al. 2022b
.

*

An Ansatz
f(R)\sim R^{\mathfrak{p}}
yields the dSCconstraint
1<\mathfrak{p}\lesssim 1.45

Mata-Pacheco 2024
;
García-Compeán et al. 2023
. GRitself is ruled out in the UVby the dSCsince it corresponds to
\mathfrak{p}=1

Mata-Pacheco 2024
;
García-Compeán et al. 2023
.
Benetti et al. 2019
report that
\mathfrak{p}<2/3
and
\mathfrak{p}>2
satisfy the dSC.

·

Channuie 2022
found the constraints from the refined dSCincompatible with observations, while the combined dSCallows for observationally viable models.

·

For
\mathfrak{p}\lesssim${10}^{\order{\num{e-1}}}$
, power-law potentials
\propto\phi^{2}
, the E-model (
eq.

115
), the T-model (
eq.

114
), and a D-brane model were found to be compatible with observations, the dSC, and the DC
Oikonomou et al. 2022
.

·

Natural inflation and quadratic hilltop inflation were deemed incompatible if
\mathfrak{p}\lesssim${10}^{\order{\num{e-1}}}$

Oikonomou et al. 2022
.

*

The Starobinsky model with
f(R)\simeq R+\alpha R^{2}
is found to violate the dSCin the late Universe
Benetti et al. 2019
.

–

The presence of matter fields makes it harder to fulfil
eq.

13
, as matter contributes positively to the second derivative of the potential:
V^{\prime\prime}_{\textnormal{eff}}=V^{\prime\prime}+\left(1-3w\right)^{2}\rho_{m}/6

Artymowski and Ben-Dayan 2019
.

Mata-Pacheco 2024
claims that
eq.

13
is always violated, but others find it to hold if GBor Chern–Simons terms are present
Gitsis et al. 2023
;
Odintsov et al. 2023
.

•

A coupling to a parity-violating Chern–Simons term affects the tensor-to-scalar ratio as tensor modes behave differently based on their chirality, but since only tensor modes are affected, the dSCremains applicable
Fronimos and Venikoudis 2023
.

•

Topological massive gravity, more precisely a spin-2 truncation of a higher-spin theory with Chern–Simons terms, fermions, and scalar fields, is found to satisfy the refined dSC: with a dimensionless coupling constant
g_{6}
for the conformal, sixth-order self-coupling of the scalar field,
\mathfrak{s}_{2}<3/2
is found for
V=3H^{2}\phi^{2}/8-\absolutevalue{g_6}\phi^{6}/6

Álvarez García et al. 2022a
.

•

Lorentz invariance seems not to be a requirement for the applicability of the dSC
Mata-Pacheco 2024
;
Trivedi 2022c
;
García-Compeán et al. 2023
.

–

F(\bar{R})
Hořava–Lifshitz theories are constraint but compatible with
eq.

12
, which shows on the one hand that Hořava–Lifshitz theories are promising UVextensions of theories of gravity, but on the other hand it also indicates that the dSCmight be applicable to theories that break Lorentz invariance
Mata-Pacheco 2024
;
García-Compeán et al. 2023
. However,
F(\bar{R})
theories violate
eq.

13

Mata-Pacheco 2024
.

–

A constant Hubble parameter is incompatible with GRand
f(R)
theories, but not with more general
F(\bar{R})
Hořava–Lifshitz theories that break Lorentz invariance in the UV
Weinfurtner et al. 2010
;
Sotiriou 2011
;
Wang 2017
;
Mukohyama 2010
;
Calcagni 2009
;
Chaichian et al. 2010
;
Elizalde et al. 2010
;
Hořava 2009

Mata-Pacheco 2024
;
García-Compeán et al. 2023
.

•

Applying the dSCto exponential, power-law and logarithmic inflaton potentials in scalar–tensor theory leaves a window of compatibility that partially overlaps with observational constraints
Gashti and Sadeghi 2022
.

•

Horndeski theory, a further generalisation of scalar–tensor theories with derivative self-interactions and non-minimal couplings, is constrained by but compatible with the dSC
Heisenberg et al. 2019
;
Brahma and Hossain 2019a
.

What are the exact values of
\mathfrak{s}_{1}
and
\mathfrak{s}_{2}
?

The literature on speculations about the value of
\mathfrak{s}_{1}
is rich, even though any value of
\mathfrak{s}_{1}
would do to exclude (meta-)stable dSvacua from the landscape
Akrami et al. 2019
.
To just exclude dSvacua,
\absolutevalue{\nabla V}>\mathfrak{c}
, with
\mathfrak{c}
some constant, would be sufficient
Liu 2021b
. However, there are supersymmetric vacua with flat directions that pose as counterexamples to this constraint
Liu 2021b
. To include those in the landscape,
\mathfrak{c}
has to be promoted to
\mathfrak{c}(\phi)\leq 0
, of which
\mathfrak{c}(\phi)=\mathfrak{s}_{1}V(\phi)
is one possibility
Liu 2021b
. The value of
\mathfrak{s}_{1}
is the subject of ongoing debate. We give an overview in the following, but first make some remarks about
\mathfrak{s}_{2}
, which is less often discussed:

\mathfrak{s}_{2}^{2}=-V^{\prime\prime}/V\gtrsim\mathcal{O}(1)
is required to have a concave potential with a lower bound on the curvature
Raveri et al. 2019
;
Storm and Scherrer 2020
,
the
\order{1}
bound pops up in numerical analysis
Garg et al. 2019
,
and
\mathfrak{s}_{2}=\frac{2\left(d-1\right)}{\left(d-2\right)}
is necessary for potentials that are linear or quadratic in
\phi
to allow for eternal inflation
Rudelius 2021a
.
That the parameter
\mathfrak{s}_{2}
should be of
\order{1}
can be highlighted by the following approximation using the DC
Liu 2021b
:

M_{\textnormal{P}}^{2}\frac{\nabla_{j}\nabla_{j}V}{V}\sim-\frac{M_{\textnormal{P}}^{2}}{\left(\Delta\phi\right)^{2}}\leq-\order{1}.

(118)

The TCCyields

\frac{\absolutevalue{\Delta V}}{V}>\frac{16}{\left(d-1\right)\left(d-2\right)\log^{2}\!\left(V\right)}

(119)

as a bound
Bedroya 2020
.

Raveri et al. 2019
rule out
\mathfrak{s}_{2}>1.4
at the 95% confidence level based on CMBand SNobservations for DEpotentials of the form
V=k\cos(\mathfrak{s}_2\phi)
, with
k
a numerical factor.
117
117

117

Note that such potentials always fail
eq.

12
.

Regarding the value of
\mathfrak{s}_{1}
, we start by presenting a concrete yet rather general setting where
\mathfrak{s}_{1}=0
is ruled out:

Shiu et al. 2023b
study multi-field multi-exponential potentials for scaling solutions of late-time attractors and find a lower relative bound on the potentials that support the dSC, without relying on a specific model.
118
118

118

It is important to note that they do not assume slow-roll.

The potentials with
n
exponential terms and
k
scalar fields
\phi^{a}
(such that
a=1,\dots,k
) are of the general form

V=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Lambda_{i}e^{-\mathfrak{c}_{ia}\phi^{a}},

(120)

with
\Lambda_{i}>0
and
\mathfrak{c}_{ia}
constants that depend on the microscopic origin of the scalar potential.
119
119

119

A universal upper bound for multi-field multi-exponential potentials that are positive-definite is presented by
Shiu et al. 2023c
:
\epsilon_{H}\leq d-1
.

The couplings
\mathfrak{c}_{ia}
span a vector
v_{i}
with
\left(v_{i}\right)_{a}=\mathfrak{c}_{ia}
. These vectors then span a hypersurface, the convex hull of the exponential couplings
v_{\textnormal{CH}}
.
As
Shiu et al. 2023b
analytically show,

\epsilon_{H}\geq\frac{d-2}{4}v_{\textnormal{CH}}^{2}

(121)

holds for any late-time solution with cosmic acceleration.
Now, with
\frac{d-2}{4}v_{\textnormal{CH}}^{2}\leq\epsilon_{H}\leq d-1
it can be shown that
\mathfrak{s}_{1}>0
, as predicted by the dSC, even though without quantifying the exact value of
\mathfrak{s}_{1}
:

\absolutevalue{\nabla V}/V=0
is only possible for
\left(d-1\right)-\epsilon_{H}=\eta_{H}/2
.
120
120

120

See the work by
Shiu et al. 2023b
for the derivation of this result.

This equation can be restated in terms of the Hubble parameter as
d-1+\ddot{H}/2\dot{H}H=0
, and then
be recast as

\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\left[\left(d-1\right)H^{2}+\dot{H}\right],

(122)

which is solved by
\left(d-1\right)H_{0}^{2}+\dot{H}_{0}=\mathfrak{c}_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
.
From the bounds on
\epsilon_{H}
and the expectation that
\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}H(t)=0
, it follows that
\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}\dot{H}(t)=0
, which yields
\mathfrak{c}_{0}=0
.
This finding allows us to integrate the solution, to find
H_{0}=1/\left[\left(d-1\right)t+\mathfrak{c}_{1}\right]
. This solution corresponds to pure kination and can only be achieved for
\epsilon_{H}=d-1
, in which case we find
\eta_{H}=0
.
The conclusion here is that for
\epsilon_{H}>0,\neq d-1
, the gradient condition
\mathfrak{s}_{1}>0
is a strict inequality. This holds for (multi-) field (multi-) exponential potentials of accelerating late-time attractor solutions.

That
\mathfrak{s}_{1}
should be of
\order{1}
comes from several assumptions
Calderón-Infante et al. 2023b
:
First, it is assumed that even in a multi-field case
\epsilon_{\textnormal{H}}=\epsilon_{V}
.
Second, the value is obtained in the asymptotic limit of perturbative string theory, but assumed to hold in any asymptotic limit of moduli space, outside the lamppost of known string theory.
Third, if applied to cosmology, this asymptotic limit is applied in the bulk, i.e. in the middle of moduli space.
And fourth, it is assumed the condition holds under dimensional reduction.
We elaborate on all the steps in the following, following closely the work by
Calderón-Infante et al. 2023b
.

0
th

We start with an effective action of the following form:

\displaystyle S=\int\!\sqrt{-g}\left(\frac{R}{2}+\frac{1}{2}g^{\mu\nu}G_{ab}\partial_{\mu}\phi^{a}\partial_{\nu}\phi^{b}-V(\phi)\right)\,\mathrm{d}^{d}x,

(123)

with
R
the Ricci scalar,
g_{\mu\nu}
the
d
-dimensional spacetime metric, and
G_{ab}
the moduli space metric.
The Friedmann equation and EoMare:

\displaystyle\frac{\left(d-1\right)\left(d-2\right)}{2}H^{2}-\frac{1}{2}G_{ab}\dot{\phi}^{a}\dot{\phi}^{b}-V(\phi)

\displaystyle=0

(124)

\displaystyle\ddot{\phi}^{a}+\tilde{\Gamma}^{a}_{bc}\dot{\phi}^{b}\dot{\phi}^{c}+\left(d-1\right)H\dot{\phi}^{a}+\partial^{a}V(\phi)

\displaystyle=0,

(125)

where the Christoffel symbols
\tilde{\Gamma}^{a}_{bc}
are with respect to the moduli space metric
G_{ab}
,
\partial_{a}
are derivatives with respect to the fields
\phi^{a}
, and indices are raised and lowered with the moduli space metric.
The slow-roll conditions are that the potential dominates over the kinetic term and that the friction term dominates over the second-order terms:

{AmSalign}

Slow-rolling holds for
\mathfrak{s}_{1}<2\sqrt{\left(d-1\right)/\left(d-2\right)}
.

1
st

The slow-roll parameter for the field reads

\displaystyle\epsilon_{V}

\displaystyle=\frac{d-2}{4}\frac{\absolutevalue{\nabla V}^{2}}{V^{2}}

(126)

\displaystyle=\epsilon_{\textnormal{H}}\left(1+\frac{\Omega^{2}}{\left(d-1\right)^{2}H^{2}}\right)

(127)

\displaystyle\Rightarrow\mathfrak{s}_{1}

\displaystyle<\frac{2}{\sqrt{d-2}}\left(1+\frac{\Omega^{2}}{\left(d-1\right)^{2}H^{2}}\right)^{1/2}.

(128)

For a single field, the slow-roll conditions
121
121

121

See also our discussion around
eq.

90
.

becomes
\epsilon_{\textnormal{H}}=\epsilon_{V}
and therefore

\mathfrak{s}_{1}<\frac{2}{\sqrt{d-2}}.

(129)

2
nd

The value of
\absolutevalue{\nabla V}/V
varies with
\phi
, but
\mathfrak{s}_{1}
is generally assumed to correspond to the value in the asymptotic limit where
\int_{t_{0}}^{t}\!\sqrt{G_{ab}\dot{\phi}^{a}\dot{\phi}^{b}}\,\mathrm{d}t\rightarrow\infty
holds.
Furthermore, it is assumed that this bounds holds in any asymptotic regime, not only in the string theory lamppost of weak string coupling. This is a strong assumption, mainly motivated by the absence of counterexamples.
122
122

122

See e.g. the work by
Cicoli et al. 2022b
;
Bento et al. 2020
;
Garg et al. 2019
for examples.

3
rd

In the bulk, where cosmology happens, this does not apply. There can even be dSvacua and the value of
\mathfrak{s}_{1}
is less constrained / unconstrained by theory (so far).

4
th

The strong form of the dSCassumes that
\mathfrak{s}_{1}=2/\sqrt{d-2}
holds as a strict bound. This is motivated by the finding that this bounds remains consistent under dimensional reduction.

Other constraints on the value of
\mathfrak{s}_{1}
are the following:

•

CMBdata is compatible with high values of
\mathfrak{s}_{1}
(if DEis modelled with a single scalar field), but when combined with distance–redshift data, only small values remain compatible with observations (as large
\mathfrak{s}_{1}
lead to a substantial deviation from
\omega_{\text{DE}}=-1
)
Raveri et al. 2019
.

•

DESIdata indicates
\mathfrak{s}_{1}\gtrsim\order{1}
at present time and even higher values in the past
Arjona and Nesseris 2024
.

•

Using Gaussian process reconstruction and observational data on
H(z)
,
Elizalde and Khurshudyan 2019
derive upper bounds on
\mathfrak{s}_{1}
without assuming a form for the potential
V(\phi)
. They use two different kernels, a squared exponential kernel, and a Matern kernel with
\nu=9/2
that allows for higher derivatives and therefore more smoothness, to reconstruct
\absolutevalue{\nabla V}/V
from
H(z)
. We summarise their obtained bounds in
table

1
.

•

Andriot et al. 2024
found
\mathfrak{s}_{1}<\sqrt{3}
as a requirement to explain the current accelerated expansion with quintessence.

•

\mathfrak{s}_{1}^{\textnormal{sugra}}=6/\sqrt{\left(d-2\right)\left(11-d\right)}
from 11d supergravity
Obied et al. 2018

•

\mathfrak{s}_{1}^{\textnormal{SEC}}=2\sqrt{\left(D-2\right)/\left[\left(D-d\right)\left(d-2\right)\right]}
in 11d supergravity regarding the SEC
123
123

123

The SECcan be violated in string theory.
Obied et al. 2018
deemed this bound too strong, as a
d
-dimensional universe needs
\absolutevalue{\nabla V}/V<\sqrt{4/\left(d-2\right)}
to undergo accelerated expansion.
The violation of the SECin our Universe tells us that we are in the bulk of moduli space, and not in an asymptotic boundary region
Rudelius 2021b
.

Obied et al. 2018
;
Sun and Zhang 2021

•

For DE,
Wang 2020
find
\mathfrak{s}_{1}\lesssim 1.62
for an extended
\Lambda
CDMcosmology with 18 parameters, respectively
\mathfrak{s}_{1}\lesssim 1.60
for a Chevallier–Polarski–Linder
Chevallier and Polarski 2001
;
Linder 2003
(CPL)parametrisation.

•

Every known string theory potential satisfies

\frac{\norm{\nabla V}}{V}\geq\frac{2}{\sqrt{d-2}}

(130)

in the asymptotic limit
Castellano et al. 2022
;
Castellano et al. 2023a
;
Van Riet and Zoccarato 2023
;
Rudelius 2021b
;
Alestas et al. 2024
;
McAllister and Quevedo 2023
;
Grimm et al. 2020
;
Obied et al. 2018
;
Andriot 2019
;
Andriot et al. 2020a
;
Calderón-Infante et al. 2023b
;
Shiu et al. 2023b
;
Shiu et al. 2023c
;
Cremonini et al. 2023a
;
Hebecker et al. 2023
;
Rudelius 2022
;
Rudelius 2021a
;
Bedroya and Vafa 2020
;
Andriot et al. 2023a
;
Rudelius 2022
;
Etheredge et al. 2022
;
Rudelius 2021b
;
Rudelius 2021a
.
124
124

124

Calderón-Infante et al. 2023b
present counterexamples to the bound
\mathfrak{s}_{1}=2/\sqrt{d-2}
in CYflux compactifications with competing terms of the potential in the asymptotic limit by studying the gradient flow trajectories among more general infinite distance limits, besides the usual large volume and weak string coupling limits. However, those counterexamples have not been rigorously tested, as only a subsector of the moduli field space was explored.

–

With this bound, the dSCis exactly conserved under dimensional reduction
Rudelius 2021b
;
Rudelius 2021a
.

–

It can be obtained by combining the dSCwith the EP
Lee et al. 2022a
and the refined DC
Etheredge et al. 2022
.

–

This bound still allows dSspace with DE; however, its lifetime
\tau
is limited to

e^{\tau\sqrt{\Lambda}}\lesssim\frac{1}{\sqrt{\Lambda}}\Rightarrow\tau\lesssim\frac{1}{\sqrt{\Lambda}}\log\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{\Lambda}}\right)

(131)

as the horizon scales as
\frac{1}{\sqrt{\Lambda}}
and regions smaller than the Planck scale cannot exit the horizon and freeze
Vafa 2024a
.
125
125

125

Vafa 2024a
states that this also explains the puzzling observation that
t_{0}\sim\frac{1}{\sqrt{\Lambda}}
, i.e. that the current age of our Universe corresponds to the DEhorizon, since this is the typical time in such a universe. This comes with the worrying consequence that the dSphase is about to end. However, this could be circumvented if DEis dynamical and not a cosmological constant.

–

The bound
\frac{\norm{\nabla V}}{V}\geq\frac{2}{\sqrt{d-2}}
does not directly translate into a bound for the value of
\mathfrak{s}_{1}
in the bulk.

–

Such a strong bound does not allow for accelerated expansion, unless the Universe inhibits negative spatial curvature
Andriot et al. 2023a
;
Alestas et al. 2024
. However, the required curvature needs to be so strong that
\Omega_{k}
would be a dominant contribution to the Universe’s energy budget, which is incompatible with observations
Alestas et al. 2024
.

–

A simple scalar field model with
V\sim\exp\left(-\mathfrak{p}\phi\right)
has the EoS
w=-1+\frac{\mathfrak{p}^{2}}{2}\frac{d-2}{d-1}
, such that
\mathfrak{p}>\mathfrak{s}_{1}
yields the SECthat rules out an accelerating expansion of the Universe
Rudelius 2021b
.

Rudelius 2021b
concludes that this condition, and therefore a strong application of the dSC, is only viable in the asymptotic limits of the scalar field itself.

•

Slow-roll inflation suggests
\mathfrak{s}_{1}<\sqrt{2}
, as otherwise
\epsilon_{V}\ll 1
is violated
Garg and Krishnan 2019
.

•

Heisenberg et al. 2018a
claim that
\mathfrak{s}_{1}\leq 1.35
satisfies both, observational and theoretical constraints.
126
126

126

Akrami et al. 2019
consider the approach of extending existing
1\sigma
and
2\sigma
contours to
3\sigma
used by
Heisenberg et al. 2018a
incorrect, and suggest a full statistical analysis.
Heisenberg et al. 2018b
reply that the purpose of their work was to check if the discrepancy is strong (order of magnitude difference) or more subtle, for which they deemed their approach of extrapolating the
1\sigma
and
2\sigma
contours sufficient. No such strong tension is found.

•

\mathfrak{s}_{1}^{\textnormal{NEC}}=2\sqrt{\left(D-d\right)/\left[\left(D-2\right)\left(d-2\right)\right]}
respecting the NEC
127
127

127

Obied et al. 2018
deem this bound too strong, as observational bounds indicate that
\mathfrak{s}_{1}\leq 0.6

Agrawal et al. 2018a
. Furthermore, the NECcan be violated quantum mechanically. However, see

for a conjecture that states that theories violating the NECare in the swampland.

Obied et al. 2018
;

.

•

\order{1}
values for
\mathfrak{s}_{1}
were also derived studying a 10d string background with 4d dSsubspaces

.

•

for all viable quintessence models with

(note that such models always fail

Raveri et al. 2019
).

•

The TCCprovides a lower bound on the variable
\mathfrak{s}_{1}\geq 2/\sqrt{\left(d-1\right)\left(d-2\right)}

Basile and Lanza 2020
;
Bedroya and Vafa 2020
;
Calderón-Infante et al. 2023b
;
Bedroya 2020
;

;

. It is not violated in any string theory compactification studied by

(

;

;

The same bound is derived from Hodge theory

Bedroya and Vafa 2020
.
The bound stems from trajectories driven by exponential potentials combined with positive energy contributions from a tower of states — leaving the tower aside yields the
\mathfrak{s}_{1}\geq 2/\sqrt{d-2}
discussed above
Agmon et al. 2023
.

•

Casas and Ruiz 2025
link the EPand the SSCto the DCand the dSC, to find a bound that links the dSCto the DC:
\mathfrak{s}_{1}\geq 2/\mathfrak{a}\left(d-2\right)
, with
\mathfrak{a}\geq 1/\sqrt{d-2}
the
\order{1}

•

find a lower bound of
\mathfrak{s}_{1}<0.6
, (and an upper bound of

) for

\Omega_{\phi}(z=0)\approx 0.7
and
\Omega_{\phi}(z>1)\ll 1
.

•

For DBImodels,
Wang 2020
found an upper bound of
\mathfrak{s}_{1}\lesssim 0.58
, whereas for inflationary models with concave potentials
\mathfrak{s}_{1}\lesssim 0.14
is obtained.

•

In a string gas setting,
Laliberte and Brandenberger 2020

\absolutevalue{V^\prime_\textnormal{eff}}/V_{\textnormal{eff}}\geq 1/\left(D-d\right)
.

•

Agrawal et al. 2021
use
\mathfrak{s}_{1}=0.1
, which is one order of magnitude away from
1
.

•

Even a value of
\mathfrak{s}_{1}=0.01
is not explicitly ruled out, according to
Palti 2019
.

To summarise, we can state that the jury on the exact value of
\mathfrak{s}_{1}
is still out, as the applicability of an exact bound derived for the asymptotic limit in the bulk, where cosmology happens, is questionable. However, it can act as a guiding principle for model building.

Combined Conjecture

The motivation to formulate a combined conjecture (
eq.

14
) comes from the critique that the refined dSCcomes as two distinct conditions on two different quantities:
\epsilon_{V}
and
\eta_{V}
.
The constraint on
\epsilon_{V}
is derived in a semi-classical, weak-coupling limit, while the second constraint comes directly from requiring
\eta_{V}\geq-1

Liu 2021a
.
The combined dSCcombines the two slow-roll parameters into one condition
Andriot and Roupec 2019
;
Sadeghi et al. 2023a
;
Roupec 2021
:

\left(2\epsilon_{V}\right)^{\mathfrak{c}/2}-\mathfrak{p}_{2}\eta_{V}\geq 1-\mathfrak{p}_{2}.

(132)

Both variations of the dSCrule out slow-roll scenarios with small
\epsilon_{V}
and positive
\eta_{V}
, but make different assessments for intermediate values
Roupec 2021
.

•

Inflationary models with
\mathfrak{p}_{2}\simeq 1
(and
\mathfrak{p}_{1}\ll 1
) are allowed by the combined dSC, i.e. even single-field slow-roll models, especially if they satisfy
\epsilon_{V}\leq-\eta_{V}\simeq\mathfrak{p}_{1}\ll 1

Roupec 2021
.

•

V\left(\phi\right)=V_{0}e^{-\mathfrak{s}_{1}\phi}
is not in agreement with the combined conjecture but could be in agreement with
eq.

12
if
\mathfrak{s}_{1}<0.6
was allowed
Agrawal et al. 2018a
.

•

V\left(\phi\right)=\frac{V_{0}}{2}\left(1-\tanh\left(\mathfrak{p}_{2}\phi\right)\right)
with
V_{0},\,\mathfrak{p}_{2}>0
would be in agreement with the combined conjecture but not necessarily with the original dSC
Roupec 2021
.

•

Sadeghi et al. 2023a
present a model of inflation using non-local Friedmann equations that is compatible with the combined bound
eq.

14
but violates
eqs.

12
and
13
.

•

Yuennan and Channuie 2023
examine four different models of inflation with composite scalar fields and find that the models they tested are in tension with the refined dSC, but not necessarily with the combined bound.

•

Liu 2021a
investigates the compatibility between the combined dSCand Higgs inflation, Palantini–Higgs inflation, and a Higgs–Dilaton model. While the former two are compatible with the combined dSC, only the Higgs–Dilaton model is compatible with the refined dSC.
128
128

128

In later work,
Liu 2022
presents the constraints on the potentials coming from the scalar WGC(see
section

II.19.2
).

•

Inflationary models in mimetic
129
129

129

In mimetic gravity, inflation, DE, and DMare explained without additional scalar fields but as inherent characteristics of gravity. See
Sebastiani et al. 2017
for a review.

f(R,T)=R+\delta T
gravity, with
T
the trace of the stress-energy tensor, respectively in
f(\mathfrak{G})
gravity
130
130

130

\mathfrak{G}
being the GBterm from
eq.

269
.
, are shown to be incompatible with the refined dSC(as
\mathfrak{s}_{1},\mathfrak{s}_{2}\sim\order{0.1}
) but have a window of compatibility with the combined dSC
Gashti et al. 2022b
;
Gashti et al. 2023
.
131
131

131

For
f(R)
theories with
f(R)\propto R^{\mathfrak{l}}
the combined dSCwith
\mathfrak{c}>2
reads

\frac{2\left(\mathfrak{l}-2\right)^{2}}{3\left(\mathfrak{l}-1\right)^{2}}\left[\left(\frac{2\left(\mathfrak{l}-2\right)^{2}}{3\left(\mathfrak{l}-1\right)^{2}}\right)^{\frac{\mathfrak{c}}{2}-1}-\mathfrak{p}_{2}\right]>1-\mathfrak{p}_{2},

which holds for
1<\mathfrak{l}<\sqrt{6}-1

Artymowski and Ben-Dayan 2019
.

We depict the parameter space for different values of
\mathfrak{c}
and
\mathfrak{p}_{2}
, and compare it to the swampland region predicted by the refined dSCin
fig.

Figure 2:
The refined dSC(meshed box) and the combined dSC(shaded regions) overlap for a large fraction of the parameter space, but do not exactly match. The landscape of EFTsis to the right and below the curves respectively outside the box. The shaded regions are the swampland. The combined dSChas free parameters, which allow for different curves:
The minimal
\mathfrak{c}=2
is depicted as dashed lines. A higher exponent makes the swampland bigger as it rules out more intermediate
\{\mathfrak{s}_{1},\,\mathfrak{s}_{2}\}

\mathfrak{s}_{1}
(actually for larger values of
\absolutevalue{\nabla V}/V
, for which we use

A higher value of
\mathfrak{p}_{2}
(darker shades) rules out more high-value pairs, where
\mathfrak{s}_{1}>1
and
\mathfrak{s}_{2}>1

\mathfrak{s}_{2}
closer to

when
\mathfrak{s}_{1}
is small.
In the lower-right corner of the refined dSCbox is a region that is allowed by the combined dSC.
Roupec 2021
found dSsaddle points in this region.

Some authors consider the combined dSCas too loose, given the success of various inflation models that are deemed incompatible with the refined dSC.

For example

, or

II.5
Distance Conjecture

The
distance conjecture
is often referred to as the
Swampland Distance Conjecture (SDC)
. To avoid confusion with the
de Sitter Conjecture (dSC)
we use the abbreviation
DC
for the distance conjecture.

The DCsays that in a theory that couples to gravity, with a moduli space
133
133

133

The moduli space
\mathcal{M}
cannot be compact and has at least one boundary point at infinite distance
Grimm et al. 2022a
(see also
Hamada et al. 2022
).

\mathcal{M}
parametrised by the expectation values of
a scalar field
\phi\in\mathcal{M}
with no potential,

\forall\phi\in\mathcal{M}\,\exists\,\phi_{\text{b}}\in\partial\mathcal{M}

(133)

such that the geodesic distance
d(\phi,\phi_{\text{b}})
between
\phi_{\text{b}}
on the boundary and
\phi
in the bulk is infinite,
and that there is an infinite tower of states with an associated mass scale
m
, where

m(\phi)\sim m(\phi_{0})\exp(-\mathfrak{a} d(\phi,\phi_0)),

(134)

where
\phi_{0}
is sufficiently far away from
\phi
and
\mathfrak{a}\geq 0
is a real
Grimm et al. 2022a

constant
Palti 2019
;
Ooguri et al. 2019

of order one
Graña and Herráez 2021
;
Basile and Montella 2023
;
Blumenhagen et al. 2018
;
Hebecker et al. 2017a
;
Ashmore and Ruehle 2021
;
Ooguri and Vafa 2007
.
Not only does the DCstate that such an infinite tower of states becomes massless at infinite distance, it also quantifies this behaviour as exactly exponential
Grimm et al. 2019
;
Ooguri and Vafa 2007
and specifies this exponential suppression as the geodesic distance
Grimm et al. 2022a
.
134
134

134

It is worth noting that
m(\phi)>M_{\textnormal{P}}
,
m(\phi_{0})>M_{\textnormal{P}}
is possible. In that case, the DCplaces no meaningful constraints on the EFT
Rudelius 2023a
.

II.5.1
Implications for Cosmology

If you have a scalar field that travels a long range in its field space, you will get a tower of states, i.e. particles, of which the lowest mass scale is given by

m(\phi)=M_{\textnormal{P}}e^{-\mathfrak{a}\Delta\phi}

(135)

with
\sqrt{1/2}\leq\mathfrak{a}\leq\sqrt{3/2}
.
This finding is applied to a plethora of settings, as we elucidate in the following.

Boson stars

are found to satisfy the DC
Choi et al. 2019
.

Dark Sector

A model independent analysis of observational data found the DCto be compatible with observations at low redshift
Arjona and Nesseris 2021
.
135
135

135

A parametrisation-dependent Bayesian machine learning algorithm that reconstructs
H(z)=H_{0}+H_{1}z^{2}/\left(1+z\right)
finds that the DCis well respected by DE, yet, a sign switch in the DEEoStakes place within the redshift range
z\in\left[0,\,5\right]
, i.e. DEis in the phantom regime for some time
Elizalde and Khurshudyan 2021
. The reconstructed model satisfies the gradient dSCcriterion
eq.

12
but violates the Hessian criterion
eq.

13
.

We present more details on concrete models in the following.

Quintessence

generally satisfies the DC, as a model-agnostic reconstruction of quintessence yields
\absolutevalue{\Delta\phi}<0.23
at
2\sigma

Park et al. 2021
.
Various formulations of single-field quintessence cosmologies agree well with the DC, as stated by
Schöneberg et al. 2023
.

Emelin and Tatar 2019
find that for axion-like quintessence with a hilltop potential stemming from a Kähler modulus, a modified potential that shows a minimum along the axionic direction is needed to satisfy the DC(as well as the dSC).

Thompson 2018
;
Thompson 2019
presents
beta function quintessence
that satisfies the DCyet violates the dSC, as the
\order{1}
of
\mathfrak{s}_{1}
cannot be reached. The refined dSCis not tested as no value for
\mathfrak{s}_{2}
is derived. The model introduces a function
\beta(\phi)=\mathrm{d}\phi/\mathrm{d}\log a
and repackages the scalar field potential as

V(\phi)=\frac{-6H_{0}}{4}e^{-\int\!\beta(\phi)\,\mathrm{d}\phi}\left(1-\frac{\beta^{2}(\phi)}{6}\right),

(136)

which achieves that the Hubble constant as a cosmological observable is directly part of the potential.

Thompson 2018
;
Thompson 2019
studies four explicit cases with
\beta_{i}
constants:

Exponential

V(\phi)=\frac{-6H_{0}}{4}e^{-\beta_{\textnormal{e}}}\left(1-\frac{\beta_{\textnormal{e}}^{2}}{6}\right)

Logarithm

V(\phi)=\frac{-6\beta_{\textnormal{l}}H_{0}}{4}\log\left(\phi\right)\left(1-\frac{\beta_{\textnormal{l}}^{2}}{6\left(\phi\log\phi\right)^{2}}\right)

Power-Law

V(\phi)=\frac{-6H_{0}}{4}\phi^{\beta_{\textnormal{p}}}\left(1-\frac{\beta_{\textnormal{p}}^{2}}{6\phi^{2}}\right)

Inverse Power-Law

V(\phi)=\frac{-6H_{0}}{4}\phi^{-\beta_{\textnormal{i}}}\left(1-\frac{\beta_{\textnormal{i}}^{2}}{6\phi^{2}}\right)

For a DEEoSof
\omega_{0}=0.98
each model satisfies the DCwith
\Delta\phi\in\left(0.45,\,0.58\right)
, yet violates the first dSC, as the gradient is too flat with
\nabla V/V\sim 0.1
.

Thawing Quintessence

Storm and Scherrer 2020
find that the DCbecomes redundant for thawing quintessence models (or hilltop quintessence) that are in agreement with observational data.
Montefalcone et al. 2020
partially agree, yet reject hilltop potentials (as well as scenarios with a turning point): in such models, the kinetic energy changes too rapidly, violating observational constraints from LSSand BBN(as such a quintessence field would dominate over matter and radiation far earlier than observed) as well as the refined dSC.
Tada and Terada 2024
derive a thawing quintessence model and axion-like quintessence by reconstructing
\omega_{0}\omega_{a}
CDM from DESI-data. Both potentials satisfy the DC(as well as the dSCand the WGC).

Multi-field quintessence

seems to naturally satisfy the DCbound:
For a setting with two fields and action

{AmSalign}

S=∫​

-g

(

RM

P

2

2

-V(
ϕ
_1)

-

(

)

2

2

-

λ
(

(

2

) d^4x,
EoM

\displaystyle\ddot{\phi}_{1}+3H\dot{\phi}_{1}-\lambda\lambda^{\prime}\dot{\phi}_{2}^{2}+V^{\prime}

\displaystyle=0

(137)

\displaystyle\ddot{\phi}_{2}+3H\dot{\phi}_{2}+2\lambda^{\prime}\dot{\phi}_{2}\dot{\phi}_{1}/\lambda

\displaystyle=0,

(138)

and geodesic distance

\Delta\phi=\int\!\sqrt{G_{ab}\dot{\phi}^{a}\dot{\phi}^{b}}\,\mathrm{d}t,

(139)

the field traversal is given by

\frac{\Delta\phi}{M_{\textnormal{P}}}=\int\!\sqrt{-\left(\frac{3}{2M_{\textnormal{P}}}\frac{\lambda}{\lambda^{\prime}}\right)^{2}+3\frac{V^{\prime}}{V}\frac{\lambda}{\lambda^{\prime}}}\,\mathrm{d}N,

(140)

which, for
N_{e}\leq 10
(during a DEdominated epoch), yields
\Delta\phi/M_{\textnormal{P}}\sim N_{e}/\sqrt{-M_{\textnormal{P}}\lambda^{\prime}/\lambda}<1
, which satisfies the DCbound
Cicoli et al. 2020b
.
136
136

136

\lambda
is a model-defining function, an overdot represents a derivative with respect to time, and a prime a derivative with respect to the scalar field
\phi
.

However, the models are constraint by their turning rate
\mathfrak{T}

Freigang et al. 2023
:

\mathfrak{T}<\mathfrak{l}\sqrt{-\dot{H}},

(141)

with
\mathfrak{l}
an
\order{1}
constant that depends on the curvature of the moduli space and the decay rate of the mass scale of the tower of particles, and
H
the Hubble parameter. This means that a field that drives an accelerated cosmic expansion cannot undergo rapid turns or deviate from geodesics too strongly. The definition of the turning rate
\mathfrak{T}
needs some context, which is all explained by
Freigang et al. 2023
: Assume a setting with
a=1,\,\dotsc,\,n
scalar fields
\phi^{a}
. When the fields are free, massless, and homogeneous, the Lagrangian
L
in Minkowski spacetime
\eta
can be written as

L=-\frac{1}{2}\eta^{\mu\nu}G_{ab}\partial_{\mu}\phi^{a}\partial_{\nu}\phi^{b},

(142)

with
G_{ab}
the internal field space metric. The EoMcan then be written as follows:

\displaystyle\ddot{\phi}^{a}+\Gamma^{a}_{bc}\dot{\phi}^{b}\dot{\phi}^{c}=

\displaystyle\,0

(143)

\displaystyle\Gamma^{a}_{bc}=

\displaystyle\,\frac{1}{2}G^{ad}\left(\frac{\partial G_{bd}}{\partial\phi^{c}}+\frac{\partial G_{cd}}{\partial\phi^{b}}-\frac{\partial G_{bc}}{\partial\phi^{d}}\right)

(144)

\displaystyle\mathrm{D}_{t}v^{a}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=

\displaystyle\,\dot{v}^{a}+\Gamma^{a}_{bc}v^{b}\dot{\phi}^{c}

(145)

\displaystyle\mathrm{D}_{t}\dot{\phi}^{a}=

\displaystyle\,0,

(146)

where
\Gamma^{a}_{bc}
are the Christoffel symbols of the moduli space,
v^{a}
is any vector,
\mathrm{D}_{t}
is the covariant derivative on the field space, and
eq.

146
is the now much shorter EoM. The turning rate is now defined as

\mathfrak{T}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\absolutevalue{\mathrm{D}_tT}=\sqrt{G_{ab}\mathrm{D}_{t}T^{a}\mathrm{D}_{t}T^{b}},

(147)

where we introduced the tangent vector

T^{a}=\frac{\dot{\phi}^{a}}{\sqrt{G_{ab}\dot{\phi}^{a}\dot{\phi}^{b}}}.

(148)

Seeing the definition of
\mathfrak{T}
allows us to make another interpretation of it: it’s a measure of how
‘well’
parallel transport of
T^{a}
along
\phi^{a}
works, i.e.
\mathfrak{T}
measures the
non-geodesity
. The importance of this will become clear when studying the general remarks on distance measures and geodesics below.

Eskilt et al. 2022
present a spinning two-field model of DEthat satisfies the DC. We discuss it in
section

II.4.1
.

Payeur et al. 2024
study rapid-turn DE, a multi-field DEmodel in highly curved field space, and find that the DCas well as the TCCare violated, except for a small region in the parameter space, where both conjectures, as well as the dSCand observational constraints can be met. In their model, expansion is realised as non-geodesic motion in a highly curved field space. An attractor solution with an EoSof
\omega\simeq-1
keeps the accelerated expansion eternal (which violates the TCC) as the field can keep rolling (which violates the DCwhen it rolls a trans-Planckian distance). Their model is related to hyperinflation and has a two-field action
S
with field space metric
G_{ab}
and potential
V(\phi)
:

\displaystyle S

\displaystyle=\int\!\sqrt{-g}\left(\frac{G_{ab}(\phi)\partial_{\mu}\phi^{a}\partial_{\nu}\phi^{b}}{2}-V(\phi)\right)\,\mathrm{d}^{4}x

(149)

\displaystyle G_{ab}

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
0&e^{-\phi/M_{\textnormal{P}}L}\end{pmatrix}

(150)

\displaystyle L

\displaystyle=\sqrt{-1/M_{\textnormal{P}}^{2}R}

(151)

\displaystyle V(\phi)

\displaystyle=V_{0}e^{-\mathfrak{c}\phi/M_{\textnormal{P}}}

(152)

with
R
the Ricci scalar of the field space. Observational constraints require
\mathfrak{c}L\leq 0.0265
. The EoSis
\omega\approx-1+2\mathfrak{c}L
. The DCand the TCCare violated at the same time if

690L\left(\mathfrak{c}L+1\right)=-2\mathfrak{c}L+1,

(153)

i.e. the field curvature is bounded by
L\approx 1/690
.

Brinkmann et al. 2022
discuss that multi-field quintessence is tricky to implement in string theory: if the system starts in a matter dominated phase, the system does typically
not
evolve into a state with
\omega_{\textnormal{DE}}\simeq-1
and
\Omega_{\textnormal{DE}}\simeq 0.7
. This can be achieved if the system starts in an epoch of kinetic domination. However, even then, fine-tuning is required to match today’s Universe.

DMand EDE

EDEis widely studied as a remedy to the Hubble tension, yet it appears to be incompatible with the observed LSSsand their formation in the concordance model
Schöneberg et al. 2022
;
Di Valentino et al. 2021
.

Kaloper 2019
takes inspiration from the DC
137
137

137

He incorrectly calls it the WGCin his paper, yet the WGCis concerned with charged particles. However, he refers to the mass and towers of states — exactly what the DCis about.

to propose a model of EDEas an axion-like field that rapidly decays into radiation at a redshift of
z\sim 3000\sim 5000
to solve the Hubble tension. The tower of states is a testable prediction by this model.

Furthermore, it is suggestive to interpret the predicted light tower of states as DM, in particular DMcoupled to DE. Various studies come to positive conclusions regarding the compatibility of such models with observational and swampland constraints.
For example,
McDonough et al. 2022
are inspired by the DC, and investigate if it is possible to construct a LSSformation–compatible EDEmodel that reduces the Hubble tension by introducing a coupling of DMto DE,
138
138

138

Vafa 2024a
states that the unification of DEand DMis a natural expectation from the DC, and
Carroll 1998
even shows that there is a coupling unless a symmetry prevents it.

where the DMmass is given by
m_{\text{DM}}=m_{0}e^{-\mathfrak{a}\Delta\phi/M_{\textnormal{P}}}
.
139
139

139

In their paper, they write
m_{\text{DM}}=m_{0}e^{c\phi/M_{\textnormal{P}}}
and mention that
c>0
, and the initial field value,
\phi_{i}>0
,
decreases
over cosmic time. We have a leading minus sign in our notation, as we use
\mathfrak{a}>0,\,\Delta\phi>0
.

The DEpotential resembles an axion potential
V(\phi)=V_{0}\left[1-\cos\left(\phi/f\right)\right]^{n}
. The model successfully reduces the Hubble tension to
1\sigma\sim 2\sigma
. LSSgrowth is also addressed:
\mathfrak{a}>0
increases LSSgrowth, as matter–radiation equality takes places earlier and the DMfrom their model is self-interacting, i.e. it has an attractive fifth force. To reduce the
S_{8}
tension,
\mathfrak{a}<0
works best. Using different datasets, they find
\mathfrak{a}<0.1
, which is in tension with the
\order{1}
predictions of the DCfor the value of this parameter. However, we suspect that the fit could be improved if only a fraction of DMwere coupled to DE. Such a model is studied by
Wang and Piao 2023
: Having only a fraction of DMinteracting with DEhelps alleviate the
S_{8}
tension, but a vanishing coupling agrees with observational data as well. The issue is that matter clusters in the matter-dominated era, and a higher Hubble constant
H_{0}
increases
S_{8}
.
Another (DC- and dSC-inspired) model that addresses the Hubble tension by coupling DEto DMis presented by
Agrawal et al. 2021
:

The DMis the tower of states that becomes light for large field excursions, with

Even though not intended by

Since the exponential suppression of the DMmass only starts at late time, when
\phi

The strength of the coupling between DMand DEis constraint by late-time observations such as tidal tails of satellite galaxies and warping of stellar disks within galaxies, as the coupling introduces a fifth force in the dark sector that affects the DM — if only a fraction of the DMparticipates in the interaction, an observational effect can be illusive.

141

However,
Bruck and Thomas 2019
