Also
Baldes et al. 2019
state observational probes for DM–DEcoupling of different models:

Quintessence

with a single- or a multi-exponential potential can satisfy the DCand the dSCif fine-tuning takes place.

Self-interacting DM

with a light scalar mediator whose mass depends on quintessence matches constraints from dwarf galaxies if the mediator mass varies by a factor of
{10}^{-9}
per year.

Kinetic mixing

between U(1) sectors that is controlled by quintessence is hard to detect as the late-time mixing is suppressed, yet a lower bound on DMmass in the early Universe constrains the model.

Quintessence–Higgs coupling

shifts the Higgs parameter, which in turn modifies the timescale for vacuum decay.

Further DEFindings

The following work mislabels the DCas the WGC, yet derives conclusions about DEbased on the idea that field ranges have to be sub-Planckian:
142
142

142

Sub-Planckian field ranges are not a general feature of the WGC. The electric WGCcould be satisfied by trans-Planckian BHs(in a very mild form of the WGCat least), and the magnetic WGCcan be satisfied by a very weak coupling.
However, in the case of axions, the WGCactually limits the field range to be sub-Planckian, as we discuss in
section

II.19.1
.

Ma and Zhang 2008
show that holographic DE
Li 2004
with a density given by
\rho=3\mathfrak{p}^{2}M_{\textnormal{P}}^{2}/\Lambda^{2}
is compatible with the DCfor
\mathfrak{p}\geq 1
and corresponds to quintessence in this case. This does not indicate that quintessence is generally in the landscape, as
Wu and Zhu 2008
show that a Chaplygin-gas model in the form of quintessence does not satisfy the DC. It also does not prove that holographic DEgenerally works:
Chen et al. 2008
;
Liu et al. 2010
show that holographic DEis incompatible with observational data if compatible with the DC. This is contrasted by work of
Sun 2011
;
Sun and Yue 2011
, which shows that holographic DE
is
compatible with the DC, if it has interaction terms and the Universe is curved.
Sun 2011
;
Chen et al. 2011
suggest that DEthat decays into DMis compatible with the DC.

Gravitinos

The limit when the gravitino mass
m_{\textnormal{3/2}}
goes to zero is explored by

Castellano et al. 2021
;
Cribiori et al. 2021a
.
They observe that when the gravitino mass vanishes, the EFTbreaks down and an infinite tower with
m\sim m_{\textnormal{3/2}}^{\mathfrak{g}}
appears.
Castellano et al. 2021
show a direct link to the DCand claim that this goes as
\sim e^{-\mathfrak{a}d(\phi,\phi_{0})}
, such that
\mathfrak{g}
is related to
\mathfrak{a}
and is in the range of
\frac{1}{3}\leq\mathfrak{g}\leq 1
for 4d type IIA orientifolds.
143
143

143

m=\mathfrak{c}m_{\textnormal{3/2}}^{\mathfrak{g}}=\tilde{\mathfrak{c}}\exp\left(-\mathfrak{a}d(\phi,\phi_{0})\right)
with
\mathfrak{c}
and
\tilde{\mathfrak{c}}
two constants would be an exact expression. For vanishing gravitino mass and infinite distance, the constant contributions can be neglected, and we find that
\mathfrak{a}=-\mathfrak{g}\log\left(m_{\textnormal{3/2}}\right)/d(\phi,\phi_{0})=\sqrt{3/2}\mathfrak{g}
, where in the last step an explicit relation between the gravitino mass and the distance along this path from
Castellano et al. 2021
was used. Together with the discovered range for
\mathfrak{g}
, this leads to bounds for
\mathfrak{a}
:
1/\sqrt{6}\leq\mathfrak{a}\leq\sqrt{3/2}

Castellano et al. 2021
, which is consistent with the lower DCbound in 4d of
\mathfrak{a}\geq 1/\sqrt{2}
. The lower bound of
1/\sqrt{6}
is also confirmed in a type IIB string theory setting, using Hodge theory
Bastian et al. 2021
.

Moreover, they make phenomenological predictions: To have supersymmetry at the
1\text{\,}\mathrm{TeV}
–scale, a KKtower
\order{10^8}
is required.
Furthermore, the Hubble constant (after inflation) is bounded by

H\lesssim m_{\textnormal{3/2}}^{\mathfrak{g}}M_{\textnormal{P}}^{\left(1-\mathfrak{g}\right)}.

(154)

Their main idea is that in an EFTwith a consistent extension into QG, the gravitino mass is not arbitrarily separated from the UVscale and the limit
m_{\textnormal{3/2}}\rightarrow 0
is at infinite distance.
144
144

144

Even before the swampland programme was formally initiated,
Kallosh and Linde 2004
showed that
H\lesssim m_{\textnormal{3/2}}
has to hold in KKLT, and that if AdSis uplifted to dS,
m_{\textnormal{3/2}}\approx V_{\textnormal{AdS}}/3
.

The bound is compatible with the SSCand the AdSDC. Further constraints come from the magnetic WGC(
H<\Lambda_{\textnormal{S}}<g_{\textnormal{3/2}}M_{\textnormal{P}}
) and the FLB(
m_{\textnormal{3/2}}>\sqrt{q_{\textnormal{3/2}}g_{\textnormal{3/2}}M_{\textnormal{P}}H}
).

A vanishing gravitino mass leading to inconsistencies is also observed by
Coudarchet et al. 2021
;
Cribiori and Farakos 2023
.
A tower of states becoming light, and therefore missing in the EFTdescription, is observed when the gravitino sound speed vanishes and particles with arbitrary momentum (above the cutoff) are produced
Kolb et al. 2021a
(see our section on the GSC).

Horizons

The DCalso sheds some light on asymptotic observables and the question of what we can see in the infinite future or on the cosmic horizon — if such asymptotic observables exist in cosmology —, as the work by
Rudelius 2021b
shows: In AdSspace or asymptotically flat spacetimes, we can define asymptotic observables via correlation functions of conformal field theories using the AdS/CFTcorrespondence or via the S-matrix elements, respectively. However, in FLRWcosmology, we are facing a challenge: starting with a singularity in the infinite past prevents an S-matrix description, and in the infinite future, we do not expect a zero energy density on a fixed Cauchy slice of spacetime, which leaves us with geometry fluctuations that might extend infinitely. Furthermore, there is, for any observer, always an unobservable part of the Universe, which can contain an infinite amount of energy and information, i.e. the global state of the Universe is not observable and cannot be described by an S-matrix. If well-defined observables in the asymptotic limit of our Universe exist is unclear; however, having light massive states would make it more likely than having massless modes. The DCpredicts the existence of such light massive states.

Rudelius 2022
studies late-time cosmologies under the assumption that late-time cosmologies are governed by the infinite-distance limits of scalar fields, which feature exponential decays of their potential

V(\phi)\sim\exp\left(-\lambda\phi\right),

(155)

such that the Hubble scales goes like

H\sim\sqrt{\dot{\phi}^{2}+2V(\phi)}\sim\exp\left(-\mathfrak{a}_{H}\phi\right),

(156)

with (
d
the number of large dimensions)

\mathfrak{a}_{H}=\min\left(\frac{\lambda}{2}\,,\,\sqrt{\frac{d-1}{d-2}}\right).

(157)

If
\lambda\geq 2\sqrt{\left(d-1\right)\left(d-2\right)}
, the kinetic energy of the scalar field dominates its potential energy. Now, we have
m\sim\exp\left(-\mathfrak{a}\phi\right)
from the DCand
H\sim\exp\left(\mathfrak{a}_{H}\phi\right)
.
If
\mathfrak{a}>\mathfrak{a}_{H}
, the late-time cosmology is either dominated by the KKtower or the fundamental string (whichever of the two is the lightest tower).
145
145

145

According to the EP, these are the only two options.

If it is the KKtower,
m_{\text{KK}}<H
, which implies that the compactified dimension
m_{\text{KK}}^{-1}
becomes larger than the Hubble horizon
H^{-1}
, i.e. we are no longer in a
d
-dimensional FLRWuniverse. If it is the fundamental string, then the universe is dominated by the stringy effects.
If
\mathfrak{a}<\mathfrak{a}_{H}
, the cosmology remains a
d
-dimensional FLRWcosmology with expanding spacetime — accelerated expansion for
\mathfrak{a}_{H}<1/\sqrt{d-2}
, decelerated expansion
\mathfrak{a}_{H}>1/\sqrt{d-2}
. The light tower of states remains at or above the Hubble scale, and the strong and dominant energy conditions are both satisfied. Unless there is an asymptotic boundary at future spacelike infinity, the Universe cannot accelerate indefinitely in asymptotic scalar field space. Therefore,
\mathfrak{a}_{H}\geq 1/\sqrt{d-2}
is required. These considerations can be summarised in the following bounds:

\sqrt{\left(d-1\right)/\left(d-2\right)}\geq\mathfrak{a}_{H}\geq\mathfrak{a}\geq 1/\sqrt{d-2}.

(158)

Inflation,

especially large-field inflation, is probably the most striking field of applicability of the DCfrom a cosmologist’s perspective.
146
146

146

Rudelius 2023a
points out that this is not obvious. The DCis concerned with infinite field traversals, which are not necessarily implemented into inflation. The inflaton field might travel only a finite field range or even oscillate, like in the case of axion-like models (see for instance
Valenzuela 2017a
,
sections

II.4.1
,
II.20.26
and
II.19.1
).

Scalisi and Valenzuela 2019
provide a bound for the inflaton field range that also takes into account the exponential decay of the mass scale:

\Delta\phi<\frac{1}{\mathfrak{a}}\log\frac{M_{\textnormal{P}}}{\Lambda_{\textnormal{S}}}.

(159)

A higher cutoff
\Lambda_{\textnormal{S}}
implies a smaller allowed field range
\Delta\phi
, such that infinite field ranges become incompatible with the theory. If we describe inflation with an EFT, then we find that

H\leq\Lambda_{\textnormal{S}},

(160)

and we can deduce that in a successful model of inflation

\Delta\phi<\frac{1}{\mathfrak{a}}\log\frac{M_{\textnormal{P}}}{H}

(161)

must hold
Scalisi and Valenzuela 2019
;
Matsui and Terada 2020
. For slow-roll inflation with
\mathfrak{a}=1
, this translates into
\Delta\phi<10M_{\textnormal{P}}
regarding Planck 2018 CMBdata
Scalisi and Valenzuela 2019
, which is in line with the
refined DC
, which claims that
d(\phi,\phi_{0})<10M_{\textnormal{P}}

Palti 2019
;
Rudelius 2023a
. A smaller value of
\mathfrak{a}
would allow for a bigger
\Delta\phi
. Some authors claim that large-field inflation is ruled out by the DCif
\Delta\phi<M_{\textnormal{P}}
is required
Bjorkmo and Marsh 2019
;
Blumenhagen 2018
;
Blumenhagen et al. 2017
. However, we do not agree with this notion, even though we have not heard of a model that follows this idea: the DCsays that if a field travels a trans-Planckian range, a tower of light states emerges with exponentially suppressed masses. Assuming the inflaton rolls a trans-Planckian range, a tower of light states emerges, with masses much smaller than the Planck mass. This is what happens at the end of inflation, namely during reheating: quarks and leptons emerge, towers of states, with masses much smaller than the Planck scale (
m_{e}\sim$4\text{\times}{10}^{-23}$M_{\textnormal{P}}\sim e^{-51}M_{\textnormal{P}}
,
m_{\text{up}}\sim$2\text{\times}{10}^{-22}$M_{\textnormal{P}}\sim e^{-50}M_{\textnormal{P}}
). Even applying the very stringent (and not particularly well justified or tested) bounds
1/\sqrt{2}<\mathfrak{a}<\sqrt{6}
allows
\Delta\phi\sim\order{10M_\textnormal{P}}
. If the emerging tower was equidistant, we could even deduce the number of states:
N=\frac{M_{\textnormal{P}}}{m}=e^{\Delta\phi}

Bonnefoy et al. 2021
.
The EPstates that the tower is either a KKtower that corresponds to a decompactification, or a tower of string oscillator modes. In the latter case, inflation and the very early Universe have to be described by string cosmology. In the former case, inflation would either describe a decompactification from 3 to 4 spacetime dimensions, or from 4 to 5, if the dark dimension proposal (see
section

II.1.1
) holds.
A challenge such a model has to overcome is that otherwise suppressed operators of
\order{\Delta\phi/M_\textnormal{P}}
have to be properly incorporated in the theory. Furthermore, it is a conceptual challenge to explain why the spacing would be in such a way that we only discovered the lightest states of the SMtowers — there is no continuing electron tower in the entire energy range probed so far. Furthermore, such a model would have to be tested for its compatibility with other swampland conjectures and observational constraints.

In the following, we discuss the implications of the DCfor more established models of inflation.

Slow-roll inflation

gets constraint by combining the DCwith the Lyth bound: The DCgives an upper bound on the geodesic distance
\Delta\phi_{\text{g}}
that separates two points along the inflationary trajectory,
and the Lyth bound — determined by the tensor-to-scalar
147
147

147

Scalar fluctuations are induced by matter inhomogeneities, while tensor fluctuations correspond to GWs
Brandenberger 2021a
.

ratio
r_{\textnormal{ts}}
— gives a lower bound on the non-geodesic distance the inflaton traverses between those two points,
\Delta\phi_{\text{ng}}
;
148
148

148

The background solution trajectory in multi-field inflation can locally bend, which generates interactions between primordial curvature perturbations and field fluctuations orthogonal to the inflaton trajectory, i.e. isocurvature fluctuations
Bravo et al. 2020
.

the two distance measures respect the relation
\Delta\phi_{\text{g}}\leq\Delta\phi_{\text{ng}}

Bravo et al. 2020
by definition.
The Lyth bound
Lyth 1997
sets a lower limit for the field excursion

\frac{\Delta\phi}{M_{\textnormal{P}}}\geq\Delta N_{e}\times\sqrt{\frac{r_{\textnormal{ts}}}{8}},

(162)

which violates the DCin the single-field inflation case, where
\Delta\phi
corresponds to the geodesic distance and has to be sub-Planckian
Landete and Shiu 2018
,
149
149

149

Already
Ooguri and Vafa 2007
pointed out that the DCrules out slow-roll inflation.

but can be compatible with the DCin the multi-field case, where the
\Delta\phi
relevant for the Lyth bound does not necessarily correspond to a geodesic
Bravo et al. 2020
.

Constant-roll inflation,

where terms quadratic in the energy density are present in the Friedmann equation, takes place in a 5d brane-world scenario, and allows for sub-Planckian field excursion while retaining
\eta_{V}\sim\order{1}
, is compatible with the DCas well as the dSC
Mohammadi et al. 2020c
.

A model of anisotropic constant roll inflation with a complex quintessence field tested by
Sadeghi et al. 2022a
violates the DC.

Eternal inflation

is in strong tension with the DCor swampland conjectures in general
Matsui and Takahashi 2019
;
Rudelius 2019
;
Wang et al. 2020
;
Blanco-Pillado et al. 2020
;
Dimopoulos 2018
;
Kinney 2019
;
Brahma and Shandera 2019
;
Seo 2022
;
Matsui and Terada 2020
.
150
150

150

See the work by
Lin 2020
for a one-sentence summary of most of those sources.
In
•
‣
section

II.4.1
, we discuss eternal inflation in the context of the Hartle–Hawking no-boundary proposal
Hartle 2022
;
Hartle and Hawking 1983
in the light of the DCand the dSC.

Seo 2022
argues that eternal inflation is incompatible with the DC:
a rapidly changing inflaton field causes the appearance of a tower of light states, which increases the entropy and the energy inside the cosmological horizon. If this happens quickly enough, the energy density is high enough to produce BHs, i.e. QGeffects are important to describe the particle interactions. The EFTbreaks down, when the entropy inside the cosmological horizon exceeds the CEB.

Cohen et al. 1999
quantify when the EFTbreaks down due to such a particle production in flat space, namely when

l^{3}\Lambda_{\text{UV}}^{4}\lesssim lM_{\textnormal{P}}^{2}\Rightarrow\Lambda_{\text{UV}}\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{M_{\textnormal{P}}}{l}},

(163)

with
\Lambda_{\text{UV}}
the EFTcutoff and
l
the size of the region.
151
151

151

This bound is more stringent that the Bekenstein–Hawking bound
l^{3}\Lambda_{\text{UV}}^{3}\lesssim S_{\text{BH}}=\pi l^{2}M_{\textnormal{P}}^{2}
such that
\Lambda_{\text{UV}}\lesssim\left(M_{\textnormal{P}}^{2}/l\right)^{1/3}
.

Seo 2022
combines this bound
with the DC, to derive a bound on the slow-roll parameter:

\epsilon\gtrsim\frac{H^{2}}{M_{\textnormal{P}}^{2}}+\frac{\mathfrak{a}}{6\sqrt{\pi}}\frac{H^{3}}{M_{\textnormal{P}}^{3}}+\order{\left(\frac{H}{M_\textnormal{P}}\right)^4},

(164)

which almost rules out eternal inflation, for which

\epsilon\lesssim\left(2n+1\right)H^{2}/M_{\textnormal{P}}^{2}

(165)

has to hold,
n
being the quantum number if excitations are considered
Seo 2020b
.

However, eternal inflation is not ruled out completely.
Lin 2020
presents a model of topological inflation with a hilltop potential
on a 3-brane
:
For inflation to continue eternally, the field has to remain near the top of its potential, which happens if it is close to a topological defect (a monopole, a cosmic string, or a domain wall)
Vilenkin 1994
;
Linde 1994a
;
Linde and Linde 1994
. In the inflated region, those defects (such as magnetic monopoles) are diluted away.
Lin 2020
examines the case of a domain wall. Since we do not observe domain walls, their thickness has to be larger than the observable Universe. The Friedmann equation tells us how the Hubble parameter and the inflaton potential relate:

H^{2}=\frac{V}{3M_{\textnormal{P}}^{2}}\left(1+\frac{V}{2\Lambda_{\text{B}}}\right),

(166)

with
\Lambda_{\text{B}}=6\pi\frac{M_{\textnormal{P};5}^{6}}{M_{\textnormal{P}}^{2}}\gtrsim\left($1\text{\,}\mathrm{MeV}$\right)^{4}
(the bound stemming from BBN),
M_{\textnormal{P};5}
the reduced 5-dimensional Planck mass;
the second term in the brackets is only present in a brane-world
Lin 2020
.
In the double-well potential studied by
Lin 2020
, the DCand the dSCare only satisfied with the extra brane term.

Small-field inflation

appears to be compatible with the DC, as
Osses et al. 2021
show, studying different models.
152
152

152

We present the models (and implications from the dSC) in
section

II.4.1
.

However, they do not consider precise bounds on the parameter
\mathfrak{a}
for the DC.

Mohammadi et al. 2022
study several models of inflation on a brane, and find that power-law inflation of the form
V\sim\phi^{n}
, natural inflation of the form
V\sim\left(1-\cos{\frac{\phi}{f}}\right)
, and T-model inflation (
eq.

114
) are compatible with the DC.

Cribiori and Farakos 2023
study single-field D-term inflation, chaotic inflation, and Starobinsky inflation in supergravity and find that if a massless gravitino is present in the theory, the models are in tension with the gravitino DCand the FLB, and last only a few
e
-foldings (
N_{e}=13
in the case of chaotic inflation,
N_{e}=7
in the case of Starobinsky inflation).

Warm inflation

is characterised by the inflaton field
\phi
decaying into radiation at the rate
\Upsilon
, which depends on
\phi
and the temperature of the photon bath,
T

Das and Ramos 2020
:

\displaystyle\ddot{\phi}+3H\dot{\phi}+V^{\prime}

\displaystyle=-\Upsilon(\phi,T)\dot{\phi}

(167)

\displaystyle\rho_{r}+4H\rho_{r}

\displaystyle=\Upsilon(\phi,T)\dot{\phi}^{2}

(168)

\displaystyle H^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{3M_{\textnormal{P}}^{2}}\left(\frac{\dot{\phi}^{2}}{2}+V(\phi)+\rho_{r}\right).

(169)

To evaluate the compatibility with the DCit is beneficial to express the bound in terms of the slow-roll parameter
\epsilon_{V}

Motaharfar et al. 2019
:

\frac{\Delta\phi}{M_{\textnormal{P}}}=\frac{\dot{\phi}}{H}N_{e}\simeq\frac{\sqrt{2\epsilon_{V}}}{1+\Upsilon}N_{e},

(170)

which can per se be satisfied in the strong as well as the weak dissipation regime (small or large
\Upsilon
)
Das 2019b
;
Motaharfar et al. 2019
.
The Lyth bound for warm inflation,

\frac{\Delta\phi}{M_{\textnormal{P}}}\gtrsim\sqrt{\frac{r_{\textnormal{ts}}}{8}\frac{T}{H}\sqrt{1+\Upsilon}}N_{e},

(171)

can as well be satisfied in the strong as well as in the weak dissipation regime: for
N_{e}\sim 60
,
H/T\sim${10}^{-1}$
, and
r_{\textnormal{ts}}<${10}^{-2}$
, the strength of dissipation is not particularly constraint, i.e. even the regime of weak dissipation is completely allowed
Das 2019b
.
Overall, models of warm inflation tend to satisfy the DC:

•

Warm inflation scenarios with dissipative effects with a cubic temperature dependence are compatible with the DC, as well as with the dSCand the TCC
Das and Ramos 2020
;
Santos et al. 2022
.

•

Models with strong dissipation satisfy the DC, the TCC, as well as the dSC
Kamali et al. 2020
;
Das and Ramos 2020
;
Berera and Calderón 2019
;
Berera et al. 2021
;
Kamali et al. 2021a
;
Arya et al. 2024
;
Das 2020
;
Santos et al. 2022
.

•

Not all models of warm inflation satisfy the DCautomatically: a model studied by
Bastero-Gil et al. 2019a
is in tension with the DCas well as with the dSCin the regime of weak dissipation. Their distributed mass model requires the inflaton to scan over many mass states, ergo, it needs a large field traversal, which violates the DC.

•

Mohammadi et al. 2020d
found that it depends on the dissipation function itself: They study a model where a tachyon field decays into radiation. If the dissipation coefficient of their model is a power-law function of the tachyon field, neither the DC, nor the dSCis satisfied. However, if the dissipation coefficient also depends on the temperature of the photon bath, both conjectures are satisfied for observationally allowed parameter values in the strong dissipation regime.
153
153

153

The model tested by
Mohammadi et al. 2020d
has the following conservation equations:

\displaystyle\dot{\rho}_{\phi}+3H\left(\rho_{\phi}+p_{\phi}\right)

\displaystyle=-\Xi\left(\rho_{\phi}+p_{\phi}\right)

\displaystyle\dot{\rho}_{\textnormal{r}}+3H\left(\rho_{\textnormal{r}}+p_{\textnormal{r}}\right)

\displaystyle=\Xi\left(\rho_{\textnormal{r}}+p_{\textnormal{r}}\right)

\displaystyle\Xi\left(\rho_{\phi}+p_{\phi}\right)

\displaystyle=3\Xi H^{2}\dot{\phi}^{2}

\displaystyle\Upsilon

\displaystyle=\Xi/3H,

where the second-last equation stems from the Friedmann equation.

Inflation including higher spin states

faces additional constraints from the DC, if combined with the Higuchi bound
Higuchi 1987
, as shown by
Scalisi 2020
:
The Higuchi bound ensures unitarity of the EFTand demands for a state with spin
s
that

m^{2}>s\left(s-1\right)H^{2}.

(172)

Combined with the DC, this limits the allowed field range:

\Delta\phi<\frac{1}{\mathfrak{a}}\log\left[\frac{m_{0}}{H}\frac{1}{\sqrt{s\left(s-1\right)}}\right],

(173)

i.e. a tower where all spins are allowed is incompatible with inflation, as this bound is violated for high-spin states and exponentially suppressed masses. Therefore, a maximum spin value

\frac{m_{0}}{H}>\sqrt{s_{\text{max}}\left(s_{\text{max}}-1\right)}

(174)

must exist. The masses are always super-Hubble for spin states with
s>1
with a minimum value of
m_{0}>\sqrt{2}H
.
For the tower related to the string, the Regge trajectory gives a relation for the string scale
M_{\textnormal{s}}
:

m^{2}=sM_{\textnormal{s}}^{2}.

(175)

Combining this with the Higuchi bound and the DCgives a bound for the field range of

\Delta\phi<\frac{1}{\mathfrak{a}}\log\left[\frac{M_{\textnormal{s}}(0)}{H}\frac{1}{\sqrt{s-1}}\right],

(176)

M_{\textnormal{s}}(0)
being the highest value of the string scale at
\Delta\phi=0
.
In (quasi) dSbackground, the string scale comes with an additional cutoff, this time a cutoff for the applicability of the Higuchi bound, which helps to circumvent the just presented constraint:
the length of the string
l_{\textnormal{s}}\sim\sqrt{s}/M_{\textnormal{s}}
has to be smaller than the Hubble radius
1/H
, which gives an upper limit for the spin state for which the Higuchi bound is applicable:

s_{\text{max}}=\left(\frac{M_{\textnormal{s}}}{H}\right)^{2}.

(177)

We also have a bound for strings shorter than the Hubble radius; their energy scale has to be super-Planckian:

M_{\textnormal{s}}>\sqrt{HM_{\textnormal{P}}},

(178)

which limits the energy scale of inflation, which is expected to be below the string energy
Noumi et al. 2020
;
Lust and Palti 2019
. Combining all this gives a refined bound on the field excursion:

\Delta\phi<\frac{1}{\mathfrak{a}}\log\frac{M_{\textnormal{s}}(0)}{\sqrt{HM_{\textnormal{P}}}}.

(179)

Mutli-field inflation

can circumvent the issues presented by the Lyth bound and slow-rolling by only having the combined, effective field slow-rolling, but not the individual fields.
154
154

154

See
section

II.4.1
about slow-rolling and multi-field inflation.

Multi-field hyperinflation
155
155

155

In hyperinflation
Brown 2018
;
Mizuno and Mukohyama 2017
, the field space is a hyperbolic plane with constant curvature
\ll 1
and the inflaton never slow-rolls. Instead, it orbits the bottom of the potential, supported by a centrifugal force.

can be brought into agreement with the DC
or

and

Bjorkmo and Marsh 2019

Inflation in Modified Gravity

Various models of inflation satisfy the DCin a
f(R)
theory

\absolutevalue{\Delta\phi}=\absolutevalue{\Delta\log\abs{\partial_Rf}}/2=\absolutevalue{\Delta R\left(\partial_R^2f\right)/2\partial_Rf}\approx\order{1}
.

setting with either a GBterm incorporating higher-order curvature terms (
R^{2}-4R_{\mu\nu}R^{\mu\nu}+R_{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}R^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}
), or a parity-violating Chern–Simons term (
\varepsilon^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}R_{\mu\nu}^{\hskip 8.19447pt\alpha\beta}R_{\rho\sigma\alpha\beta}

A window of compatibility for holographic DEas inflaton is found for a

Spacetime

Examining RNBHsin dSspace with a dynamic cosmological constant,
Lüben et al. 2021
derive the result that different spacetimes are separated by an infinite distance if and only if they are separated by infinite proper time: All spacetimes with at least one mass parameter are a finite distance away from each other, and the Minkowski limit of infinite mass (or charge) is infinitely far away from every other spherically-symmetric and static spacetime.

158

158

For instance, the size of the smallest possible BHis fixed by applying the DCto the spacetime solutions
Angius et al. 2023a

II.5.2
General Remarks

A rough but directly applicable expression of the DCis

\frac{\Delta\phi}{M_{\textnormal{P}}}\lesssim\order{1},

(180)

i.e. the field excursion is small in Planck units over the cosmic history
Raveri et al. 2019
. In the following, we’d like to answer questions that might help to get a refined understanding of the DC.

What is
\mathfrak{a}
and which values can it take?

\mathfrak{a}
can be thought of as a
decay rate
, as it regulates how fast the mass decreases in field space
Freigang et al. 2023
or, in the context of CYdecompactifications, how much of the space decompactifies
Perlmutter et al. 2021
. In the literature, usually a constant
\mathfrak{a}
is studied. If we allow a varying
\mathfrak{a}
, e.g. allowing lower values, the mass scale decays more slowly, but we are no longer following geodesics and the constraining power of the DCis weakened
Freigang et al. 2023
.
A lower bound of

\mathfrak{a}\geq 1/\sqrt{d-2}

(181)

for the lightest
159
159

159

A heavier tower might be present as well and violate this bound, but not the lightest. The lightest tower is the one with the largest
\mathfrak{a}
, as this tower suffers the strongest exponential suppression.

tower of states is found to be valid in various string theory and M-theory compactifications
Etheredge et al. 2022
;
Etheredge et al. 2023
;
van de Heisteeg et al. 2023b
;
Bedroya 2022
;
Rudelius 2022
;
Castellano et al. 2023b
, derived from the EP
Agmon et al. 2023
;
Etheredge et al. 2024a
;
van de Heisteeg et al. 2023b
;
van de Heisteeg et al. 2023a
, and saturated in CFTs
160
160

160

For 2-dimensional CFTs,
Ooguri and Wang 2024
derive
1/\sqrt{c}<\mathfrak{a}<1
, with
c
the central charge.

Perlmutter et al. 2021
and for fundamental strings
Bedroya et al. 2023
.

van de Heisteeg 2022
derives
\mathfrak{a}\geq 1/\sqrt{6}
using asymptotic Hodge theory.
Calderón-Infante et al. 2023a
find, considering the CEBin dSspace,

\sqrt{\frac{d-1}{d-2}}\geq\mathfrak{a}\geq\frac{1}{\sqrt{\left(d-2\right)\left(d-1\right)}}.

(182)

The lower bound is also motivated by the TCC
Bedroya 2020
;
Bedroya and Vafa 2020
;
Andriot et al. 2020a
, and
the upper bound by an observation by
Casas and Ruiz 2025
: if a tower of states decays too fast, no feasible FLRWcosmology can be obtained, in particular, all the solutions they find violate the Higuchi bound for dSsolutions.
161
161

161

Casas and Ruiz 2025
find a link to the dSCand state that the actual upper bound for the tower decay rate is
\mathfrak{a}\leq\min\{\mathfrak{s}_{1}/2,\sqrt{\left(d-1\right)/\left(d-2\right)}\}
.

Due to the dimension-dependence, the bounds weaken in spaces with many large dimensions.
Bonnefoy et al. 2021
propose to introduce a dependence on the dimensionality of the higher-dimensional space:
\mathfrak{a}=\sqrt{\frac{D-2}{\left(D-d\right)\left(d-2\right)}}
, which agrees with the upper bound in
eq.

182
for
D=d+1
, as well as with the bound derived from the EP:
The EPstates that the lightest towers that emerge in the infinite distance limit are either KKtowers that correspond to a decompactification
or towers of string oscillators that corresponds to a fundamental string becoming tensionless. The two cases limit the range of
\mathfrak{a}
:

\sqrt{\frac{D-2}{\left(D-d\right)\left(d-2\right)}}\geq\mathfrak{a}\geq\frac{1}{\sqrt{d-2}},

(183)

where the lower bound corresponds to the emergent string limit and the upper bound to a decompactification, with the highest value for
D=d+1

van de Heisteeg et al. 2023a
.

Bounds on
\mathfrak{a}
are generally derived for massless scalar fields. It hasn’t been shown yet if such bounds are exactly saturated for massive fields as well. For strings and membranes charged under a single gauge field,
Lanza et al. 2021a
motivate the value
\mathfrak{a}=\left.\frac{1}{2}\frac{q}{\mathcal{T}}\right|_{\text{extremal}}
, with
q
the charge and
\mathcal{T}
the tension of the object, whereas the value to be taken is the value from the extremal object that saturates the WGC(see
section

II.19
).
162
162

162

If there are multiple gauge charges, the bound weakens to
\mathfrak{a}\geq 1/\sqrt{2n_{h}}
, with
n_{h}
the no-scale/homogeneity factor from the Kähler potential.

Etheredge et al. 2024b
propose the
brane distance conjecture

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}

\displaystyle\sim e^{-\mathfrak{a}\Delta\phi}

(184)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{a}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{\sqrt{d-p_{\text{max}}-1}}

(185)

with
p_{\text{max}}\in\{1,\,2,\,\dots,\,d-2\}
; this bound depends solely on the minimum codimension
d-p_{\text{max}}
of the brane that satisfies the conjecture.

Where does the exponential suppression come from?

An example can be given in the context of 5D Einstein gravity, with the metric

G_{MN}\mathrm{d}X^{M}\mathrm{d}X^{N}=g_{\mu\nu}(x)\mathrm{d}x^{\mu}\mathrm{d}x^{\nu}+r(x)^{2}\mathrm{d}y^{2}

(186)

and an effective action for the modulus field
r(x)

S=\int\!\sqrt{g}\frac{1}{\left(\lambda r\right)^{2}}\partial_{\mu}r\partial^{\mu}r\,\mathrm{d}^{4}x,

(187)

with
\lambda
a numerical constant
Blumenhagen 2018
. For the canonically normalised field
\rho(x)
we find

\rho=\frac{1}{\lambda}\log r

(188)

and for
\rho>1/\lambda
there are infinitely
163
163

163

Bedroya et al. 2023
argue that the number of string limits is actually countable.
See
section

II.8
for other finiteness arguments.

many KKmodes for which

m_{\text{KK}}\sim\frac{n}{r}\sim ne^{-\lambda\rho}

(189)

holds
Bonnefoy et al. 2020a
, i.e. their masses are exponentially suppressed and the 4-dimensional EFTwith the radial modulus breaks down
Blumenhagen 2018
.

In the case of Kähler manifolds, the predicted exponential suppression becomes obvious when treating the case of a complex field with

d(\phi,\phi_{0})=\int_{\gamma}\!\sqrt{g_{ij}\frac{\partial\phi^{i}}{\partial s}\frac{\partial\bar{\phi}^{j}}{\partial s}}\,\mathrm{d}s,

(190)

with definitions analogue to the real case from
eq.

194

Blumenhagen et al. 2018
.
The asymptotic leading behaviour of the Kähler potential

K\sim-\sum_{i=1}^{3}\left(d_{i}-d_{i-1}\log\left(\Im{\phi^i}\right)\right)

(191)

leads to the Kähler metric

g_{ij}\sim\text{diag}\left(\frac{d_{i}-d_{i-1}}{\left(\Im{\phi^i}\right)^{2}}\right),

(192)

which in turn leads, for fixed
\Re{\phi^i}
, to

{AmSalign}

d(

,
ϕ

∫_

​[∑_i=1(d_i-d_i-1)(

i.e. for a sufficiently simple path a logarithmic change in the distance is achieved
Grimm et al. 2019

eq.

, but the geodesic equation

\frac{\mathrm{d}^{2}x^{\rho}}{\mathrm{d}s^{2}}+\Gamma^{\rho}_{\mu\nu}\frac{\mathrm{d}x^{\mu}}{\mathrm{d}s}\frac{\mathrm{d}x^{\nu}}{\mathrm{d}s}=0

(193)

can still be solved numerically
Erkinger and Knapp 2019
.
For alternative derivations of the logarithmic behaviour of the field distance, see the work by
Grimm et al. 2018
;
Lanza et al. 2021b
.

Heidenreich et al. 2018a
show that the same exponential suppression follows from two assumptions: (i) there is a tower of states that becomes light near a particular point in field space, (ii) the scalar field and gravity have a common strong coupling energy scale.
164
164

164

The common strong coupling scale also leads to the finding that trans-Planckian field ranges yield mass changes of the order of the cutoff scale of the EFT, indicating that trans-Planckian field ranges cannot be accommodated within an EFT
Heidenreich et al. 2018a
.

What does it mean for states to become massless?

A state becoming massless means that the ratio of the mass scale of this state to the Planck mass
M_{\textnormal{P}}
becomes zero
Font et al. 2019
. It means that those states have been integrated out
165
165

165

To
integrate out
a state means that a Lagrangian has been re-expressed such that massive states are derivatives that don’t contribute to the integral, i.e. to the action. Assume that you have a Lagrangian with two bosonic fields,
L\sim\frac{1}{2}\left(\partial\phi\right)^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\left(\partial\varphi\right)^{2}+m^{2}(\phi)\varphi^{2}
. Let us write
\phi=\expectationvalue{\phi}+\delta\phi
. For the action, we find now that
S=\int\!L\sim\int\!L_{0}+\delta\phi^{2}
, i.e. as long as our EFTis below energy ranges of
m_{0}
, we can ignore the
\order{\delta\phi^2}
terms. A bosonic — or rather scalar — mass state can be expanded as
\frac{1}{2}m^{2}(\phi)\varphi^{2}=\frac{1}{2}m^{2}(\expectationvalue{\phi})\varphi^{2}+\left(m\partial_{\phi}m\right)|_{\expectationvalue{\phi}}\delta\phi\varphi^{2}+\dots
, where the second term gives rise to the 1-loop wave function renormalisation
Seo 2023b
. For fermions, we find
m(\phi)\bar{\Psi}\Psi=m(\expectationvalue{\phi})\bar{\Psi}\Psi+\partial_{\phi}m|_{\expectationvalue{\phi}}\delta\phi\bar{\Psi}\Psi

Seo 2023b
. This idea is often generalised in the expansion of the Lagrangian into
L=L_{\text{EFT}}+\sum_{n}\frac{\mathcal{O}_{n+4}}{\Lambda_{\textnormal{S}}^{n-4}}
, where
\mathcal{O}
are some higher-order operators and
\Lambda_{\textnormal{S}}
is the high-energy cutoff of the EFT — the questions arise what that cutoff shall be, what can be part of
L_{\text{EFT}}
, and what can go into
\mathcal{O}

De Biasio 2023
;
Valenzuela 2023
.

illegally

Baume et al. 2020
. They should be part of the EFT, but have been neglected, assuming they were only subleading, higher-order terms that don’t significantly contribute. The appearance of massless states can be thought of as inducing a running gauge coupling, which vanishes in the infinite limit, where the states are massless
Lee et al. 2018
;
Grimm et al. 2018
;
Klaewer and Palti 2017
;
Harlow 2016
;
Heidenreich et al. 2018a
;
Heidenreich et al. 2018b
.

In the context of CFTand holography,
Conlon and Revello 2020
make the observation that heavy states appear if one goes in the opposite direction with
\absolutevalue{\Delta\phi}\gg M_{\textnormal{P}}
and
\Delta\phi<0
. Duality would imply that in this case the winding mode should become light — but in the case they study, winding modes only become light in the infinite limit, not in the limit of very large field displacements. This is a conundrum. However, they find hints that this might point to a more foundational notion of the DC: a negativity condition for the sign of anomalous dimensions for mixed double trace operators, which works in either direction.

How is distance defined?

How far apart two theories are can be examined using different measures: based on the information metric
Stout 2021
;
Stout 2022
, the number of microstates of a BHwithin one EFT
Li 2021
, the low-velocity distance that measures the physical space-time distance between moving BPSobjects, and the de Witt distance — with or without a volume factor — that measures the distance between EFTsin moduli space
Li 2022
.
166
166

166

For the DC, typically the de Witt distance including a volume factor is used — neglecting the volume factor leads to light towers of states already at finite distance
Li 2022
—, with
\mathfrak{l}
an
\order{1}
constant,
\lambda
parametrising the one-dimensional path, and
g
the Riemann metric on the manifold
\mathcal{M}

Demulder et al. 2024
:

d_{\textnormal{dW}}=\mathfrak{l}\int_{\lambda_{\textnormal{i}}}^{\lambda_{\textnormal{f}}}\sqrt{\frac{1}{\textnormal{Vol}\left(\mathcal{M}\right)}\int_{\mathcal{M}}\sqrt{g}g^{MN}g^{OP}\frac{\partial g_{MO}}{\partial\lambda}\frac{\partial g_{NP}}{\partial\lambda}}\mathrm{d}\lambda.

It is even possible that two theories are an infinite distance apart according to one measure, but a finite distance according to another one
Li 2022
. Furthermore, different measures do not necessarily measure the distance in spaces of equal dimension: e.g. the low-velocity distance is measured in a higher-dimensional moduli space. The notion of
distance
is typically based on a measure of
energy
;
167
167

167

Defining a (quasi-)distance can be done using the domain wall tension, which is even valid if there is a potential
Mohseni et al. 2024
.

however,
entropy
can be used if not all axioms that define a distance measure are required and a
quasi-distance
is sufficient
Gusin et al. 2024
.

A general definition of the distance is given by the integral over
\gamma
, the shortest geodesic connecting
\phi
and
\phi_{0}
, with
\mathrm{d}s
the line element along that geodesic:

d(\phi,\phi_{0})\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\int_{\gamma}\!\sqrt{g_{ij}\frac{\partial\phi^{i}}{\partial s}\frac{\partial\phi^{j}}{\partial s}}\,\mathrm{d}s,

(194)

for real scalar fields
\phi^{i}
, which are coordinates on
\mathcal{M}
and whose kinetic terms define a metric
g_{ij}
on
\mathcal{M}

Palti 2019
;
Grimm et al. 2018
;
De Biasio 2023
.

As shown in
fig.

3
, the presence of a potential can change the distance between two points, as the shortest available path between the two points is modified by the potential. This can also be understood as a modification of the distance measure, e.g.

d(\phi,\phi_{0})=\int_{\gamma}\!\sqrt{g_{ij}\frac{\partial\phi^{i}}{\partial s}\frac{\partial\phi^{j}}{\partial s}+\left(\frac{1}{\Lambda^{3}}\frac{\partial\phi^{i}}{\partial s}\frac{\partial V}{\partial\phi^{i}}\right)^{2}}\,\mathrm{d}s,

(195)

with
\Lambda
some overall scaling,
incorporates the potential into the definition of distance, with a larger gradient of the potential leading to a bigger deviation from the case without potential
Schimmrigk 2019
.
Another proposal to incorporate potentials is

d(\phi,\phi_{0})=\int_{\gamma}\!\sqrt{2E_{\text{kin}}+2V}\,\mathrm{d}s,

(196)

where
\gamma
is the on-shell trajectory; this does not necessarily correspond to a geodesic, and
\gamma
can only connect points that belong to an attractor trajectory
Debusschere et al. 2025
.
168
168

168

Attractors are independent of the initial conditions and the total energy
Debusschere et al. 2025
.

If the cosmological attractor is a geodesic, this distance measure closely corresponds to our ordinary understanding of distance
Debusschere et al. 2025
.

Basile and Montella 2023
treat the case of a discrete set of vacua. To define the distance between discrete vacua, they rely on domain walls to interpolate between the isolated vacua and to get a finite-energy solution:

\displaystyle\frac{\Delta(\phi,\phi_{0})}{M_{\textnormal{P}}(\phi_{0})^{\frac{d-2}{2}}}

\displaystyle=

(197)

\displaystyle\min\sum_{k}\frac{d(\phi+\sum_{l<k}\mathcal{E}_{l},\phi+\sum_{l\leq k}\mathcal{E}_{l})}{M_{\textnormal{P}}^{\frac{d-2}{2}}(\phi+\sum_{l\leq k}\mathcal{E}_{l})},

where they explicitly use the dependence of the Planck mass on the vacuum — i.e. the position in moduli space — and gradually adjust it along the path, and where
d()
is meant to be the distance between two points in moduli space as defined in
eq.

194
. Since this is a discretised space, we cannot just take a path along the geodesic, which would automatically minimise the path, but we have to explicitly minimise the path under the constraint that
\sum_{j}\mathcal{E}_{j}=\phi_{0}-\phi
, and sum over all steps along a domain wall that leads from
\phi
to
\phi_{0}
, by modifying the charges by a basis vector of the lattice
\mathcal{E}

Basile and Montella 2023
.

What does it mean to investigate the infinite distance limit of moduli space?

Moduli are massless scalar fields. Each field value corresponds to a vacuum expectation value in string theory, and therefore to the value of a continuous
169
169

169

For example, the 1-sphere
\mathcal{S}^{1}
, where the maximum distance is
2\pi
as
\phi\sim\phi+2\pi
, violates the DC. Periodic scalars must be part of a larger moduli space, to be allowed in QG
Palti 2019
. Moduli spaces are non-compact
Brennan et al. 2018
;
Hamada et al. 2022
if non-trivial.

parameter in an EFT
Castellano et al. 2023b
;
Ooguri and Vafa 2007
. The infinite distance limit in field space corresponds to the perturbative regime of the EFT
Castellano et al. 2023b
, where QGeffects might become observable but are unaccounted for in the theory
Calderón-Infante et al. 2024
;
Corvilain et al. 2019
, i.e. the EFTbreaks down.
This implies that not every thinkable field is also suited to be part of a consistent theory of QG
Ooguri and Vafa 2007
, and that the distance a field can travel is limited, i.e. the energy scale that the EFTcan accommodate is finite
Corvilain et al. 2019
;
Lanza et al. 2021b
.

What happens in the infinite field limit?

The DCstates that whenever a field develops a large expectation value, decoupling in effective QFTbreaks down at exponentially lower energy scales than expected
Palti 2019
.
To turn the logic around, you can also say that you cannot follow a geodesic in moduli space for a distance bigger than
\sim M_{\textnormal{P}}
, as after that, your EFTbreaks down
Blumenhagen et al. 2018
.
The basic light degrees of freedom are directed by towers of particles with masses
m(\phi_{0})

Ooguri et al. 2019
.
In the infinite field limit, the theory either decompactifies, which causes the appearance of a tower of light KKmodes or winding states that are unaccounted for in the EFT
Cicoli et al. 2023a
;
Baldes et al. 2019
;
Lee et al. 2022a
;
Ooguri and Vafa 2007
;
Heisenberg et al. 2021
,
or it reduces to a weakly coupled, asymptotically tensionless string theory
Lee et al. 2022a
;
Castellano et al. 2023b
;
Xu 2020
;
Baume et al. 2020
;
Lee 2021
;
Corvilain et al. 2019
.
170
170

170

Some infinite distance limits yield a tower of instantons, but the instantons modify the action, such that the limit turns into a finite distance point in moduli space
Agmon et al. 2023
;
Marchesano and Wiesner 2019
.
In
section

II.6
we elaborate on why it is either a KKtower or a fundamental string.

The tower becoming light acts as a censorship mechanism:
171
171

171

Remmen 2021
studies singular isothermal sphere solutions —
‘a special class of singular solutions for a self-gravitating perfect fluid in general relativity’
with an EoS
w=p/\rho
and a
1/r^{2}
density profile —, and shows that the tower of states predicted by the DCshields the naked singularity that would otherwise appear and violate cosmic censorship.

it appears at the boundary of field space, where we would encounter an exact global symmetry
Castellano et al. 2023c
;
Lee et al. 2018
;
Hamada et al. 2022
;
Rudelius 2024a
— something we cannot have, as is further discussed in
section

II.10
.
172
172

172

See also the work by
Cordova et al. 2022a
who motivate the DCfrom the no global symmetries conjecture.

The DCquantifies
how
the tower of states becomes light, which means it quantifies how to approximate a global symmetry
Castellano et al. 2023c
.

Is there more than one infinite distance limit?

Spaces with a richer topology can have different points that are infinitely far away from each other and
different paths that lead to different towers of light states
Grimm et al. 2019
— there are several open questions regarding this aspect: how many towers are there and does each path come with its own individual tower of states, or can paths be deformed or grouped together, such that they lead to the same tower of states, i.e. is there also an infinite number of different towers or are there families of different towers
Grimm et al. 2022a
?
In different regions of moduli space, different towers may be the first to emerge new states
Lanza 2024
.
Furthermore, in the absence of a clear mass hierarchy, i.e. if there are competing towers, it might no longer be possible to safely model the path of a single field through moduli space as a geodesic; physical paths that solve the EoMare required
Landete and Shiu 2018
.
When there are multiple charges, a
convex hull conjecture
,
173
173

173

In analogy to the convex hull conjecture presented in
section

II.19
.
might be the guiding principle instead
Calderón-Infante et al. 2021
.
To only consider the lightest tower would be insufficient, as there could be an arbitrary number of towers below the EFTcutoff above the lightest tower
Castellano et al. 2022
.
However, the Tameness Conjecture (
section

II.16
) implies that it suffices to consider a finite number of different types of states to always be able to define a cutoff for the EFT
Lanza 2024
.

Does the DCalso apply to scalar fields with a potential?

A potential acts as a constraint on the available field space in
\mathcal{M}
, as we are now faced with an energy scale dependence, which means that only the subspace
\widehat{\mathcal{M}}\subset\mathcal{M}
is accessible
Grimm et al. 2022a
.
There are three equivalent ways to understand this:
first, the field space gets reduced because we have to integrate out massive scalars at a now lower energy scale;
second, the potential acts as a constraint on the available paths, which means that the shortest available path
d_{\widehat{\mathcal{M}}}(\phi,\phi_{0})
might now be longer than the geodesic through the full moduli space
d_{\mathcal{M}}(\phi,\phi_{0})
, i.e.
d_{\mathcal{M}}(\phi,\phi_{0})\leq d_{\widehat{\mathcal{M}}}(\phi,\phi_{0})
(e.g. in
Hebecker et al. 2017a
);
174
174

174

This can be understood as the effect of the potential as an external force that drives the motion to non-geodesic paths
Demulder et al. 2024
;
Calderón-Infante et al. 2021
.

and third, graphically, as in
fig.

3

Grimm et al. 2022a
.
A longer path length means a stronger exponential suppression of the mass scale, i.e. a different tower, which decays faster, might be required in this case, and the DCmight no longer hold. This can be viewed as a constraint on the scalar potential: only potentials are allowed that provide paths that are compatible with the DC
Grimm et al. 2022a
.
Calderón-Infante et al. 2021
show that paths that asymptotically approach a geodesic in the potential-less (parent) moduli space are in agreement with the DC.

Figure 3:
On the left we see the unrestricted moduli space
\mathcal{M}
with the geodesic going from
\phi
to
\phi_{0}
. On the right, we see the same space, but this time with a potential, which selects a subspace
\widehat{\mathcal{M}}
. The previous geodesic is not contained in this subspace, i.e. the path connecting
\phi
and
\phi_{0}
is longer. Figure taken from
Grimm et al. 2022a
.

It is important to note that the DCis formulated regarding the distance in
moduli spaces
, i.e. it is a notion about
theories
. If it’s also directly applicable to the field space of a physical field is less clear.
In the literature, the DCis often assumed to hold in the field space as well, even if the fields have a potential.
Some argue that the mass scale suppression may deviate from its exponential form in this case
Freigang et al. 2023
;
Grimm et al. 2022a
;
Hebecker et al. 2017a
.
Others argue that also massive scalar fields with a potential show an exponential suppression in the asymptotic limit
Rudelius 2022
;
Hebecker and Wrase 2019
;
Ooguri and Vafa 2007
;
Dine and Seiberg 1985
;
Klaewer and Palti 2017
;
Obied et al. 2018
, however, the value of
\mathfrak{a}
is less constraint.
A possible counterexample to the applicability of the DCin the bulk of the field space is discussed by
Hebecker et al. 2019b
: studying axions, they find towers of states with approximately constant masses — and not, as required by the DC, exponentially suppressed masses — under the assumption that the field distance is parametrically large, but not infinite. Their idea of axion (mis)alignment is also discussed by
Palti 2019
. Contrarily,
Reig 2021
states that maximally misaligned quintessence always satisfies the DC, as
Nf^{2}\ll M_{\textnormal{P}}^{2}
, with
N
the number of axions and
f
the decay constant, holds always.
The jury on the applicability of the DCto scalar fields with potentials is still out. What can be said is that if we deviate from geodesic paths or allow the scalar field to have a potential, the constraining power of the DCis weakened.

II.6
Emergence Proposal

The strong form of the EPstates the following
Blumenhagen et al. 2024a
;
Castellano et al. 2023a
;
Gendler and Valenzuela 2021
;
Castellano et al. 2023d
:
In QG, light particles in the perturbative regime have no kinetic terms in the UV. In the IR, kinetic terms emerge due to loop corrections involving the sum over a tower of massless states, i.e. kinetic terms emerge from integrating out massive states up to the species scale (
section

II.14
,
eq.

276
).

A weaker form states that for each singularity at an infinite distance in moduli space of an EFT, there is an associated infinite tower of states that becomes light and that induces quantum corrections to the metrics, which match the tree-level
179
179

179

Feynman diagrams at lower order look like a tree, whereas higher-order diagrams include loops (and additional factors of
\hbar
).

singular behaviour
Blumenhagen et al. 2024a
;
Castellano et al. 2023a
.

A more hands-on definition of the EPis the prediction that every infinite-distance limit in moduli space corresponds either to a decompactification, and therefore to a tower of KKmodes; or to a fundamental string becoming tensionless, and therefore to a tower of string oscillator modes
Rudelius 2024b
;
Kaufmann et al. 2024
.

II.6.1
Implications for Cosmology

Cosmological Constant

Castellano et al. 2023d
use the EPto motivate the idea that the cosmological constant
\Lambda_{\text{cc}}
is a fundamental scale, and that all other energy scales can be expressed as

m\sim\Lambda_{\text{cc}}^{1/n}M_{\textnormal{P}}^{1-1/n},

(198)

which corresponds to the AdSDC(
eq.

1
), is compatible with the DC, the no non-supersymmetric theories conjecture (
section

II.12
), and the (magnetic) WGC(
eq.

461
), and leads to the relations between the scales presented in

Neutrinos

Equation

indicates that the lightest neutrino
\nu_{1}
is a Dirac neutrino
Castellano et al. 2023d
.
180
180

180

While a Majorana neutrino gets its mass through the seesaw mechanism
Gell-Mann et al. 2013
;
Yanagida 1980
;
Dienes et al. 1999
, the towers of states predicted by the EPlead to suppression of Yukawa couplings that yield light Dirac neutrinos, or put differently:
‘the right-handed components receive large wave-function renormalisation effects through their coupling to such an infinite tower of states’

Castellano et al. 2023d
.

The small mass is explained by an anomalous extra dimension that opens up due to a tower of SMsinglet states of mass
m_{\textnormal{t}}\simeq Y_{\nu_{3}}M_{\textnormal{P}}\simeq$7\text{\times}{10}^{5}\text{\,}\mathrm{GeV}$
that relates the cosmological constant
\Lambda_{\text{cc}}
to the EWscale
m_{\text{EW}}
as
m_{\textnormal{t}}m_{\text{EW}}\lesssim 10^{2}\Lambda_{\text{cc}}M_{\textnormal{P}}

Castellano et al. 2023d
.
181
181

181

In early work,
Arkani-Hamed et al. 2005
thought that the discovery of such a relation between the three different energy scales, relating the cosmological constant, EWscale, and the Planck scale, would be based on an anthropic principle, not on underlying dynamics.

Since the EWscale and the cosmological constant are constrained by observations, and the Planck scale is a definition, the neutrino mass has to be light to satisfy the (swampland) constraint(s).
Furthermore, to be consistent with observations, the tower mass scale
m_{\textnormal{t}}\lesssim${10}^{6}\text{\,}\mathrm{GeV}$
, i.e. at
{10}^{6}\text{\,}\mathrm{GeV}
a 5
th
dimension opens up that is only felt by the right-handed neutrinos, since no other particle/field couples to that tower
Castellano et al. 2023d
.
182
182

182

It is important to note that in this extra dimension scenario, the fundamental scale of gravity is still many orders of magnitude above the EWscale
Castellano et al. 2023d
, unlike e.g. in refs.
Arkani-Hamed et al. 1998
;
Dvali and Redi 2009
.
A similar scenario is also invoked by
Sabir et al. 2025
: neutrino mixing is explained by a higher-dimensional 4-point operator.

Scalar field

potentials emerge from integrating out towers of states
Hamada et al. 2022
. A typical scale for 1-loop Casimir potentials that arise from integrating out particle scales is
m^{d}

Hamada et al. 2022
;
Gonzalo et al. 2021
;
Rudelius 2021a
, which is compatible with the Higuchi bound
183
183

183

See also our comments on inflation in
section

II.5.1
.

that demands that the exponent is larger than 2.
For scalar potentials with a Lagrangian of the form

L=\frac{1}{2}\dot{\phi}-V(\phi),

(199)

and
\lim_{\phi\rightarrow\infty}V(\phi)=0
,
184
184

184

Note that for asymptotically divergent potentials, infinite distance limits of the EFTare obstructed by the potential and no issue arises
Hamada et al. 2022
.
Furthermore, the potential cannot asymptote a finite positive value, as this is restricted by the Higuchi bound
Higuchi 1987
;
Hamada et al. 2022
.

we find that the cosmological constant
\Lambda_{\text{cc}}
vanishes in the infinite distance limit where the tower of states becomes light:

\Lambda_{\text{cc}}=H^{2}\sim m_{\textnormal{t}}^{\mathfrak{p}},

(200)

with
\mathfrak{p}>2
as demanded by the Higuchi bound
Hamada et al. 2022
.

SMof Particle Physics

The kinetic terms of all SMparticles arise from loop corrections and the coupling of KKor string towers to the SMparticles
Castellano et al. 2023d
. The details of these kinetic terms depend on the interactions between the particles and the specific towers or combinations of towers the particle in question couples to
Castellano et al. 2023d
.

Kawamura 2023
shows that the quark flavor structure can be obtained by applying the EP: the structure emerges from radiative corrections to towers of massive states.

II.6.2
General Remarks

Emergence can be understood as a form of IRduality: light fields describe the same physics as towers of states, but in different variables
Hattab and Palti 2023
;
Palti 2019
;
Harlow 2016
;
Grimm et al. 2018
;
Hattab and Palti 2024a
.
185
185

185

For example,
Gendler and Valenzuela 2021
write that
‘[t]he emergence proposal […] suggests that the small gauge coupling and the infinite field distance are generated from quantum corrections of integrating out the towers of states becoming light. Hence, the relations between (gauge and scalar) charges and masses that the Swampland conjectures predict would simply be a consequence of the renormalization group flow equations.’

While the towers of states predicted by the EPare infinite (in accordance with the DC), the states integrated out in an EFTare below the species scale cutoff (
section

II.14
)
Blumenhagen et al. 2024a
.

Castellano et al. 2023a
present a deeper connection between emergence and other swampland conjectures:
If the corresponding (dual) flux in the vacuum is turned on, kinetic terms that emerge for
\left(d-1\right)
-forms create scalar potentials that are in agreement with the AdSDC, the dSC, the DC, as well as with the magnetic WGC(
eq.

461
).
This once more shows the mutual compatibility between the different swampland conjectures, even though they are independently motivated.

There is evidence that the precise formulation of the EPin QGis as yet unclear
Blumenhagen et al. 2024a
;
Blumenhagen et al. 2023d
: QFTcomputations lead to different results than BHphysics, ranging from different numerical factors and higher-order terms to additional logarithmic enhancement factors for emergent string limits.
Despite the open questions regarding a quantitative understanding of the EP, we’d like to address some questions to gain a better qualitative understanding of the EPin the following.

What is actually emerging?

Dynamics. Kinetic terms emerge in the IRby integrating out towers of states from the UVspecies scale
\Lambda_{\textnormal{S}}

Blumenhagen et al. 2024b
;
Palti 2019
.
A gauge coupling
g
emerges at 1-loop as

\frac{1}{g^{2}}\sim\sum_{n=1}^{N_{\textnormal{S}}}q_{n}^{2}\log\left(\frac{m_{n}}{\Lambda_{\textnormal{S}}}\right)

(201)

from integrating out a tower of charged states with
m_{n}=n\Delta m
and
q_{n}=nq
, such that
\Lambda_{\textnormal{S}}=N_{\textnormal{S}}\Delta m

Blumenhagen et al. 2024a
.

Blumenhagen et al. 2024b
present another example, referring to the work of
Heidenreich et al. 2018a
;
Heidenreich et al. 2018b
;
Grimm et al. 2018
;
Castellano et al. 2023a
:
Given an effective
d
-dimensional action of

\displaystyle S=

\displaystyle M_{\textnormal{P}}^{d-2}\int\!\frac{1}{2}G_{\phi\phi}\partial_{\mu}\phi\partial^{\mu}\phi

\displaystyle\qquad+\sum_{n}\frac{1}{2}\partial_{\mu}h\partial^{\mu}h+\frac{1}{2}m_{n}^{2}(\phi)h_{n}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}^{d}x

(202)

with

G_{\phi\phi}
the field space metric,

\phi
a light modulus,
and
h_{n}
a tower of massive KKstates with mass
m_{n}=n\Delta m(\phi)
that induces a 1-loop correction to the kinetic term for
\phi
through the 3-point couplings
y=\left[m_{n}\partial m_{n}\right]h_{n}^{2}\phi
,
the 1-loop corrections

G_{\phi\phi}^{\text{1-loop}}\simeq\frac{\Lambda_{\textnormal{S}}^{d-1}}{M_{\textnormal{P}}^{d-2}}\frac{\left(\partial_{\phi}\Delta m\right)^{2}}{\left(\Delta m\right)^{3}}+\dots,

(203)

are obtained by
integrating out these modes up the species scale
\Lambda_{\textnormal{S}}
.
For a KKtower, we find
G_{rr}^{\text{1-loop}}\simeq 1/r^{2}
, which shows the same functional dependence on the modulus
r
as the tree-level metric from dimensional reduction of the Einstein–Hilbert action
Blumenhagen et al. 2024b
.
In the UVregime, fields are not dynamical, and the 1-loop renormalisation group contributions to the moduli field metric, which arise from integrating out a tower of states that are lighter than the natural cutoff of the effective theory, are proportional to the tree-level metric and dominate over the classical contributions to the kinetic terms, i.e. the kinetic terms vanish above
\Lambda_{\textnormal{S}}

Blumenhagen et al. 2020b
;
Palti 2019
.

At the core of the EPis the statement that the lightest tower at an infinite distance in moduli space is either a KKtower, stemming from a decompactification, or a tower of string oscillator modes, stemming from a fundamental string becoming tensionless
Bedroya and Hamada 2023
;
Lee et al. 2019a
;
Lee et al. 2022a
;
Lee 2021
;
Lee et al. 2022b
;
Lee et al. 2018
;
Blumenhagen et al. 2024a
;
Kaufmann et al. 2024
.
The implications for an EFTare profoundly different in the two scenarios: in the case of the KKtower, the QGcutoff is at an energy scale
g^{1/3}M_{\textnormal{P}}
, whereas in the case of the fundamental string, the cutoff is already at
gM_{\textnormal{P}}

Draper et al. 2022
.
In the case of decompactification, multiple string-like objects can emerge, which are emergent (wrapped) branes that are becoming asymptotically tensionless
Blumenhagen et al. 2024a
.
In the case of the fundamental string, there is exactly one string-like object: the fundamental string
Blumenhagen et al. 2024a
.
A fundamental string has a graviton as a string state, and the scattering amplitude of the string state is given by string perturbation theory
Bedroya and Hamada 2023
.
Since there is always a unique critical string that becomes tensionless at the fastest rate, there is at most one unique massless graviton as part of the spectrum of the light states, never multiple massless gravitons
Castellano 2024
.

To give one concrete example of the tensionless fundamental string and the decompactification limit, we refer to
Bedroya et al. 2024a
: In 10d type IIA supergravity, new QGeffects appear when the first-order perturbative expansion breaks down — the 1-loop four-graviton scattering amplitude is at the tree-level. This happens at the species scale (
section

II.14
)

{AmSequation}

i.e. the

With
-\phi

\Lambda_{\textnormal{S}}\sim M_{\textnormal{P};D}\sim\exp\left(-\phi\sqrt{\left(D-d\right)/\left[\left(D-2\right)\left(d-2\right)\right]}\right)

Bedroya et al. 2024a
.

,
and the

.
As an aside, we notice that the species scale is always sub-Planckian.

Where should the kinetic terms come from in the IR?

Assume that there is an infinite-distance limit with a tower of states becoming light.
Furthermore, assume that there is a fermion that couples to this tower.
The wave function
\Psi
of the fermion is enhanced by factors of
\Psi\sim\left(\Lambda_{\text{QG}}/m_{\text{t}}\right)^{\mathfrak{l}}
with
\Lambda_{\text{QG}}
the UVcutoff scale,
m_{\text{t}}
the mass scale of the tower, and
\mathfrak{l}
of
\order{1}
,
and the canonical kinetic terms respectively the Yukawa couplings
Y^{ijk}
— which we assume to be of
\order{1}
in the UV — get suppressed
Y^{ijk}\rightarrow\Psi_{i}\Psi_{j}\Psi_{k}Y^{ijk}
and generate hierarchies of fermion masses

Castellano et al. 2023d
;
Castellano et al. 2023a
.
188
188

188

The limit
Y\rightarrow 0
corresponds to an infinite distance limit with vanishing coupling and a tower of
gonions

Aldazabal et al. 2001
— gonions are charged chiral states in massive vector-like representations of the D6-brane gauge group, where every state has the same bifundamental charge
Casas et al. 2024c
.

Could there be other towers than KKmodes and string oscillators?

In a bottom up approach based on considerations regarding the smallest possible BHradius,
Bedroya et al. 2024b
find that in a weak-coupling limit of Einstein gravity in asymptotically flat space, the lightest tower is either a KKtower, or a tower with exponential density of states,
\rho(E)\sim e^{E/\Lambda_{\textnormal{S}}}
, with
E
the energy — a behaviour known from the fundamental string, but not exclusive to the fundamental string. This acts as evidence in favour of the EP, but not as conclusive proof, as also other towers of states could show the exponential density.
Other towers discussed in string theory involve
Etheredge et al. 2024b
:
KKmodes,
KKmonopoles,
more general particle towers,
189
189

189

Particle towers can be generated by oscillation modes of unwrapped branes
Etheredge et al. 2024b
.

winding modes of branes that fully wrap a torus,
oscillation modes of branes that partially wrap a torus,
wrapped branes carrying compact momentum,
or asymptotically tensionless membranes
Castellano 2024
.
There is some evidence that the lightest object must not correspond to a higher-dimensional
p
-brane with
p\geq 2
, as this would spoil the consistency of the conjecture under dimensional reduction
Castellano 2024
;
Álvarez García et al. 2022b
, and

Álvarez García et al. 2022b
discuss censorship against emergent membrane limits.

What is the difference between the strong and the weak form of the EP?

The definitions seem to have only a slight difference, namely whether the metric evaluated at the QGcutoff vanishes entirely (strong proposal) or just becomes subleading (weak proposal)
Castellano 2024
. Conceptually, however, the implication of this slight difference is tremendous: the statement that graviton dynamics completely emerge from the UVfundamental degrees of freedom is accurate regarding the strong proposal but inaccurate regarding the weak proposal
Castellano 2024
.

The strong form of the proposal indicates that there could be a topological fundamental theory, where particles do not propagate, as there are no kinetic terms
Castellano et al. 2023a
;
Grimm et al. 2018
;
Palti 2019
;
Harlow 2016
;
Agrawal et al. 2020a
.

A Tale of
Three

Four

Five

Six
Seven Scales

The vast corpus of literature brings up different energy scales, which are not always obviously distinct. The main idea is always the same: to have a QGcutoff below or at the Planck mass
\Lambda^{d-2}N(\Lambda)\lesssim\order{1}M_{\textnormal{P}}^{d-2}

Caron-Huot and Li 2024
. But the details are often obfuscated.
There is a trinity of four scales with five meanings, six flavours, and seven names.
We’d like to present an attempt to disentangle the eight scales, and use them with clearly defined meanings:

Planck Scale
M_{\text{P};d}

M_{\textnormal{P};d}^{d-2}=\hbar^{d-3}c^{5-d}/8\pi G_{\text{N}}
, which is equal to
M_{\textnormal{P};D}^{D-2}\left(2\pi r_{D-d}\right)^{D-d}
in higher dimensional theories with
r_{D-d}
the (dimensionless) radius (in Planck units) of an extradimensional torus
Bedroya et al. 2024a
;
Castellano et al. 2023a
;
Castellano 2024
.

Non-Locality Scale
\Lambda_{\text{\sout{QFT}}}

Caron-Huot and Li 2024

At length scales shorter than
1/\Lambda_{\text{\sout{QFT}}}
, fields become strictly non-local. This scale coincides with the scale at which higher-spin states can no longer be neglected in graviton scattering processes.
In decompactification limits,
\Lambda_{\text{\sout{QFT}}}\sim M_{\textnormal{P};D}
, whereas in the weakly coupled fundamental string limit, the scale corresponds to the mass of spin
J\geq 4
string states that strongly couple to two gravitons,
M_{\textnormal{s}}
.

Emergence Scale
\Lambda_{\text{QG}}

In the emergent string limit, string gravity becomes
strong
.
The EPconjectures that this happens either at the string scale or at the KKscale, whichever scale is lower in a given setting.
The majority of texts would set the emergence scale equal to the species scale. However, we would advocate the notion that the species scale is often used as a more general umbrella term, as we will outline below.
Furthermore,
Hattab and Palti 2023
note that emergence, as an IRphenomenon, cannot come from integrating
out
states that are fundamental in the frame of the emergent field, e.g. integrating out KKmodes of a fundamental field cannot lead to the emergence of KKphotons, and integrating out string oscillator modes cannot lead to an emergent string limit. Therefore, string oscillator modes cannot be the lightest tower of states at the string scale by definition.
At the string scale, string theory is weakly coupled.
At the emergence scale, gravitational interactions become strong, which could be the ones described by string gravity.
As a technical definition for the emergence scale, we would propose to use a definition that is often used as one of the — non-identical — definitions of the species scale: The scale of emergence is the scale at which the 1-loop four-graviton scattering amplitude is at the tree-level
Dvali and Redi 2008
;
Basile et al. 2024b
;
Blumenhagen et al. 2024a
;
Bedroya et al. 2024a
;
Cribiori et al. 2023b
;
Cribiori and Lust 2023
;
Caron-Huot and Li 2024
.
As a motivation for this definition, we see the notion that the 1-loop propagator of the graviton gives a
‘worst-case scenario’

Aoufia et al. 2024
of the scale at which perturbation theory fails.

Species Scale
\Lambda_{\text{S}}

According to a rather loose definition, the species scale
\Lambda_{\textnormal{S}}
is the scale after which new gravitational dynamics appear. There is an overlap with the emergence scale, but that the two are not always equal
Hattab and Palti 2023
can for example be seen in figure 2 by
Bedroya et al. 2024a
.
Furthermore, as we will see in
section

II.14
, the cutoff scale of a lower-dimensional theory can be represented by the BHhorizon radius of the smallest BHthat can be described within this EFT. This cutoff does not necessarily correspond to the cutoff given by higher-curvature terms of the higher-dimensional theory
Bedroya et al. 2024b
.
We propose to consider the species scale as a wider umbrella term that corresponds, in a given setting, to the lowest of the following scales in that setting.

String Scale
M_{\text{s}}

A tower of string oscillator modes emerges at
M_{\textnormal{s}}
. At this scale, a fundamental string becomes tensionless
Etheredge et al. 2024a
respectively
‘an infinite tower of high-spin states on the graviton Regge trajectory become[s] light’

Caron-Huot and Li 2024
.

KKScale
M_{\text{P};D}

The mass scale of a tower of KKstates. At this scale,
\mathfrak{p}
dimensions decompactify
Etheredge et al. 2024a
, and we find
N(\Lambda)\sim\Lambda^{\mathfrak{p}}\mathcal{V}_{\mathfrak{p}}

Caron-Huot and Li 2024
, i.e. in the infinite volume limit (
\mathcal{V}\rightarrow\infty
) we get an infinite number of states unless the validity of the theory vanishes (
\Lambda\rightarrow 0
). The number of light states corresponds to the number of KKmodes, and the cutoff scale corresponds parametrically with the higher-dimensional Planck mass
\Lambda^{d+\mathfrak{p}-2}\sim M_{\textnormal{P}}^{d-2}/\mathcal{V}_{\mathfrak{p}}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=M_{\textnormal{P};d+\mathfrak{p}}^{d+\mathfrak{p}-2}

Caron-Huot and Li 2024
;
Etheredge et al. 2024a
.

BHScale
\Lambda_{\text{BH}}

Bedroya et al. 2024a
propose a scale, which they coin the
BHscale
, at which BHsencounter a Gregory–Laflamme instability
Gregory and Laflamme 1993
;
Reall 2001
;
Gregory and Laflamme 1994
and undergo a phase transition into a state of lower free energy that is not visible in the EFT.
190
190

190

A weaker cutoff related to BHsis presented by
Brustein et al. 2002
: the quasi-classicality scale for BHsof
M_{\textnormal{P};d=4}/N^{1/4}
, which coincides with a 3-level unitarity bound on the production of species in one-graviton exchange amplitudes
Dvali and Redi 2009
, whereas the scale at which gravitational interaction of elementary particles are strongly coupled is
M_{\textnormal{P};D}/N^{1/\left(D-2\right)}

Dvali and Gomez 2009a
.

They find two cases for their BHscale:
In the emergent string limit, the emergence scale corresponds to the Hagedorn temperature, while the BHdescription breaks down at a lower scale, the Horowitz–Polchinski
Horowitz and Polchinski 1997a
;
Horowitz and Polchinski 1997b
temperature, where the BHtransitions into self-gravitating strings. Ergo, this is the string scale.
In the decompactification limit, the emergence scale corresponds to
M_{\textnormal{P};D}
, while the
d
-dimensional BHtransitions into a
D
-dimensional BHalready at a lower energy scale, where the KKtower emerges. Ergo, this is the KKscale.

Castellano 2024
;
Bedroya et al. 2024a
reasons that the BHscale and the species scale have a deeper connection and fulfil the relation

\frac{\nabla\Lambda_{\text{BH}}}{\Lambda_{\text{BH}}}\frac{\nabla\Lambda_{\textnormal{S}}}{\Lambda_{\textnormal{S}}}=\frac{1}{d-2},

(204)

which can also be understood as a relation between the BHand the species entropy

\nabla\log\mathcal{S}_{\text{BH}}\cdot\nabla\mathcal{S}_{\heartsuit}=d-2.

(205)

UVCutoff Scale
\Lambda_{\text{UV}}

The smallest scale that suppresses all — but finitely many — higher-derivative operators in the effective action, respectively kinematic forms on the IRexpansion of scattering amplitudes / AdSboundary correlators
Aoufia et al. 2024
. This scale coincides with the parametric curvature scale of the smallest possible BH
Aoufia et al. 2024
;
Bedroya et al. 2024b
.

That some confusion appears is understandable: In general, we find
\Lambda_{\text{UV}}\lesssim\Lambda_{\text{QG}}\lesssim\Lambda_{\textnormal{S}}\lesssim M_{\textnormal{P};d}
, e.g. for the weakly coupled critical string, we find an inequality
Aoufia et al. 2024
:
\Lambda_{\text{UV}}=M_{\textnormal{s}}
,
\Lambda_{\text{QG}}=M_{\textnormal{s}}\sqrt{\log g_{s}^{-2}}
,
\Lambda_{\textnormal{S}}=M_{\textnormal{s}}\log g_{s}^{-2}
, whereas in decompactification limits, the scales are all equal.

To give an example of the relative sizes of those scales,
191
191

191

An absolute scale for QGeffects is for example presented by
Aoki et al. 2021
: taking into account the SMof particle physics and GR, scattering amplitudes become inconsistent before
{10}^{16}\text{\,}\mathrm{GeV}
, i.e. new physics has to emerge below this energy scale.

we refer to the dark dimension scenario presented in
section

II.1.1

Bedroya et al. 2024a
:

II.7
Festina Lente

Even though the FLB
193
193

193

Festina Lente
is the Latin translation of the Greek

σπευδε βραδεως

and is the imperative
hasten slowly
. The German translation
eile mit Weile
is a widely used proverb. It is the advice to do things quickly but with sufficient diligence.
Prominent personae, such as the emperors Augustus and Titus or Cosimo I de’ Medici,
made this proverb to their motto
Suetonius Tranquillus 2007
.
Among others, it is used in Goethe’s
Hermann und Dorothea

Goethe 2000
and Bram Stoker’s
Dracula

Stoker 2020
.
In the context of the swampland programme, it is used to summarise the expected behaviour of charged BHsin dSspace: they should haste to decay, but not too quickly and reveal a naked singularity.

is an original and independent conjecture, most of the current studies are the reconciliation of results derived in the context of the WGC. To become familiar with many of the concepts we discuss in this section, we would advise reading this section after
section

II.19
about the WGC.

The FLBstates the following:
A particle of mass
m
under a U(1) gauge field with coupling
g
and charge
q
in a dSvacuum of energy density
V
has a mass of at least

m^{4}\gtrsim 2\left(gq\right)^{2}V=8\pi\alpha V=6\left(gqM_{\textnormal{P}}H\right)^{2},

(210)

where we used the fine-structure constant
\alpha=g^{2}q^{2}/4\pi
and
V=\Lambda/8\pi G=3M_{\textnormal{P}}^{2}H^{2}
with the Hubble parameter
H=\sqrt{\Lambda/3}
;
today, with
M_{\textnormal{P}}\sim${10}^{27}\text{\,}\mathrm{eV}$
and
H\sim${10}^{-33}\text{\,}\mathrm{eV}$
, the bound holds for every charged SMparticle
Gonzalo et al. 2022b
;
Montero et al. 2021
;
Montero et al. 2020b
;
Chrysostomou et al. 2023
.
194
194

194

Note that the FLBis independent of the dimension of the decompactified space because the gauge coupling scales with
E^{2-d/2}

Montero et al. 2021
.
While the electron with
m_{\textnormal{e}}\sim$0.5\text{\,}\mathrm{MeV}$\gg\sqrt{eM_{\textnormal{P}}H}\sim${10}^{-3}\text{\,}\mathrm{eV}$
satisfies the FLBwith 8 orders of magnitude, it is noteworthy that the bound itself is realised by combining two quantities that are 60 orders of magnitude apart
Graña and Herráez 2021
.
