II.11
Non-Negative Null Energy Condition Conjecture

Every 4d theory in flat or closed space with a completion to M-theory satisfies the NEC, i.e.

T_{\mu\nu}l^{\mu}l^{\nu}\geq 0

(261)

for a light-like vector
l^{\mu}
and the matter stress–energy tensor
T_{\mu\nu}

Bernardo et al. 2021d
.

II.11.1
Implications for Cosmology

Violating the NECallows for wormholes
262
262

262

A wormhole solution that satisfies the NECbut requires closed timelike curves is presented by
Engelhardt and Horowitz 2016
.

Visser et al. 2003
, warp-drives, and time machines
Visser and Barcelo 2000
.
Forbidding NEC-violation rules these out.

Folkestad 2023
studies the Penrose inequality
263
263

263

The Penrose inequality
m_{\text{ADM}}\geq\sqrt{A/16\pi}
guarantees that the Arnowitt–Deser–Misner
Arnowitt et al. 1961
;
Arnowitt et al. 1959
(ADM)mass — measuring the total mass–energy content of an asymptotically flat spacetime — is always bigger than the mass of a BHthat would cover the same surface area as the local spacetime patch. A loose interpretation of this might be that a universe cannot be smaller than a BHof the same mass as the universe, i.e. a universe will always be bigger than a BHof the same mass. It also relates to the CCCand rules out naked singularities.

and finds that satisfying the NECis not a sufficient condition to guarantee that the Penrose inequality is satisfied: moreover, the theory has to be supersymmetric or satisfy the stronger dominant energy condition.
264
264

264

A theory that satisfies the dominant energy condition
T_{\mu\nu}v^{\mu}u^{\nu}\geq 0
for any time-like vectors
u^{\mu}
,
v^{\nu}
automatically satisfies the NEC.

Montefalcone et al. 2020
claim that for cosmic acceleration to happen, one of the following three conditions must be violated:

1.

The NEC

2.

The compact dimensions are of fixed size

3.

The metric is (conformally) Ricci-flat

They argue that for models that satisfy all three conditions, Newton’s gravitational constant would have to vary more strongly than compatible with observational bounds. Furthermore, they note that if the NECis violated, the source of the NEC-violation has to reside in the compact dimension and vary in such a way that it precisely tracks the observed EoSof DE,
w_{\textnormal{DE}}(z)
. On the one hand, this sounds feasible, given the popularity of tracking solutions for scalar fields. On the other hand, it sounds unnecessary: The size of compact dimensions is parametrised by dynamical moduli. Furthermore, decompactifications occur on finite timescales, which allows transient phases of cosmological acceleration.

Dark energy

models with
w<-1

Sawicki and Vikman 2013
;
Aref’eva and Volovich 2008
;
Qiu et al. 2008
;
Creminelli et al. 2006
, e.g. phantom fields or some forms of
k
-essence, violate the NEC
Bernardo et al. 2021d
, and are therefore in the swampland.

Inflation

with a cosmological bounce in flat spacetime violates the NEC
265
265

265

Molina-Paris and Visser 1999
present a model that satisfies the NECif the SECis violated instead.

Qiu et al. 2011
;
Lin et al. 2011
;
Cai et al. 2007
;
Brandenberger and Peter 2017
;
De Rham and Melville 2017
;
Easson et al. 2011
;
Creminelli et al. 2006
and is therefore in the swampland.
The same holds for
Superinflation
, where the Hubble parameter grows during inflation
Baldi et al. 2005
;
Bernardo et al. 2021d
.

II.11.2
General Remarks

Since any inequality of the form
f(u_{1},u_{2})\geq 0
with timelike vector fields
u
and continuous function
f
implies
f(l_{1},l_{2})\geq 0
for null vector fields as well, stronger energy conditions, like for example the SEC, imply the NEC
Bernardo et al. 2023
;
Maeda and Martínez 2020
.

The NECitself is neither derived from fundamental principles
Curiel 2017
;
Parikh and Van Der Schaar 2015
;
Parikh and Svesko 2017
, nor does it uphold in higher-dimensional theories (including M-theory), and there are various EFTsviolating it
Bernardo et al. 2021d
;
Qiu et al. 2011
;
Lin et al. 2011
;
Cai et al. 2007
;
Brandenberger and Peter 2017
;
De Rham and Melville 2017
;
Easson et al. 2011
;
Kobayashi et al. 2010
;
Creminelli et al. 2010b
;
Alberte et al. 2016
;
Visser and Barcelo 2000
;
Nicolis et al. 2011
;
Nicolis et al. 2010
;
Rubakov 2014
;
Sawicki and Vikman 2013
;
Aref’eva and Volovich 2008
;
Qiu et al. 2008
;
Creminelli et al. 2006
;
Buniy et al. 2006
;
Dubovsky et al. 2006
, e.g. theories with superluminal propagation, pathological instabilities, or unitarity respectively causality violations
Wesley 2009
;
Koster and Postma 2011
;
Carroll et al. 2003
;
Hsu et al. 2004
;
Cline et al. 2004
;
Dubovsky et al. 2006
;
Buniy et al. 2006
;
Tipler 1976
;
Eling et al. 2007
;
Lee et al. 2011
.

Das et al. 2019
show that to have dSexpansion in 4d with static compactified dimensions, either the NECis violated everywhere in the compactified dimensions, the extra-dimensions have positive curvature, or the 4d expansion sources warping. This follows from the geometry, i.e. the metric, irrespective of matter content or the Einstein field equations.
Imposing the NECto higher-dimensional spaces yields geometric restrictions that cannot be produced from the lower-dimensional spacetimes, as this would require null vectors in the higher-dimensional space with vanishing components in external directions
Bernardo et al. 2021d
.
In M-theory, the NECdoes not have to be satisfied in 11 dimensions, since higher-order terms allow the M-theory action to be free of pathologies respectively to cancel anomalies
Wesley 2009
.
266
266

266

Yet if an energy condition is satisfied in a higher-dimensional manifold — given some reasonable assumptions, e.g. constant internal volume and positive well-defined internal metric (meaning Newton’s constant is constant, and the internal metric is non-singular) —, the lower-dimensional energy condition is respected as well
Bernardo et al. 2023
.

Steinhardt and Wesley 2009
;
Wesley 2008
;
Wesley 2009
argue that if the higher-dimensional theory satisfies the NEC, then accelerated expansion in 4d is only a transient phenomenon of a few
e
-folds — ruling out most models of inflation and forbidding various forms of DE. However,
Koster and Postma 2011
show that this only holds if one makes overly strong assumptions, namely it holds only in the absence of branes and localised matter sources for KKcompactifications that have a warp factor in the metric that depends on the volume modulus of the compact space — the last assumption is incompatible with a time-dependent setup, ergo cannot lead to accelerated expansion in 4d.

Deffayet et al. 2024b
note that if a universe becomes trapped in a meta-stable phase without extra-dimensions decompactifying, then the NECmust be violated with a inhomogeneously distributed, time-varying component that is synchronised with the EoSof the decompactified EFT. However, none of their tested potentials in heterotic M-theory shows such a behaviour that would require NEC-violation.

Bernardo et al. 2021d
show that this cancellation takes place if there is a hierarchy among the higher-order terms, such that the M-theory action is well-defined, which imposes the following constraint on the string coupling:

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}g_{\textnormal{s}}}{\mathrm{d}\tau}

\displaystyle\propto g_{\textnormal{s}}^{1+1/n}

(262)

\displaystyle{1+1/n}

\displaystyle\geq 0

(263)

\displaystyle\Rightarrow\frac{1}{n}

\displaystyle\geq-1.

(264)

This can be compared to the NEC:

\displaystyle-\frac{\mathrm{d}^{2}\log t^{\mathfrak{c}}}{\mathrm{d}t^{2}}+kt^{-2\mathfrak{c}}

\displaystyle\geq 0

(265)

\displaystyle\Rightarrow\mathfrak{c}+kt^{2\left(1-\mathfrak{c}\right)}

\displaystyle\geq 0,

(266)

with
k\in\{0,\pm 1\}
the curvature.
For a 4d FLRWmetric with
a(t)\propto t^{\mathfrak{c}}
respectively
a(\tau)\propto\tau^{n}
, we find that
\mathfrak{c}=n/\left(1+n\right)
.
For flat and closed spacetimes (
k\in\{0,+1\}
), the NECcorresponds to
eq.

264
, yet for negatively curved space,
eq.

264
does not imply the NEC. This observation leads to the swampland conjecture.
The work by
Bernardo et al. 2021d
rests on four assumptions:

1.

The external FLRWspacetime is parametrised by
a(\tau)\propto\tau^{n}
.
267
267

267

This holds for example for perfect fluids in 4d with
p=w\rho

Bernardo et al. 2021d
.

2.

The Wilsonian EFTdescription is well-defined, i.e. the quantum corrections have a hierarchy in the underlying M-theory.
268
268

268

This means that the quantum corrections are of different order and the action allows for truncation
Bernardo et al. 2021d
.

3.

G_{\textnormal{N}}=\text{cst.}
269
269

269

This does not rule out time-dependent fluxes or internal dimensions
Bernardo et al. 2021d
.

4.

The type IIB dilaton is time-independent.
270
270

270

This should not be too difficult to relax
Bernardo et al. 2021d
.

Introducing a non-minimal coupling, e.g. through a contribution to the Lagrangian of the form
\delta\mathcal{L}=\Xi R\phi^{2}
, where
\Xi
is the coupling strength,
R
the Ricci scalar and
\phi
the field strength, leads to a violation of the NEC
Fliss et al. 2023
. This violation disappears if one goes (using a conformal transformation) from the Jordan frame, where the field strength
\phi
appears as a product with the Ricci scalar
R
, to the Einstein frame, where no such multiplicity appears
Fliss et al. 2023
.
Fliss et al. 2023
raise the question if not both frames should lead to similar spacetime geometries. In the EFTview they adopt, the metrics are close. They show that in their example, the NECis only violated for large field values. And since this only happens in the Jordan frame, they conclude that the EFTbreaks down at large field values, which is predicted by the DCas well. Furthermore, they derive a bound on the maximum number of particles in the EFT:

N_{\text{max}}\leq\frac{5}{8}\frac{\pi^{d-2}}{\Gamma(d-2)V_{d-3}}\frac{\phi_{\text{max}}^{2}}{\lambda^{d-2}_{\bot}},

(267)

with
\lambda_{\bot}
an arbitrary parameter and
V_{n-3}
the volume of unit sphere
S^{n-3}=\frac{2\pi^{\frac{n-2}{2}}}{\Gamma\left(\frac{n-2}{2}\right)}
. This bound is interesting regarding the SSC, as it places a lower bound on the cutoff scale than the SSCitself:

\Lambda<\frac{M_{\textnormal{P}}^{d}}{\left(\frac{5}{8}\frac{\pi^{d-2}}{\Gamma(d-2)V_{d-3}}\frac{\phi_{\text{max}}^{2}}{\lambda^{d-2}_{\bot}}\right)^{\frac{1}{d-2}}}<\frac{M_{\textnormal{P}}^{d}}{N^{\frac{1}{d-2}}}.

(268)

II.11.3
Evidence

Bernardo et al. 2021d
promote the NECto a swampland conjecture. Their paper is to our knowledge the first and so far only paper that does this. The NECitself is too broad to be reviewed here in depth; therefore, we mostly focus on the paper by
Bernardo et al. 2021d
, the sources they cite, as well as the papers that cite
Bernardo et al. 2021d
. A relation to the TCCis mentioned in
section

II.20.9
.

II.12
No Non-Supersymmetric Theories Conjecture

The no non-SUSY conjecture states that there are no non-supersymmetric stable AdSvacuum solutions in 4 dimensions
Ooguri and Vafa 2017
;
Freivogel and Kleban 2016
;
Hebecker and Leonhardt 2021
. It’s been suggested that even metastable vacua are ruled out, but there are discussions about and counterexamples to this more stringent version of the conjecture
Hebecker and Leonhardt 2021
;
Fischbacher et al. 2010
;
Guarino et al. 2020
;
Guarino et al. 2021
;
Almuhairi and Polchinski 2014
;
Narayan and Trivedi 2010
;
Horowitz et al. 2008
;
Ooguri and Vafa 2017
;
Seo 2024a
.

II.12.1
Implications for Cosmology

Big Bang

Avoiding a big bang and a past-incomplete beginning of our Universe,
Nam 2024
proposes that our Universe could have started as the transition of an unstable, non-supersymmetric AdSvacuum into a supersymmetric AdSvacuum. This predicts that our Universe is closed. A thin bubble-wall is mediating the decay, and there is an ADMmass term that decays into radiation and therefore takes care of reheating. Their model goes into inflation right after the tunnelling.

Black Holes

AdSdescribes the near-horizon geometry of extremal BHs. The instability of AdSgoes hand in hand with the conjectured instability of BHsas described in
section

II.19
.

Dark Energy

If a stronger form of the no non-SUSY conjecture were true, namely that no stable nor metastable AdSvacua were allowed, then late-time cosmologies with a small negative cosmological constant were forbidden
Hebecker and Leonhardt 2021
.

Neutrinos

Minkowski and dSvacua are generally allowed if there is a surplus of light fermions present; surplus meaning that there are more (light) fermions than (massless and light) bosons, light meaning
m\lesssim\Lambda^{1/d}

Gonzalo et al. 2021
. This leads to interesting predictions for neutrinos:
On the one hand, the mass of the lightest neutrino is constrained to
m_{\nu}\lesssim\Lambda^{1/4}

Gonzalo et al. 2022b
; on the other hand, the neutrino is either a Dirac particle
Ooguri and Vafa 2017
;
Hamada and Shiu 2017
;
Gonzalo et al. 2018
; or a Majorana particle accompanied by a sufficiently large number of axions, a very light Weyl fermion, or a light gravitino in Minkowski or dSspace
Anchordoqui et al. 2024k
;
Anchordoqui et al. 2024c
;
Gonzalo et al. 2021
;
Ibanez et al. 2017
;
Graña and Herráez 2021
.
271
271

271

See also
section

II.19.1
on neutrinos with similar conclusions.

If the lightest neutrino had a mass
m_{\nu}>\Lambda^{1/4}
, a stable AdSpotential would form,
272
272

272

Compactification of the SMyields a potential for the radion field depending on two terms: the cosmological constant and a neutrino-mass-dependent one-loop Casimir potential
Gonzalo et al. 2022b
.

which would push the SMinto the swampland —which does not happen if the lightest neutrino has a mass
m_{\nu}\lesssim\Lambda^{1/4}

Gonzalo et al. 2022b
.
If we write the neutrino mass in terms of it’s Yukawa coupling
g_{\textnormal{Y}}
and the Higgs vacuum expectation value
h
, we find that the naturalness problems of the cosmological and EWenergy scales are related
van Beest et al. 2022
:
\expectationvalue{h}\lesssim\Lambda^{1/4}/g_{\textnormal{Y}}
. The swampland programme might remedy the hierarchy problem by showing that EFTswith higher EWenergy scales simply do not admit UVcompletions into QG.

In a generalisation of the neutrino case,
Nakayama et al. 2019
explore a DMmodel with two additional light chiral fermions: a quasi-stable fermion and an ultralight stable fermion. The quasi-stable fermion in their model acts as DMwith two dominant decay channels: into three neutrinos and into a neutrino and a photon. The latter decay channel could explain the observed x-ray line at
3.55\text{\,}\mathrm{keV}

Boyarsky et al. 2015
;
Boyarsky et al. 2014
;
Bulbul et al. 2014
. The ultralight stable fermion destabilises AdSvacua.

Particle Physics

Very reassuring findings regarding the SMare presented by
Gonzalo and Ibáñez 2018
: Assuming that AdSvacua are unstable, they show the need for a Higgs boson, that its experimentally determined mass lies in the narrow theoretically allowed range, and that there have to be three or more generations of particles. Without the Higgs, there would be more bosonic degrees of freedom (36) than fermionic ones (24, assuming Dirac neutrinos), rendering the potential negative, leading to a stable, non-supersymmetric AdSvacuum
Graña and Herráez 2021
.

II.12.2
General Remarks

To stabilise a vacuum,
Etxebarria et al. 2020
see two angles:
topology and dynamics – the former does not stabilise vacua in QGand the latter requires supersymmetry.
An example of a topological obstruction is the absence of global symmetries.
273
273

273

See
section

II.10
for why there are no global symmetries in QG.

An example of a dynamical obstruction is the WGC, which quantifies how close we can get to restoring a global symmetry, which puts a constraint on the dynamics of the theory, e.g. in the form of certain energy conditions.
The no non-SUSY conjecture is an offspring of the WGC: The WGCsays that gravity is weak, such that BHscan decay. This happens when the electromagnetic forces overpower the gravitational forces.
An extension of the WGCstates that the equality between gravitational attraction and electromagnetic repulsion only occurs if the theory is supersymmetric and the states in question are BPSstates
Cicoli et al. 2023a
. For non-supersymmetric states, this leads to an instability
Ooguri and Vafa 2017
;
Cicoli et al. 2023a
;
Ibanez et al. 2017
;
Banerjee et al. 2019
: Assume we have a brane charged with respect to a flux, which supports a non-supersymmetric vacuum, and a tension less than this charge. Such a brane will nucleate in AdS, the flux-induced Coulomb repulsion wins over the tension, and the brane expands, which reduces the flux when the brane reaches the AdSboundary in finite time. Only an infinite number of fluxes could stabilise a non-supersymmetric AdSsolution, which would contradict the
finite number of massless fields conjecture
(see
section

II.8
)
Gonzalo et al. 2022b
;
Cicoli et al. 2023a
.
To put it more concise: The WGCpredicts a codimension one brane with a tension smaller than its charge, which corresponds to an instability in AdS
van Beest et al. 2022
.
Another approach to show why this is problematic comes from holography: In the gravitational dual description, the decay can be slow and the brane be quasi-stable. At the horizon, however, the decay is instantaneous and there is no AdSdual description. If there are no stable non-supersymmetric AdSsolutions, then there are no non-supersymmetric CFTsthat admit a gravity dual
Narayan and Trivedi 2010
;
Freivogel and Kleban 2016
.
The no non-SUSY conjecture generalises these observations and rules out non-supersymmetric states entirely
Harada and Nakayama 2022
.
However, a word of caution is in order:
The no non-SUSY conjecture is motived by BHphysics and is therefore based on GR — non-supersymmetric theories based on topological field theories are therefore
not
ruled-out
Ooguri and Vafa 2017
.

Freivogel and Kleban 2016
generally question the stability of vacua. They claim that every non-supersymmetric AdSand Minkowski vacuum can decay, and that every supersymmetric vacuum is marginally unstable and connected to at least one vacuum through a stability bound–saturating domain wall. Every dSvacuum is meta-stable and decays on scales smaller than the horizon
Freivogel and Kleban 2016
. Their train of thought runs as follows: If the Universe lives long enough to produce Boltzmann brains, Boltzmann brains would outnumber
‘ordinary observers’
. Since this is not the case, the vacuum must decay on timescales smaller than the timescale that leads to Boltzmann brains, i.e. the decay process occurs on a scale smaller than the horizon scale. If the vacuum is stable, the time frame where Boltzmann brains live is so much bigger than the time span before that, that it is very unlikely to live in the pre–Boltzmann brain period. This reasoning is very similar to the anthropic principle, and we leave it to the reader to judge to what degree they want to follow this reasoning, which is challenged by
Banks 2016
(in the appendix of the paper):
The stability of dSis an issue if our Universe began as a low entropy fluctuation of a finite ensemble, whose time development is determined by a time-independent Hamiltonian. Since the observed cosmology has no time-like Killing vector, a time-dependent Hamiltonian is more accurate
Banks 2016
;
Banks and Fischler 2015
.
Furthermore, in complete contradiction with
Freivogel and Kleban 2016
’s work is the work by
Danielsson et al. 2016
, which shows that a perturbatively stable AdSvacuum is automatically non-perturbatively stable — this forbids tunnelling within the landscape from one stable vacuum to another one.
274
274

274

It is argued that the observed instabilities in non-supersymmetric AdSvacua are of perturbative nature and originate from the interplay between open and closed strings
Danielsson and Dibitetto 2017
.

A vacuum can only decay into a lower-energy vacuum, if the energy difference is bigger than the tension in the domain wall between the two vacua. The tension is proportional to the surface area of the domain wall, which is, for AdS, proportional to the volume. Volume and surface growing at the same rate prohibits tunnelling, as the energy to tunnel is always too high.
Narayan and Trivedi 2010

An example of a stabilised 4d
\mathcal{N}=1
Minkowski vacuum is presented by
Bardzell et al. 2022
, arising from a non-geometric type IIB setup.

Banks 2019
argues that no dSnor Minkowski vacuum can decay into an AdSvacuum, as this would lead to a big crunch singularity, where no tunnelling process exists for. This agrees with the AdSDC, which states that AdSand dSspace are an infinite distance apart.

II.13
Positive Gauss–Bonnet Term Conjecture

The GBterm has to be positive:

\displaystyle\mathfrak{G}=R_{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}R^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}-4R_{\mu\nu}R^{\mu\nu}+R^{2}>0.

(269)

II.13.1
Implications for Cosmology

Black Holes

A negative contribution from the GBterm during Hawking evaporation might lead to a naked singularity
Boulware and Deser 1985
, violating the CCC
Ong 2022
and various swampland conjectures that disfavour the existence of stable remnants (
sections

II.2
,
II.10
and
II.19
).

Dark Energy

The GBterm affects the Hubble constant, as can be seen in the spatial components of the stress-energy tensor
Hu et al. 2024
:
279
279

279

Hu et al. 2024
allow for torsion in their work.

\displaystyle S

\displaystyle=\int\!\sqrt{-g}\left(\frac{R}{16\pi G}+f\left(\mathfrak{G}\right)\right)\,\mathrm{d}^{4}x

(270)

\displaystyle T_{ij}

\displaystyle=\left[-a^{2}\left(2\dot{H}+3H^{2}\right)+8\left(\ddot{f}\ddot{\mathfrak{G}}+\dddot{f}\dot{\mathfrak{G}}^{2}\right)\right.

(271)

\displaystyle\qquad\left.-a^{2}H^{2}-10a^{2}\ddot{f}\dot{\mathfrak{G}}H^{3}\right]\delta_{ij}.

(272)

The first term shows the standard cosmic expansion dynamics and is linked to the Friedmann equations.
The second term represents higher-order gravitational corrections.
The last term shows the impact of the GBterm on the Hubble parameter and the spacetime geometry.

Inflation

While the introduction of a GBterm does not introduce vector modes, it modifies the dispersion relation of scalar and tensor modes
Glavan and Lin 2020
. By imposing that GWstravel at the speed of light,
280
280

280

In Einstein–Gauss–Bonnet theory, the GWspeed is given by
c_{\textnormal{T}}^{2}=1-8\mathfrak{c}\left(\ddot{g}_{\text{GB}}-H\dot{g}_{\text{GB}}\right)/2\left(1/\left(8\pi G\right)^{2}-8\mathfrak{c}\dot{g}_{\text{GB}}H\right)
, which is observed to be equal to the speed of light
Odintsov and Oikonomou 2020
.

which yields
\ddot{g}_{\text{GB}}=H\dot{g}_{\text{GB}}
,

Odintsov and Oikonomou 2020
link the GBcoupling function to the tensor-to-scalar ratio and the scalar field dispersion:

\displaystyle\dot{g}_{\text{GB}}

\displaystyle=g^{\prime}_{\text{GB}}\dot{\phi}

(273)

\displaystyle\Delta\phi

\displaystyle>\Delta N_{e}\frac{g^{\prime}_{\text{GB}}}{g^{\prime\prime}_{\text{GB}}}

(274)

\displaystyle r_{\textnormal{ts}}

\displaystyle=8\left(\frac{g^{\prime}_{\text{GB}}}{g^{\prime\prime}_{\text{GB}}}\right)^{2},

(275)

and therewith to the DCas well as to the dSCand the TCC.

II.13.2
General Remarks

It is often assumed that the GBterm is a total derivative in 4d that does not contribute to gravitational dynamics (acting as a topological term), as the contribution to the EoMis proportional to
d-4

Glavan and Lin 2020
. However, if the coupling constant is taken to be
g_{\text{GB}}\rightarrow g_{\text{GB}}/\left(d-4\right)
, this factor cancels and the GBterm does contribute
Glavan and Lin 2020
. Even if it is acting as a topological term without introducing additional degrees of freedom, the GBinvariant can facilitate renormalisation
Hu et al. 2024
. The quadratic Ricci term helps with stability in the UVregime of a theory, where it improves the divergence behaviour
Hu et al. 2024
.

An important argument why the GBterm should be positive comes from string theory: In Einstein–Gauss–Bonnet theory, the GBterm corresponds to the inverse string tension, which is positive
Ong 2022
.

If the GBterm is considered a perturbation to the on-shell action, then the contribution is found to be positive in the
\mathcal{N}=1
axiverse settings studied by
Martucci et al. 2024
.

Other works do not constrain the GBterm itself but its coupling function,
while
Charmousis and Padilla 2008
argue that GBgravity is generally unstable,

Camanho et al. 2016
argue that
g_{\text{GB}}\gg l_{\textnormal{P}}^{2}
violates causality;

Sircar et al. 2016
find that a negative coupling leads to anomalies;

Yaida 2009
;
Buchel et al. 2010
;
Brigante et al. 2008
find
-7/36\leq g_{\text{GB}}\leq 9/100
;
and
Odintsov and Oikonomou 2020
find
\ddot{g}_{\text{GB}}=H\dot{g}_{\text{GB}}
as we discussed in
section

II.13.1
.

II.13.3
Evidence

This conjecture is only recently discussed in a swampland context, e.g. by
Odintsov and Oikonomou 2020
;
Ong 2022
.
Motivation comes from
holography
Ong 2022
,
considerations of unitarity in pure 4D gravity
Cheung and Remmen 2017
,
stability of simple dilatonic models
Etxebarria et al. 2020
,
as well as the suppression of problematic wormhole effects
Martucci et al. 2023
;
Kallosh et al. 1995
.

II.14
Species Scale Conjecture

In a
d
-dimensional theory coupled to gravity with
d
-dimensional Planck mass
M_{\textnormal{P};d}
,
there are
N_{\textnormal{S}}
particle states below a cutoff scale

\Lambda_{\textnormal{S}}=\frac{M_{\textnormal{P};d}}{N_{\textnormal{S}}^{\frac{1}{d-2}}};

(276)

after this scale, new gravitational dynamics appear, e.g. in the form of higher-derivative operators
Dvali 2010a
;
Palti 2019
;
Castellano et al. 2023a
;
Basile et al. 2024b
;
Dvali and Lust 2009
;
Dvali and Solodukhin 2008
.

An earlier definition that is recently regaining popularity is the following:
281
281

281

It has recently been noted that this definition allows for interpreting the number of species as a species entropy, which leads to the 0
th
–3
rd
law of species thermodynamics.

If the smallest BHthat can be semi-classically
282
282

282

Quantum-corrections
increase
the BHhorizon, ergo, the bound is absolute and the species scale can only increase due to quantum-corrections
Dvali and Gomez 2010
;
Brustein et al. 2009
.

described in an EFThas a Schwarzschild radius of
r_{\heartsuit}
, then there are
N_{\textnormal{S}}
weakly coupled elementary particles with a decay width much less than their mass in the theory, and the following relation holds:

r_{\heartsuit}>l_{\textnormal{S}}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=N_{\textnormal{S}}^{1/\left(d-2\right)}l_{\textnormal{P};d},

(277)

with
l_{\textnormal{P};d}=1/M_{\textnormal{P};d}
the
d
-dimensional Planck length and
l_{\textnormal{S}}
the
species scale

Dvali and Gomez 2010
;
Brustein et al. 2009
;
Dvali and Gomez 2009a
;
Dvali and Gomez 2009b
;
Dvali 2010b
;
Dvali and Redi 2008
;
Dvali 2010a
;
Antoniadis 2014
;
Anchordoqui 2022
. The species scale is the physical resolution limit, below which the
N_{\textnormal{S}}
bits of information that correspond to the
N_{\textnormal{S}}
particle species can no longer be stored
Gomez 2013
;
Dvali and Gomez 2009b
;
Dvali and Gomez 2009a
.
This earlier definition, and the more recent one above that focuses on an energy scale instead of a length scale, are related by noting that
l_{\textnormal{S}}^{-1}=\Lambda_{\textnormal{S}}
and
N_{\textnormal{S}}\simeq\left(l_{\textnormal{S}}M_{\textnormal{P}}\right)^{d-2}\simeq\mathcal{S}_{\textnormal{S}}
, where we already introduce the species entropy that will be of interest later.

The species scale gets weaker and vanishes in the limit of infinite (large) dimension
d

Bonnefoy et al. 2021
.

II.14.1
Implications for Cosmology

Axions

have non-renormalisable interactions that lead to a cutoff scale for the validity of the description

f\frac{8\pi^{2}}{g^{2}}\lesssim M_{\textnormal{P}},

(278)

with

The latter cutoff is not an EFTcutoff, but a UVcutoff where the Chern–Simons description of the axion–gluon interaction breaks down

Below

For example, quantum hair

Early Universe

Having a tower of states that emerges means that the number of (relativistic) degrees of freedom (usually denoted as
g_{*}
) increases. As we will show later, an increase in the temperature results in an increase in the number of species. This has consequences:

•

The density of relativistic species
\rho_{\text{r}}=\frac{\pi^{2}}{30}g_{*}(T)T^{4}
decreases over time;
g_{*}=106.75
in the early Universe, where all SMparticles are relativistic
Baumann 2015
. However, the SSCmight shed new light on this number and increase it for early times.

•

In the radiation dominated epoch, the Hubble parameter scales like
H\propto\sqrt{g_{*}}
, which affects the freeze-out temperature for BBN: increasing
g_{*}
increases the freeze-out temperature, which increases the neutron–proton ratio at freeze-out, which in turn increases the final helium abundance
Baumann 2015
.

Gravitinos

Cribiori and Lust 2023
identify the entropy of the species with their number,
\mathcal{S}_{\textnormal{S}}=N_{\textnormal{S}}
, show for the gravitino mass
m_{\textnormal{3/2}}
that

N_{\textnormal{S}}\simeq-\log m_{\textnormal{3/2}}^{2},

(294)

and generalise the relation for
V\leq 0
:

N_{\textnormal{S}}\leq-\log\left(-\frac{V}{3}\right)

(295)

respectively

e^{-N_{\textnormal{S}}}\gtrsim-V\geq 0.

(296)

Inflation

is still an open field for model building. The species scale can be used to assess specific inflationary models.
For flat regions of the potential,
286
286

286

r_{\text{h}_{\text{max}}}\sim 1/\sqrt{V}

there are proposed upper bounds of
V(\phi)<\Lambda_{\textnormal{S}}^{2}(\phi)

;

respectively
V<\Lambda_{\textnormal{S}}^{d}

Since the number of states
N_{\textnormal{S}}\sim m^{-\mathfrak{l}}M_{\textnormal{P}}^{\mathfrak{l}}
, with
\mathfrak{l}
some
\order{1}
parameter, we find
\Lambda_{\textnormal{S}}\sim m^{\mathfrak{l}/2}M_{\textnormal{P}}^{-\mathfrak{l}/2}
; this, and the moduli dependence of the species scale together with the exponential mass suppression predicted by the DC, motivate

\Lambda_{\textnormal{S}}=\Lambda_{0}e^{-\lambda_{\textnormal{S}}\Delta\phi},

with
\Lambda_{0}<M_{\textnormal{P}}
corresponding to the starting point with zero field displacement
Scalisi 2024
.
This yields an upper bound on the scalar field range

\Delta\phi\leq\frac{1}{\lambda_{\textnormal{S}}}\log\frac{M_{\textnormal{P}}}{\Lambda_{\textnormal{S}}}-\mathfrak{l},

(298)

with

\lambda_{\textnormal{S}}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\absolutevalue{\frac{\Lambda_\textnormal{S}^\prime}{\Lambda_\textnormal{S}}},

(299)

which measures how fast the species scale

becomes light
Etheredge et al. 2024b
;
Scalisi 2024

and
\mathfrak{l}\sim\order{1}
a correction factor (which we will omit in the following) that can be introduced to correct for an overestimation of the diameter of the moduli space in the asymptotic limit
van de Heisteeg et al. 2024a
.
In the limit
\Lambda_{\textnormal{S}}\rightarrow 0
, the EFTis nowhere valid, and in the limit
\Lambda_{\textnormal{S}}\rightarrow M_{\textnormal{P}}
, no field displacement is allowed.
We know that
\Lambda_{\textnormal{S}}\geq H=\sqrt{\pi^{2}A_{s}r_{\text{ts}}/2}M_{\textnormal{P}}
, and with the amplitude of the scalar mode
A_{s}\approx$2.1\text{\times}{10}^{-9}$

Collaboration et al. 2020
it follows that

\Delta\phi\lesssim\frac{1}{2\lambda_{\textnormal{S}}}\log\frac{10^{8}}{r_{\text{ts}}},

(300)

where we have used that
\log 1/\sqrt{r}=\frac{1}{2}\log 1/r

Scalisi 2024
.
Since the tensor-to-scalar ratio from primordial GWshas a known bound of

BICEP/Keck Collaboration et al. 2021
,
and

(301)

(302)

in the emergent string limit

Lyth Bound

\Delta\phi=\sqrt{2\epsilon_{V}}N_{e}\gtrsim\sqrt{\frac{r_{\textnormal{ts}}}{0.002}}
. The Lyth bound is considered to be a generic lower bound on the required field displacement to successfully inflate the Universe enough within
N_{e}=60

e
-folds
Lyth 1997
;
Scalisi 2024
;
Boubekeur and Lyth 2005
;
Garcia-Bellido et al. 2014
;
van de Heisteeg et al. 2023a
.

Starobinsky Inflation

\Delta\phi\simeq 15\log\left(60\right)\sqrt{2r_{\textnormal{ts}}}
. For most values of
r_{\textnormal{ts}}
, Starobinsky inflation is in tension with the species scale. We elaborate on Starobinsky inflation and a similar model below.

Hilltop Models

\Delta\phi\simeq\left(\frac{\mathfrak{p}-2}{\sqrt{2}\left(\mathfrak{p}-1\right)}\right)^{1-2/\mathfrak{p}}\mu^{2-2/\mathfrak{p}}\sim r_{\textnormal{ts}}^{1/\mathfrak{p}}
stemming from potentials of the form

V(\phi)=V_{0}\left(1-\left(\frac{\phi}{\mu}\right)^{\frac{2\left(1-\mathfrak{p}\right)}{2-\mathfrak{p}}}+\dots\right),

where the ellipsis indicates higher-order terms in
\phi/\mu
. Hilltop models are unproblematic from a species scale perspective, as well as compatible with the DCand the dSC, but incompatible with the WGCand the Lyth bound.

Chaotic Inflation

\Delta\phi\simeq 30\sqrt{2r_{\textnormal{ts}}}
. We discuss chaotic inflation in more detail in
section

II.19.1
and below.

Brane Inflation / Inverse Hilltop Models

\Delta\phi\simeq 30\sqrt{2r_{\textnormal{ts}}}/\left(2-\mathfrak{l}\right)
;
1<\mathfrak{l}<2
controls the model
Dvali and Tye 1998
;
Dvali 1999
;
Dvali et al. 2001
;
Burgess et al. 2001
;
Kachru et al. 2003b
;
Hertzberg et al. 2008
. For
\mathfrak{l}=1
we find chaotic inflation and for
\mathfrak{l}\simeq 1.51
Starobinsky inflation. For
\mathfrak{l}<1.26
no tension with the species scale arises, whereas for
\mathfrak{l}>1.7
tension with the species scale bound (
\lambda_{\text{min}}
) cannot be avoided.

Figure 5:
Depicted are the upper (

More on Starobinsky Inflation

Lust et al. 2023
show that Starobinsky inflation belongs to the swampland: In the Starobinsky model, the standard Einstein–Hilbert action has an extra term proportional to the square of the scalar curvature:

S=\int\!\sqrt{-g}\frac{M_{\textnormal{P}}^{2}}{2}\left(R+\frac{R^{2}}{M^{2}}\right)\,\mathrm{d}^{4}x

(304)

where the scale
M\sim${10}^{14}\text{\,}\mathrm{GeV}$
is fixed by CMBmeasurements. The extra term can be explained by / be interpreted as

•

scalar fields

•

additional matter fields

•

towers of states.

In their paper, they show that the
R^{2}
-term can be regarded as a renormalisation effect of a tower of states, which implies that the scale
M
is related to the species scale
\Lambda_{\textnormal{S}}
. Furthermore, they reason that
H\sim M\sim\Lambda_{\textnormal{S}}\sim${10}^{14}\text{\,}\mathrm{GeV}$
and deduce that
{10}^{10}
species are present during inflation, which has severe phenomenological consequences for inflation, for example, KKmodes should be present, even in the EFT, as
m_{\text{KK}}\sim${10}^{4}\text{\,}\mathrm{GeV}$<H
. Therefore, the Starobinsky model is an inconsistent EFTand lies in the Swampland.

Modular Inflation

A potential with similar features as Starobinsky inflation, without sharing its incompatibility with the SSC, is the following
Casas and Ibáñez 2024
:

V(\phi)=\mu^{d-2}M_{\textnormal{P}}^{2}G^{ij}\frac{\left(\partial_{i}\Lambda_{\textnormal{S}}\right)\left(\partial_{j}\Lambda_{\textnormal{S}}\right)}{\Lambda_{\textnormal{S}}^{2}}.

(305)

•

There are no singularities at finite distance in moduli space, i.e. it is described within the EFT,

•

the potential depends solely on the species scale and its derivatives,

•

a sufficiently large period of inflation can be realised due to the flat plateau that can be reached asymptotically, and

•

the potential is invariant and automorphic to the duality symmetries of the theory.

The potential can be expressed in a modular form,
288
288

288

The potential can be expressed as

V\simeq\left(\text{Im}{\tau}\right)^{2}\absolutevalue{\Tilde{G}_2}^{2}/N_{\textnormal{S}}^{2},

with
\tilde{G}_{2}
the Eisenstein modular form of weight 2,
and
N_{\textnormal{S}}\simeq-\log\left(\text{Im}\tau\absolutevalue{\eta(\tau)}^{4}\right)
,
\eta
being the Dedekind function, and

\tau
being a complex scalar modulus
Casas and Ibáñez 2024
.

that offers some noteworthy advantages
Casas and Ibáñez 2024
,
289
289

289

First, for large moduli, the model resembles the Starobinsky model,
second, the number of
e
-folds corresponds to the number of species,
N_{e}\simeq N_{\textnormal{S}}
,
and third, the tower of states seems to play an important role during inflation, as also
\epsilon_{V}\simeq\Lambda_{\textnormal{S}}^{4}
holds
Casas and Ibáñez 2024
.

but, moreover, the model can be generalised for
SL(2,\mathbb{Z})
modular symmetries in such a way that the species scale is directly connected to observables
Aoki and Otsuka 2024
:
The generalised Lagrangian is of the form

\displaystyle\frac{L\left(\tau,\bar{\tau}\right)}{\sqrt{-g}}

\displaystyle=\frac{R}{2}M_{\textnormal{P}}^{2}-\frac{M_{\textnormal{P}}^{2}\partial\tau\partial\bar{\tau}}{2\beta^{2}\tau_{2}^{2}}-V\left(\tau,\bar{\tau}\right)

(306)

\displaystyle\tau

\displaystyle=\tau_{1}+i\tau_{2}

(307)

with
\beta
a model-parameter.
Inflation takes place if
\tau_{1}=0
and
\tau_{2}\gg 1
, such that the potential will be of the form

V\left(\tau,\bar{\tau}\right)\simeq V_{0}\left(1-\frac{\varsigma}{\tau_{2}}+\dots\right),

(308)

with the model parameter
\varsigma
related to the normalisation of the potential,
290
290

290

The case
\varsigma=1
,
\beta=\sqrt{2/3\alpha}
is related to the
\alpha
-attractor model
Aoki and Otsuka 2024
.

which, in case of canonically normalised inflation with
\tau_{2}=\exp\left(\beta\phi/M_{\textnormal{P}}\right)
, can be written as

V_{\textnormal{I}}\left(\phi\right)\simeq V_{0}\left(1-\varsigma e^{-\beta\phi/M_{\textnormal{P}}}\right).

(309)

As a next step, the slow-roll parameters and the number of
e
-folds can be computed:

\displaystyle\epsilon_{V}

\displaystyle=\frac{\varsigma^{2}\beta^{2}e^{-2\beta\phi/M_{\textnormal{P}}}}{2}

(310)

\displaystyle\eta_{V}

\displaystyle=-\beta^{2}\varsigma e^{-\beta\phi/M_{\textnormal{P}}}

(311)

\displaystyle N_{e}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{M_{\textnormal{P}}}\int_{\phi_{\textnormal{rh}}}^{\phi_{*}}\!\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\epsilon_{V}}}\,\mathrm{d}\phi

(312)

\displaystyle=\frac{e^{\beta\phi_{*}/M_{\textnormal{P}}}-e^{\beta\phi_{\textnormal{rh}}/M_{\textnormal{P}}}}{\beta^{2}\varsigma}.

(313)

The last equation gives the number of
e
-foldings between the end of reheating and the time the CMBexits the horizon.
Now, we use
\phi_{*}\gg\phi_{\textnormal{rh}}
and
\tau_{2}\gg 1\Rightarrow N_{\textnormal{S}}\simeq 2\pi\tau_{2}
to find

\displaystyle\phi_{*}

\displaystyle\simeq\frac{\log\left(\beta^{2}\varsigma N_{\textnormal{S}}\right)}{\beta}

(314)

\displaystyle\tau_{2}^{*}

\displaystyle\simeq\beta^{2}\varsigma N_{\textnormal{S}}

(315)

\displaystyle N_{e}

\displaystyle\simeq\frac{\tau_{2}^{*}}{\beta^{2}\varsigma}

(316)

\displaystyle=\frac{N_{\textnormal{S}}}{2\pi\beta^{2}\varsigma}

(317)

\displaystyle\simeq\frac{1}{2\pi\beta^{2}\varsigma}\left(\frac{M_{\textnormal{P}}}{\Lambda_{\text{S}}}\right)^{2}

(318)

\displaystyle n_{s}

\displaystyle=1-\frac{2}{N_{e}}

(319)

\displaystyle\simeq 1-4\pi\beta^{2}\varsigma\left(\frac{\Lambda_{\textnormal{S}}}{M_{\textnormal{P}}}\right)^{2}

(320)

\displaystyle r_{\textnormal{ts}}

\displaystyle\simeq\frac{8}{\beta^{2}N_{e}^{2}}

(321)

\displaystyle\simeq 32\pi^{2}\beta^{2}\varsigma^{2}\left(\frac{\Lambda_{\textnormal{S}}}{M_{\textnormal{P}}}\right)^{4}

(322)

\displaystyle A_{s}

\displaystyle=\frac{V}{24\pi^{2}\epsilon_{V}}

(323)

\displaystyle\simeq\frac{V_{0}\beta^{2}N_{e}^{2}}{12\pi^{2}},

(324)

where the expression for the scalar powerspectrum can be used with the observed value of
A_{s}\simeq$2.1\text{\times}{10}^{-9}$
to fix
V_{0}
.
Observational constraints on
n_{s}=0.9649
then fix the species scale to

\Lambda_{\textnormal{S}}\simeq\frac{1}{\beta}\sqrt{\frac{2}{\varsigma}}$8.97\text{\times}{10}^{16}\text{\,}\mathrm{GeV}$.

(325)

Observational constraints on
r_{\textnormal{ts}}<0.035
fix the species scale to

\Lambda_{\textnormal{S}}\lesssim$1.74\text{\times}{10}^{17}\text{\,}\mathrm{GeV}$,

(326)

for
\beta=1
and
\varsigma=2
.

Aoki and Otsuka 2024
further generalise these considerations and derivations for CYthreefolds, to fix the species scale with inflationary observables to
${10}^{15}\text{\,}\mathrm{GeV}$<\Lambda_{\textnormal{S}}<${10}^{17}\text{\,}\mathrm{GeV}$
.

More on Chaotic Inflation

Requiring that a BHconsisting of
N_{\textnormal{S}}
heavy species can decay within the lifetime of dSspace,
Dvali and Lust 2008
present a bound on the species mass

m\lesssim\frac{M_{\textnormal{P}}}{\left(M_{\textnormal{P}}/H\right)^{1/3}}.

(327)

This bound applies during inflation, where the species are massive
Dvali and Lust 2008
:
In chaotic inflation, we have a scalar field with
V(\phi)=\frac{1}{2}m^{2}\phi^{2}+g\phi\bar{\psi}_{i}\psi_{i}
, where the last term with coupling constant
g
describes the interaction with the
N_{\textnormal{S}}
species, and the field can reach arbitrarily large values, as long as
m^{2}\phi^{2}\ll M_{\textnormal{P}}^{4}
holds.
A slow-rolling field follows the equation

\ddot{\phi}+3H\dot{\phi}+V^{\prime}(\phi)=0

(328)

with

H^{2}\simeq V(\phi)/3M_{\textnormal{P}}^{2}

(329)

and in the case of chaotic inflation

V^{\prime}(\phi)=m^{2}\phi
and
H^{2}=\frac{m^{2}\phi^{2}+\dot{\phi}^{2}}{6M_{\textnormal{P}}^{2}}
.
During inflation, the species mass is
m=g\phi
, and
eq.

327
implies

g\phi\lesssim\frac{M_{\textnormal{P}}}{\left(M_{\textnormal{P}}^{2}/\left(m\phi\right)\right)^{1/3}},

(330)

which limits the coupling to

g\lesssim\left(\frac{M_{\textnormal{P}}}{\phi}\right)^{2/3}\left(\frac{m}{M_{\textnormal{P}}}\right)^{1/3}.

(331)

For chaotic inflation with 60
e
-foldings,
\phi\lesssim 10M_{\textnormal{P}}
, and
m\sim${10}^{12}\text{\,}\mathrm{GeV}$
(derived from density perturbations), we find
g<${10}^{-3}$
.

Particle Physics

An obvious question is: What kind of additional species should we expect? Observations do not suggest that there are additional neutrino species, yet the observational constraints allow for up to
N_{\textnormal{S}}<\order{30}
for normal ordering and for up to
N_{\textnormal{S}}<\order{100}
for inverted ordering
Ettengruber et al. 2024
. Furthermore, additional Higgs bosons or a zoo of DMparticles can be in agreement with the current observational constraints. There are suggestions that entire copies of the SMcould exist
Ettengruber et al. 2024
;
Dvali and Redi 2009
.
291
291

291

The idea that a huge number of species (
N_{\textnormal{S}}=${10}^{32}$
copies of the SM) could explain the hierarchy problem, i.e. why the EWsector / (squared) Higgs mass are 32 orders of magnitude below the (squared) Planck scale, was put forward early on by
Dvali and Redi 2009
. However, since the LHCdid not show any signs of microscopic BHsat the TeV scale, this solution is ruled out.

II.14.2
General Remarks

The species scale plays an important role for the EPand in tying together the EPand the WGC(see
section

II.20.2
) respectively the EPand the DC(see
section

II.20.3
). Furthermore, there is an interplay between the SSCand the FLB(
section

II.20.1
). In the remainder of this section, we will focus on the motivation behind the SSC.

Why would the number of species play a role regarding the validity of a theory?

The effects of having multiple species become clear by studying a concrete example by
Grimm et al. 2018
. Let us start with the Lagrangian for heavy fermions:

L=\bar{\psi}\partial_{\mu}\gamma^{\mu}\psi+\frac{1}{2}\left(\partial\phi\right)^{2}-m(\phi)\bar{\psi}\psi

(332)

and integrate out
\psi
to obtain a low-energy EFT–metric that only depends on
\phi
:

g_{\phi\phi}=g_{\phi\phi}^{\text{UV}}+\frac{\left(\partial_{\phi}m\right)^{2}}{8\pi^{2}}\log\frac{\Lambda_{\text{UV}}^{2}}{m^{2}},

(333)

where the logarithmic term comes from 1-loop corrections when integrating out the heavy state. In general, higher-order corrections
\propto\left(\partial_{\phi}m\right)^{4}
are expected to be present but subleading and therefore not of relevance to the argument here. Our original theory (
eq.

332
) was no theory of QG. Therefore, it ceases to be valid at
\Lambda_{\text{UV}}
. Now, when do quantum corrections become dominant? Since we are using a perturbative approach, we continue to assume that
\left(\partial_{\phi}m\right)\ll 1
. The limit
m\rightarrow 0
is a divergence, but it is a single-point locus in moduli space up to which the field space distance remains finite. To obtain infinite distances, we actually have to consider a model with multiple particles! We can continue with the fermionic case and study the multi-field metric

g_{\phi\phi}=g_{\phi\phi}^{\text{UV}}+\sum_{i}^{N_{\textnormal{S}}}\frac{\left(\partial_{\phi}m_{i}\right)^{2}}{8\pi^{2}}\log\frac{\Lambda_{\text{UV}}^{2}}{m_{i}^{2}}.

(334)

Now, the quantum part can actually dominate the EFTmetric, when
\sum_{i}^{N_{\textnormal{S}}}\left(\partial_{\phi}m_{i}\right)^{2}\gg g_{\phi\phi}^{\text{UV}}
. The number of states in the theory,
N_{\textnormal{S}}
, is fixed by the upper cutoff scale
\Lambda_{\text{UV}}
. Above this scale, gravity becomes strongly coupled, i.e.
\Lambda_{\text{UV}}\sim\Lambda_{\text{S}}
.
This shows that the number of states is of relevance when assessing the validity of a theory.
To quantify this further, we can use that there is also a lower cutoff,
\Lambda_{0}\sim m_{0}
, where the lightest massive state is being integrated out. Our tower of states will have a mass separation scale
\Delta m
, such that
m_{k}(\phi)=m_{0}(\phi)+k\Delta m(\phi)
.

If

N_{\textnormal{S}}\Delta m\gtrsim m_{0}
, then

\displaystyle\Lambda_{\text{S}}

\displaystyle\simeq\left(M_{\textnormal{P}}^{2}\Delta m\right)^{\frac{1}{3}}

(335)

\displaystyle N_{\textnormal{S}}

\displaystyle\simeq\left(\frac{M_{\textnormal{P}}}{\Delta m}\right)^{\frac{2}{3}}

(336)

\displaystyle\Rightarrow\Lambda_{\textnormal{S}}

\displaystyle=\frac{M_{\textnormal{P}}}{N_{\textnormal{S}}^{\frac{1}{2}}}.

(337)

Equation

276
is the
d
-dimensional generalisation
Grimm et al. 2018
.

What happens if the species scale is violated?

Generally, an EFTbreaks down before reaching the Planck scale because the Planck mass of the higher-dimensional theory,
M_{\textnormal{P};D}\sim R^{\frac{1}{2-D}}
, is smaller than
M_{\textnormal{P};d}
: gravity
feels
292
292

292

Gravity cannot be localised on a submanifold of codimension zero, therefore, on a 4D spacetime embedded in a higher dimensional space with additional, curled-up, dimensions, gravity seems to
leak out
of the 4D EFT
De Biasio 2023
. Given a
\left(D-d\right)
-dimensional internal subspace of volume
V
, we find that
M_{\textnormal{P};d}^{2}=M_{\textnormal{P};D}^{2+D-d}V

Vafa 2024a
.

the extra dimensions, and is diluted, i.e. quantum gravitational effects become important earlier than expected
Palti 2019
.
This corresponds to the initial interpretation or definition by
Dvali and Redi 2008
: They conjectured that
M_{\textnormal{P}}^{2}\gtrsim N_{\textnormal{S}}\Lambda_{\textnormal{S}}^{2}
, such that a large number of species weakens gravity.
293
293

293

The motivation for this comes directly from the derivation
Dvali and Redi 2008
: each of the
N_{\textnormal{S}}
species contributes
\Lambda_{\textnormal{S}}^{2}
to the normalisation of the graviton wave function, which results in an effective contribution to
M_{\textnormal{P}}
of
N_{\textnormal{S}}\Lambda_{\textnormal{S}}^{2}
.

A concrete example of a violation of the SSCis presented by
Kaplan and Kundu 2019
:
If the species scale in a 4d large
N_{\textnormal{S}}
confining gauge theory with glueballs and mesons of spin
J>2
is violated, i.e. if
N_{\textnormal{S}}>\frac{M_{\textnormal{P}}}{\Lambda_{\text{QCD}}}
, causality is violated and gravity might be stronger than the gauge forces between the hadrons, which would also violate the WGC.

Other implications of a SSCviolation are that
BHscould become larger than the dShorizon, and that
BHscould have hair.
294
294

294

If the entropy of a BHwas smaller than then species entropy, we knew that not all species were part of this particular BH, ergo we had information about its constituents we wouldn’t have otherwise, i.e. the no-hair theorem was violated.

How can the species bound be derived?

Equation

276
arises from perturbative and non-perturbative considerations
Cribiori and Lust 2023
;
Cribiori et al. 2023b
;
Dvali and Redi 2008
:
Perturbatively, the species scale arises from loop corrections to the graviton operator.
When the first-order corrections are comparable to the tree-level term, respectively the inverse propagator becomes divergent,
the perturbation breaks down, which happens at the species scale.
Alternatively, one can study the EFTaction: higher curvature corrections become relevant around the species scale, as they control the
R^{2}
term
Cribiori et al. 2023b
, cf.
Calmet et al. 2024
.
Non-perturbatively, one can study the entropy of BHs:
The smallest BHneeds to be able to emit or absorb all
N_{\textnormal{S}}
species of the theory, i.e. the entropy of the BHshould correspond to the number of species in the theory:

\mathcal{S}_{\textnormal{S}}\simeq\mathcal{S}_{\heartsuit}\simeq N_{\textnormal{S}}.

(338)

The smallest BHhas a radius of
r_{\heartsuit}\simeq 1/\Lambda_{\textnormal{S}}
, and the Bekenstein–Hawking area law yields
\mathcal{S}_{\textnormal{S}}\simeq r_{\heartsuit}^{d-2}

Cribiori et al. 2023b
.
After this executive summary, we now present the three approaches in more detail.

Perturbative Arguments

The species scale can be interpreted as the scale that suppresses higher-derivative corrections to the EFT
Calderón-Infante et al. 2024
.
That the perturbation breaks down for higher energies can be seen at the example of the graviton propagator
Castellano 2024
:
The resummed 1-loop propagator of the graviton in Lorentzian signature in a flat, 4d background is given by

i\Pi^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}=i\left(P^{\mu\rho}P^{\nu\sigma}+P^{\mu\sigma}P^{\nu\rho}-P^{\mu\nu}P^{\rho\sigma}\right)\pi(p^{2})

(339)

with
P^{\mu\nu}=\eta^{\mu\nu}-\frac{p^{\mu}p^{\nu}}{p^{2}}
satisfying
P^{\rho}_{\sigma}P^{\sigma}_{\kappa}=P^{\rho}_{\kappa}
a projection operator onto polarisation states transverse to
p^{\sigma}
,

p
the momentum of the external graviton state,

\mu
an energy scale related to the renormalisation of
R^{2}
and
R_{\mu\nu}R^{\mu\nu}
curvature terms
Aydemir et al. 2012
;
Castellano et al. 2023a
,
and

\pi^{-1}(p^{2})=2p^{2}\left(1-\frac{N_{0}p^{2}}{120\pi M_{\textnormal{P};4}^{2}}\log(-p^2/\mu^2)\right),

(340)

with

N_{0}=N_{\textnormal{s}}/3+N_{\textnormal{W}}+4N_{\textnormal{v}}

(341)

the weighted sum of light degrees of freedom (scalars, Weyl spinors, and vectors in 4 dimensions),
such that the perturbation expansion breaks down when the second term in the bracket is comparable to
1
.
This happens at the energy scale

\Lambda_{\textnormal{S}}

Cribiori et al. 2023b
;
Castellano et al. 2023a
;
Aydemir et al. 2012
:

\frac{M_{\textnormal{P}}^{d-2}}{N_{0}}\simeq\Lambda_{\textnormal{S}}^{d-2}\log\left(\Lambda_{\textnormal{S}}/\mu\right).

(342)

A solution to this equation can be found by using the (-1)-branch of the Lambert
W
function
Cribiori et al. 2023b
;
Castellano et al. 2023a
;
Corless et al. 1996
:

\displaystyle\Lambda_{\textnormal{S}}^{d-2}

\displaystyle\simeq\frac{-\left(d-2\right)M_{\textnormal{P}}^{d-2}}{N_{0}W_{-1}\left(-\frac{d-2}{N_{0}}\left(\frac{M_{\textnormal{P}}}{\mu}\right)^{d-2}\right)}

(343)

\displaystyle\simeq\frac{M_{\textnormal{P}}^{d-2}}{N_{0}\log N_{0}}

(344)

\displaystyle\simeq\frac{M_{\textnormal{P}}^{d-2}}{\log N_{0}!},

(345)

where the second last expansion is a large
N_{0}
approximation, which yields, using Stirling’s formula, the last equation.
Note that it is a weighted sum over the number of massless states
N_{0}
that appears in the equations, not the number of species
N_{\textnormal{S}}
itself. This allows us to define the species entropy as

\mathcal{S}_{\textnormal{S}}\simeq\log N_{0}!,

(346)

paving the way for a bridge to entropic arguments
Cribiori et al. 2023b
.

The appearance and relevance of logarithmic corrections is subject to ongoing debate:
On the one hand,
the logarithmic corrections appear whenever starting from 1-loop corrections to graviton propagators
Dvali and Redi 2008
;
Basile et al. 2024b
;
Blumenhagen et al. 2024a
;
Cribiori et al. 2023b
;
Castellano et al. 2023a
;
Aydemir et al. 2012
;
Castellano 2024
, and

Cribiori and Lust 2023
show that the logarithmic corrections arise from requiring that the species scale is modular invariant.
On the other hand,
there are arguments that the logarithmic corrections are to be ignored:

•

logarithmic corrections do not appear in the BHpicture
Blumenhagen et al. 2024a
;
Cribiori et al. 2023b
;
Blumenhagen et al. 2023d
;
Agmon et al. 2023
;
van de Heisteeg et al. 2023b
,

•

logarithmic corrections only arise, when the QFTis extended to energies above the string scale — a regime where the approach is no longer fully reliable
Agmon et al. 2023
;
van de Heisteeg et al. 2023b
;
Blumenhagen et al. 2023d
,

•

logarithmic corrections only arise in the purely massless case, and do not appear with massive species
Basile et al. 2024b
;
van de Heisteeg et al. 2024a
.

At this point, we would like to refer to our
tale of scales
295
295

295

See
section

II.6.2
and venture the guess that the logarithmic terms are part of the emergence scale, but not necessarily of the species scale. The confusion arises from not distinguishing between the two scales.

Entropic Arguments

To show that pathologies are only avoided if the SSCholds, we can study a large
N_{\textnormal{S}}
Quantum Chromo Dynamics (QCD)theory in a flat 4d spacetime region of radius
l
and temperature
T
, with

l=\frac{1}{4\pi T},

(347)

which is the Hawking temperature of a BHof radius
l

Kaplan and Kundu 2019
.
Dimensional analysis tells us that the entropy of such a region is given by

\mathcal{S}\sim l^{3}T^{3}N_{\textnormal{S}}^{2},

(348)

which is supported by finite temperature lattice computations above the critical deconfinement temperature
Osborn and Petkos 1994
.
For

l\sim\frac{N_{\textnormal{S}}}{M_{\textnormal{P}}}

(349)

the spherical region approaches the entropy of a BH

\mathcal{S}=8\pi^{2}l^{2}M_{\textnormal{P}}^{2},

(350)

but for large
N_{\textnormal{S}}
and
l<N_{\textnormal{S}}/M_{\textnormal{P}}
, the entropy of the spherical region would be larger than the entropy of a BHof the same size, which can only be avoided if

N_{\textnormal{S}}\lesssim\frac{M_{\textnormal{P}}}{\Lambda_{\textnormal{S}}},

(351)

i.e. the species bound holds
Kaplan and Kundu 2019
.

Given a thermodynamic system of size
l
,
Herráez et al. 2024
show that there are three different regimes with regard to the temperature
T
of the system and the species scale:

l^{-1}<T<\Lambda_{\text{S}}

If gravitational collapse is avoided and the CEBis not violated, the entropy scales with the volume,
\mathcal{S}\sim l^{3}
.

l^{-1}\simeq T<\Lambda_{\text{S}}

If gravitational collapse is avoided and the CEBis not violated, the system transitions from one regime to the other, while the entropy is given by counting the species contributing to the thermodynamic ensemble.

l^{-1}\simeq T\rightarrow\Lambda_{\text{S}}

The rules of species thermodynamics apply, and the entropy scales like the area of the box. In the limit
l^{-1}\simeq T\simeq\Lambda_{\textnormal{S}}
, the CEBis saturated, the system should collapse gravitationally, and
\mathcal{S}\rightarrow N_{\textnormal{S}}

Castellano et al. 2022
;
Herráez et al. 2024
.

Interesting from a swampland perspective is, of course, the last case, where we are approaching limiting energy (densities).
In analogy to the laws of BHthermodynamics, the laws of species thermodynamics can be formulated by noting that time derivatives can be expressed in terms of changes in the scalar field
296
296

296

In the swampland we mostly consider adiabatic motion of a scalar field through moduli space, and we can, for time-dependent string backgrounds, identify the scalar field with time, i.e.
\phi=t
.

Cribiori et al. 2023b
:

0
th
law of species thermodynamics

\forall\phi_{0},\phi_{1},\phi_{2}\in\mathcal{M}
s.t.
T_{\textnormal{S}}(\phi_{1})=T_{\textnormal{S}}(\phi_{0})
and
T_{\textnormal{S}}(\phi_{2})=T_{\textnormal{S}}(\phi_{0})
, it follows that
T_{\textnormal{S}}(\phi_{1})=T_{\textnormal{S}}(\phi_{2})
.

1
st
law of species thermodynamics

Two neighbouring stationary towers are related to each other by
\delta E_{\textnormal{S}}=T_{\textnormal{S}}\delta\mathcal{S}_{\textnormal{S}}+V_{e}\delta q+\dots
, with
V_{e}
the electrostatic potential. While the entropy takes the place of the horizon area, the temperature, which is linearly related to the species scale
\Lambda_{\textnormal{S}}
, takes the place of the surface gravity.

2
nd
law of species thermodynamics

The species entropy is a non-decreasing function of scalar field displacement towards the boundary of moduli space.
\forall\phi_{1},\phi_{2}\in\mathcal{M}
s.t.
\min_{\phi\in\partial\mathcal{M}}\Delta(\phi_{1},\phi)>\min_{\phi\in\partial\mathcal{M}}\Delta(\phi_{2},\phi)
, we have
\Lambda_{\textnormal{S}}(\phi_{2})<\Lambda_{\textnormal{S}}(\phi_{1})
,
\mathcal{S}_{\textnormal{S}}(\phi_{2})>\mathcal{S}_{\textnormal{S}}(\phi_{1})
, and
\delta N_{\textnormal{S}}\geq 0
, i.e. the number of species must not decrease,
297
297

297

This finding can be expressed in simple and obvious terms: The number of species below temperature
T
must not decrease with an increase in temperature
Herráez et al. 2024
.

which is aligned with other swampland conjectures such as the DC
Herráez et al. 2024
;
Cribiori et al. 2023b
. It is also aligned with the EP: for KKtowers, we go in the direction of compactification, such that the volume increases (
\delta\mathcal{V}\geq 0
); and for string towers, we go into the direction of the tensionless string, such that the coupling strength decreases (
\delta g_{s}\leq 0
). Using the AdSDC, we find that the cosmological constant can only decrease (
\delta\Lambda_{\text{cc}}\leq 0
).

3
rd
law of species thermodynamics

The boundary of moduli space
\partial\mathcal{M}
cannot be reached by a finite number of steps, i.e. it is at infinite distance. No physical process can reduce the species temperature
T_{\textnormal{S}}
to zero by a finite number of operations. The limit of a vanishing cosmological constant cannot be reached by a finite sequence of operations.

BHArguments

In a semi-classical approximation, the decay rate of a BHis given by

\frac{\mathrm{d}m_{\text{BH}}}{\mathrm{d}t}\approx-N_{\textnormal{S}}T^{2}_{\text{H}};

(352)

using that the Hawking temperature scales like
T_{\text{H}}\sim 1/m

together with
eqs.

288
,
281
,
283
,
282
and
286

yields a lifetime of

\displaystyle\tau

\displaystyle\sim\frac{1}{N_{\textnormal{S}}}\int_{0}^{1/\Lambda_{\textnormal{S}}}\!\frac{1}{T_{\text{H}}^{2}}\mathrm{d}m

(353)

\displaystyle\sim\frac{1}{m_{\heartsuit}^{2}}\int_{0}^{1/\Lambda_{\textnormal{S}}}\!m^{2}\mathrm{d}m

(354)

\displaystyle\sim m_{\heartsuit}

(355)

\displaystyle\sim\frac{1}{T_{\text{H}}}

(356)

which shows that the semi-classical description breaks down if we allow for smaller BHsthan allowed by the SSC, as then the lifetime of such a BHwould be longer than the timescale over which the Hawking temperature is approximately constant,
1/T_{\text{H}}

Dvali and Redi 2008
;
Castellano 2024
;
Dvali and Gomez 2009b
;
Castellano et al. 2023a
;
Dvali and Solodukhin 2008
.
298
298

298

We worked in 4 dimensions and Planck units to shorten the notation. The general expression is
\tau\sim\frac{1}{N_{\textnormal{S}}}\int_{0}^{M_{\textnormal{P};d}^{d-2}/\Lambda^{d-3}}\!\left(\frac{m}{M_{\textnormal{P};d}^{d-2}}\right)^{\frac{2}{d-3}}\,\mathrm{d}m\sim 1/\Lambda
as
T_{\text{H}}=1/r\sim\left(M_{\textnormal{P};d}^{d-2}/m\right)^{1/\left(d-3\right)}\sim\Lambda
.

The occurrence of the species scale can also be understood by the following considerations presented by
van de Heisteeg et al. 2023b
:
In the Einstein–Hilbert action, higher-order curvature terms are suppressed by additional powers of
M_{\textnormal{P}}
. If
R\sim M_{\textnormal{P}}^{2}
, the higher-order terms are of
\order{1}
and the EFTbreaks down.
The size of the smallest BHcan be derived from entropy considerations: it would be too naive to assume that the smallest BHhas
r_{\heartsuit}\sim 1/M_{\textnormal{P}}
, as even the smallest BHcan carry all states of the theory, i.e.
\mathcal{S}_{\heartsuit}\geq\mathcal{S}_{\textnormal{S}}=\Lambda_{\textnormal{S}}^{2-d}M_{\textnormal{P}}^{d-2}
and
r_{\heartsuit}\geq l_{\textnormal{S}}=1/\Lambda_{\textnormal{S}}

Cribiori et al. 2023b
,
with
\Lambda_{\textnormal{S}}=M_{\textnormal{P}}/N_{\textnormal{S}}^{\frac{1}{d-2}}
the suppression factor for curvature terms in the EFT.

Furthermore, at least the smallest BHmust fit into the dShorizon:
Given a scalar field
\phi
with potential
V(\phi)
in an approximately flat (quasi-)dSspace with Hubble radius

r_{H}\sim\frac{1}{H}=\frac{M_{\textnormal{P}}^{\left(d-2\right)/2}}{\sqrt{V}},

(357)

the Gibbons–Hawking entropy

\mathcal{S}_{\text{dS}}\sim\left(r_{H}M_{\textnormal{P}}\right)^{d-2}

(358)

is certainly larger than the number of light states
N_{\textnormal{S}}

van de Heisteeg et al. 2023a
.
Using the species scale (
eq.

276
), we find that

\frac{\sqrt{V}}{M_{\textnormal{P}}^{\left(d-2\right)/2}}\leq\Lambda_{\textnormal{S}}=\frac{1}{r_{\text{min}}},

(359)

i.e. the smallest BHfits into the dShorizon
van de Heisteeg et al. 2023a
.
299
299

299

Curvature terms in an EFTare
V\sim R\lesssim\Lambda_{\textnormal{S}}^{2}
; using
eq.

297
then yields a bound on the field range for flat potentials:
\Delta\phi\lesssim\sqrt{\left(d-1\right)\left(d-2\right)}\log\left(1/V_{0}\right)

Vafa 2025
.

Is the species scale a constant?

When moving through moduli space, the number of species, and therewith the species scale, changes.
300
300

300

For example, the number of light species is higher in the early Universe than it is today.

The change of the species scale can be quantified and happens within bounds.
Upper bounds of the form

\absolutevalue{\frac{\nabla\Lambda_\textnormal{S}}{\Lambda_\textnormal{S}}}^{2}<\frac{\mathfrak{l}}{M_{\textnormal{P}}^{d-2}},

(360)

with
\mathfrak{l}>\frac{1}{d-2}
an
\order{1}
parameter
301
301

301

van de Heisteeg et al. 2024a
find
\mathfrak{l}=1
except in a 4d
\mathcal{N}=2
theory arising from a type II compactification on CY
3
and a
d=8
maximal supergravity setting, where corrections to the
R^{2}
respectively
R^{4}
setting force the slope to approach the asymptotic value from above.

that depends on
d
,
and
\absolutevalue{\nabla\Lambda_\textnormal{S}}^{2}=G^{ij}\partial_{\phi_{i}}\Lambda_{\textnormal{S}}\partial_{\phi_{j}}\Lambda_{\textnormal{S}}
,

G^{ij}
the inverse moduli field space metric,
hold everywhere in moduli space
Scalisi 2024
;
Cribiori and Lust 2023
;
van de Heisteeg et al. 2023b
;
Cribiori et al. 2023b

and mean
that integrating out the short-distance modes of moduli does not change the obtained EFTany further when the massive states are already integrated out, since the massive states always dominate the short-distance modes of other fields, in particular the modes of massless scalar fields, as a single massless field cannot account for the entropy of the smallest BH
van de Heisteeg et al. 2023b
.
An upper bound indicates that finite scalar field variations must be allowed
Scalisi 2024
,
but also that the cosmological constant experiences an exponential suppression
Cribiori and Lust 2023
.

Andriot 2023
shows that
\nabla\Lambda_{\textnormal{S}}/\Lambda_{\textnormal{S}}
does not grow monotonically, but has a maximum before it approaches its asymptotic value (which lies below the maximum).
The origin of the bump is not entirely clear and presumably model dependent;
Andriot 2023
finds that axions do not generate such a bump,
nor do the exponential potentials of dilatonic and volume factors,
but saxionic exponential potentials do.
The maximum point of
\Lambda_{\textnormal{S}}
— the desert point
Long et al. 2021
or centre of moduli space
van de Heisteeg et al. 2024b
— gives an estimate of the smallest number of light states that a QGtheory can have
van de Heisteeg et al. 2024a
.

A lower bound of the form

\absolutevalue{\frac{\Lambda_\textnormal{S}^\prime(\phi)}{\Lambda_\textnormal{S}(\phi)}}\geq\frac{1}{\sqrt{\left(d-1\right)\left(d-2\right)}},

(361)

is derived by

Calderón-Infante et al. 2023a
,
which represents a connection to the DC, as a vanishing value corresponds to an infinite field distance, which is forbidden according to the DC
Scalisi 2024
.

Furthermore, an equality of the form

\frac{\gradient m}{m}\frac{\gradient\Lambda_{\textnormal{S}}}{\Lambda_{\textnormal{S}}}=\frac{M_{\textnormal{P};d}^{d-2}}{d-2},

(362)

is derived by

Castellano et al. 2023b
;
Castellano et al. 2023c
;
Castellano 2024
,
where the nabla operator indicates that they have multiple species in mind.
By using
\Lambda_{\textnormal{S}}=M_{\textnormal{P};d}/N_{\textnormal{S}}^{1/\left(d-2\right)}
,
eq.

362
can be written in a form that does not explicitly depend on the number of large spacetime dimensions
Castellano 2024
:

\frac{\gradient m}{m}\frac{\gradient N_{\textnormal{S}}}{N_{\textnormal{S}}}=-M_{\textnormal{P};d}^{2-d}.

(363)

This form constrains the density variation of states below the species scale and the rate at which they are becoming light: the denser the spectrum becomes, the faster the species scale vanishes
Castellano 2024
.

How can the species scale be understood if there are multiple towers?

An individual tower has a spectrum of

m_{n}=n^{1/p}m_{\textnormal{t}},

(364)

with
m_{\textnormal{t}}
the mass of the lightest state,

n
representing the step of the state within the tower,
and
p
representing the number of towers with identical mass gap
Seo 2024b
;
Castellano et al. 2022
.
There can be multiple KKtowers, but only one fundamental string
Castellano et al. 2023a
;
Castellano et al. 2022
.
For a string tower, we find

\displaystyle m_{\textnormal{t}}

\displaystyle=m_{\textnormal{s}}=1/l_{\textnormal{s}}

(365)

\displaystyle m_{n}

\displaystyle\sim\sqrt{n}m_{\textnormal{s}}

(366)

\displaystyle p

\displaystyle\rightarrow\infty;

(367)

m_{n}
are
Regge modes
with oscillator number
n

Dvali and Lust 2009
;
Seo 2024b
.
There is an upper bound on the mass scale of towers,

m\lesssim\Lambda_{\text{IR}}^{2\lambda_{d}}M_{\textnormal{P};d}^{1-2\lambda_{d}},

(368)

coming from the size of the universe,
1/\Lambda_{\text{IR}}
, as the lowest energy that can occur must correspond to a wavelength that fits into the universe
Castellano et al. 2023a
;
Castellano et al. 2022
.
In general,
\lambda_{d}=\left(d-2+p\right)/\left[2p\left(d-1\right)\right]
, but for a single KKtower,
Castellano et al. 2022
find, using the CEB, that in dSand AdSspace
\lambda\geq 1/2
, which corresponds to the AdSDC(
eq.

1
).
For multiple towers or stringy towers, they find
\lambda\geq 1/d
; a lower value would lead to gravitational collapse.

If there are multiple towers, we have

\Lambda_{\textnormal{S},i}\simeq N_{i}^{1/p_{i}}m_{i}\simeq\frac{M_{\textnormal{P};d}}{N_{i}^{\frac{1}{d-2}}},

(369)

with
N_{i}
the number of states of the
i
-th tower
Castellano et al. 2023a
;
Castellano 2024
.
If there is no mixing between the states, i.e. the fields are independent and there are no states with mixed charges,
we find

\Lambda_{\textnormal{S}}=\min\{\Lambda_{\textnormal{S},i}\}

Castellano et al. 2023a
;
Castellano 2024
. This means that the species scale is dominated by the tower with the lightest mass scale
Castellano et al. 2023a
. The total number of states is
N_{\text{tot}}=\sum_{i}N_{i}.

If the towers mix, the leading tower alone is insufficient to give the species scale, as heavier towers can still contribute with a diverging number of states, and we find
Castellano 2024
;
Castellano et al. 2023a
:

\displaystyle p_{\text{eff}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i}p_{i}

(370)

\displaystyle m_{\text{eff}}

\displaystyle=\left(\prod_{i}m_{i}^{p_{i}}\right)^{1/p_{\text{eff}}}

(371)

\displaystyle N_{\textnormal{S}}

\displaystyle\simeq\prod_{i}N_{i}

(372)

\displaystyle\simeq\left(\frac{M_{\textnormal{P};d}}{m_{\text{eff}}}\right)^{\frac{\left(d-2\right)p_{\text{eff}}}{d-2+p_{\text{eff}}}}

(373)

\displaystyle\Lambda_{\textnormal{S}}

\displaystyle\simeq M_{\textnormal{P};d}\left(\frac{M_{\textnormal{P};d}}{m_{\text{eff}}}\right)^{\frac{-p_{\text{eff}}}{d-2+p_{\text{eff}}}}.

(374)

Which towers have to be included can be determined by an iterative process
Castellano 2024
:
You start with the lightest tower and determine its species scale.
If the second lightest towers starts below this scale, the second-lightest tower has to be included in the calculation of the species scale.
Then, you compare the mass scale of the third-lightest tower to this newly obtained species scale, and if this mass scale is below the species scale based on the two lightest tower, the third tower has to be included as well. And so forth.
In short: if
m_{i}\geq m_{i+1}^{\left(d-2+p_{\text{eff},i}\right)/p_{\text{eff},i}}
,
the
i+1
-th tower has to be included.

In
section

II.19.2
, we will encounter the convex hull conjecture of the WGC. A conceptually similar conjecture is formulated for the SSC:
‘The convex hull of species vectors
\{\vec{\mathcal{Z}}\}
defined at infinity should contain the ball of radius
\lambda_{\text{S,min}}=1/\sqrt{\left(d-1\right)\left(d-2\right)}
’
with the species vector given by
\mathcal{Z}^{a}=-\delta^{ab}e_{b}^{i}\partial_{i}\log\Lambda_{\textnormal{S}}
with
e_{i}^{a}(\phi)
a vielbein in field space that satisfies
\delta_{ab}e_{i}^{a}(\phi)e_{j}^{b}(\phi)=G_{ij}(\phi)

Castellano 2024
.
The species vector for KKmodes is given by
\mathcal{Z}_{\text{KK},n}=\sqrt{n/\left[\left(d+n-2\right)\left(d-2\right)\right]}
,
whereas the species vector for string oscillator modes is given by
\mathcal{Z}_{\textnormal{s}}=1/\sqrt{d-2}

Castellano 2024
.
A relation to the mass can be obtained by defining the scalar charge-to-mass vector
\zeta^{i}=-\partial^{i}\log m
and
\zeta^{a}=e_{i}^{a}\zeta^{i}
, which is
\zeta_{\text{KK},n}=\sqrt{\left(d+n-2\right)/\left[n\left(d-2\right)\right]}
for the KKmodes,
respectively
\zeta_{\textnormal{s}}=1/\sqrt{d-2}
for the string oscillator modes
Castellano 2024
.

How are charged towers to be treated?

When studying the stability and abundance of species, we have to differentiate between two types of species (charged and neutral) and we have to consider two different phases (before and after the first half-life).
Charged species have a lower temperature than their neutral counterparts, as
T_{\text{S,c}}=\Lambda_{\textnormal{S}}^{2}
but
T_{\text{S,n}}=\Lambda_{\textnormal{S}}

Basile et al. 2024b
.
During the first half-life, species undergo thermal decay with

\Gamma_{\textnormal{S},\text{th}}\simeq T_{\textnormal{u}}>T_{\textnormal{u}}^{2}\simeq H,

(375)

where we introduced the temperature of the universe
T_{\textnormal{u}}
and used the finding that in the early, radiation dominated universe, we have, according to Stefan’s law, an energy density
\rho\sim T_{\textnormal{u}}^{4}
and a scale factor
a\sim T_{\textnormal{u}}^{-1}
, such that
H\simeq T_{\textnormal{u}}^{2}

Basile et al. 2024b
.
After the first half-life, the species is decaying quantum-mechanically, with

\Gamma_{\textnormal{S},\text{qm}}\simeq T_{\textnormal{u}}^{3}<T_{\textnormal{u}}^{2}\simeq H,

(376)

where the species decouple and the expansion of the universe happens in a background with constant UVcutoff scale
\Lambda_{\textnormal{S}}

Basile et al. 2024b
.
This indicates that charged species are more stable than neutral ones.
Furthermore, we can use this to check for the compatibility of other proposals, such as the microscopic dark dimension proposal presented in
section

II.1.1
:
The proposal predicts a tower of
m_{\text{KK}}\simeq\Lambda_{\text{cc}}^{1/4}\simeq$1\text{\,}\mathrm{eV}$
.
Our discussion suggests that the corresponding species scale is

\Lambda_{\textnormal{S}}\simeq m_{\text{KK}}\simeq\Lambda_{\text{cc}}^{1/4},

(377)

ergo a decoupling temperature of

\displaystyle T_{\textnormal{S}}

\displaystyle=\left(\left(\Lambda_{\text{cc}}^{1/4}\right)^{\beta}\right)^{1/3}

\displaystyle=\Lambda_{\text{cc}}^{\beta/12}

(378)

where
\beta=1
(
\beta=2
) correspond to the neutral (charged) case, and we took the third root because we are interested in the decoupled behaviour.
The charged case with
T_{\text{S,c}}=\Lambda_{\text{cc}}^{1/6}\simeq$1\text{\,}\mathrm{GeV}$
corresponds to the prediction from
Gonzalo et al. 2022a
that the initial temperature for the cosmology with the correct DMabundance and 4 stable large dimensions in a dark dimension scenario is
T=\Lambda_{\text{cc}}^{1/6}\simeq$1\text{\,}\mathrm{GeV}$

Basile et al. 2024b
.

II.15
Tadpole Conjecture

The flux contributions to the D3-tadpole
303
303

303

A tadpole is a 1-loop Feynman diagram with only one external leg
Evans 2018
. If the tadpoles do not cancel, the vacuum respectively the background is not stable and does not solve the EoM
Kitazawa 2008
.
According to
Coleman 2013
, the alternative name
spermion
was rejected by the editors of physical review.

that stabilise
n_{\text{mod}}=h^{3,1}
complex-structure moduli of a CYfourfold in a smooth F-theory compactification
respectively
n_{\text{mod}}=2\left(h^{2,1}+1\right)
complex-structure moduli of a CYthreefold in a smooth type IIB string theory compactification
grow at least linearly with
n_{\text{mod}}
, such that

q_{D3}^{\text{stabilisation}}>\mathfrak{t}n_{\text{mod}},

(379)

for large

with
\mathfrak{t}
the flux-tadpole constant
Bena et al. 2021b
;
Plauschinn 2022

The strong form of the conjecture assumes that
\mathfrak{t}>1/3

Bena et al. 2021b
.
304
304

304

would suffice.

II.15.1
Implications for Cosmology

While no direct implications for cosmology are discussed in the literature, the TPCacts as supporting evidence for other swampland conjectures, as we note in
sections

II.20.31
,
II.20.32
,
II.20.30
and
II.20.29
.

II.15.2
General Remarks

Compactification gives rise to (unphysical) massless scalar fields, e.g. Kähler moduli or complex-structure moduli
Bena et al. 2021c
.
If there was a mechanism to give those moduli mass, it would explain why we do not observe them — such a mechanism is called moduli stabilisation
Prieto 2024
.
Flux can stabilise moduli
Van Riet and Zoccarato 2023
;
Bena et al. 2021c
:
the moduli become massive, if topologically non-trivial magnetic fluxes along the cycles of the internal geometry are turned on.
The flux contributes to the tadpole as
\int H_{3}\wedge F_{3}=q_{\text{flux}}=q_{\text{O3}}-q_{\text{D3}}

Van Riet and Zoccarato 2023
respectively
q_{\text{flux}}+q_{\text{D3}}=6\left(8+h^{1,1}+h^{3,1}-h^{2,1}\right)
in F-theory, where for a large number of moduli, the
h^{3,1}
-term dominates
Prieto 2024
.
Tadpole charges have to be negative
Plauschinn 2021
: empirically, settings with positive tadpole charges show runaway behaviour.
The electric brane charges have to sum up to zero on a compact manifold, i.e. the brane–tadpole cancellation condition places an upper bound on the number of (integer quantised) fluxes
Bena et al. 2021c
;
Lüst and Wiesner 2022
;
Van Riet and Zoccarato 2023
;
Lüst 2021
.
According to the TPC, the fluxes needed to stabilise the moduli carry a charge proportional to the number of stabilised moduli, if the number of moduli is large.
This presents a tension to settings with a large number of moduli, i.e. there will always remain flat directions / moduli that are not stabilised
Prieto 2024
.
305
305

305

This agrees with the
Massless Minkowski Conjecture
presented by
Andriot et al. 2022a
;
Andriot et al. 2022b
, which states that
‘10d supergravity solutions compactified to 4d Minkowski always admit a 4d massless scalar’
. A massless scalar field often — though not only — appears along a flat direction
Andriot et al. 2022b
.

Counterexamples to the TPC, where the flux contribution to the tadpole does not scale with the number of moduli, i.e.
q_{D3}^{\text{stabilisation}}
is independent of
h^{3,1}
, are presented by
Prieto 2024
and
Marchesano et al. 2021b
, and debunked by
Lüst 2021
and
Grimm et al. 2022b
.
However, it has been noted that spaces containing singularities might violate the TPC
Bena et al. 2021b
;
Gao et al. 2022
.

and studied in the context of

,
type IIB and F-theory
Tsagkaris and Plauschinn 2023
;

;

;
Becker et al. 2022
;
Gao et al. 2022
;
Marchesano et al. 2021b
;

;
Coudarchet et al. 2023
;
Grimm et al. 2022b
;
Graña et al. 2022
;
Braun et al. 2023
;
Junghans 2022
,
the
1^{9}
Landau–Ginzburg model
Becker et al. 2024a
,
the
2^{6}
Landau–Ginzburg model
Rajaguru et al. 2024
,
the
2^{6}
Gepner model
Becker et al. 2024b
, and
higher-order effects
Smith et al. 2024
.
Furthermore, relations to other swampland conjectures are highlighted in

II.20.31
,

and
