{\ddagger}=\lambda_{1}^{4}\Lambda_{1}^{2}\left(\frac{2}{3}\frac{\lambda_{1}^{2}}{f^{2}}-\frac{1}{M_{\textnormal{P}}^{2}}\right)e^{-2\lambda_{1}\phi_{0}/f}+\lambda_{2}^{4}\Lambda_{2}^{2}\left(\frac{2}{3}\frac{\lambda_{2}^{2}}{f^{2}}-\frac{1}{M_{\textnormal{P}}^{2}}\right)e^{-2\lambda_{2}\phi_{0}/f}+\Lambda_{1}\Lambda_{2}\lambda_{1}^{2}\lambda_{2}^{2}\left(\frac{10/3\lambda_{1}\lambda_{2}-\lambda_{1}^{2}-\lambda_{2}^{2}}{f^{2}}-\frac{2}{M_{\textnormal{P}}}\right)e^{-\left(\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2}\right)\phi_{0}/f}\geq 0
.

A generalisation of the scalar WGCto axions (that guarantees that the gauge repulsion exceeds the combined gravitational and scalar attraction) is presented by

Vittmann 2023
:

S_{\iota}^{2}+M_{\textnormal{P}}^{2}\partial_{\phi}S_{\iota}\bar{\partial}_{\phi}S_{\iota}\leq M_{\textnormal{P}}^{2}/f^{2},

(521)

which is derived from the more general statement

Q^{2}\geq S_{\iota}^{2}+G^{ab}\nabla_{a}S_{\iota}\bar{\nabla}_{b}S_{\iota},

(522)

with
G^{ab}
the moduli space metric.
417
417

417

Since the term with the derivatives is positive definite, a lower bound for the axion decay constant can be derived:

f<M_{\textnormal{P}}/\sqrt{1+\absolutevalue{\partial_\phi S_\iota}^{2}M_{\textnormal{P}}^{2}}\leq M_{\textnormal{P}}
.

Strong Scalar WGC

Gonzalo and Ibáñez 2019
present the
strong scalar WGC
, which applies to all scalars and implies that every scalar interaction has to be stronger than the gravitational interaction:

\frac{1}{M_{\textnormal{P}}^{2}}\left(\frac{\mathrm{d}^{2}V}{\mathrm{d}\phi^{2}}\right)^{2}\leq 2\left(\frac{\mathrm{d}^{3}V}{\mathrm{d}\phi^{3}}\right)^{2}-\frac{\mathrm{d}^{2}V}{\mathrm{d}\phi^{2}}\frac{\mathrm{d}^{4}V}{\mathrm{d}\phi^{4}},

(523)

and that there is a tower of extremal states that satisfies the DC.

Equation

523
is equivalent to

M_{\textnormal{P}}^{2}m^{2}\partial_{\phi}^{2}\frac{1}{m^{2}}\geq 1,

(524)

for
m^{2}=V^{\prime\prime}

Dudas et al. 2023
.
Based on this conjecture,
Gonzalo and Ibáñez 2019
derive a set of implications:

•

The axion decay constant is sub-Planckian:
f<M_{\textnormal{P}}
.

•

Scalar field potentials have to be linear and allow for trans-Planckian excursion.
418
418

418

Applying
eq.

523
to a linear potential
V(\phi)=a\phi+b
always satisfies the scalar WGC, even for trans-Planckian field values
\phi>M_{\textnormal{P}}
.

•

Massive scalars always have interaction channels beside gravity.
419
419

419

Equation

523
is always violated for purely quadratic potentials
V(\phi)=m^{2}\phi^{2}
. This rules out tachyonic instabilities in the potential
Brahma and Hossain 2019c
.

•

If inflation occurs in the form of large single field inflation, the tensor-to-scalar ratio is
r_{\textnormal{ts}}\approx 0.07
.

•

Neutrinos have normal mass ordering.

•

The lightest neutrino is Dirac with
m_{\nu_{1}}\lesssim\Lambda_{4}^{1/4}
.

The strong scalar WGCis disputed by
Freivogel et al. 2020
;
Benakli et al. 2020
.
The derivation of
eq.

523
from first principle is challenging, as it mixes long-range and short-range forces
Dall’Agata and Morittu 2020
;
Kusenko et al. 2020
. If the fourth derivative was absent, the condition itself were incompatible with the dSC
Kusenko et al. 2020
;
Shirai and Yamazaki 2019
. Furthermore, a dilute gas of non-relativistic atoms would violate
eq.

523

Freivogel et al. 2020
;
Dudas et al. 2023
.

A multi-field extension of the strong scalar WGCis presented by
Gonzalo and Ibáñez 2020
.
Their guiding principle is that any force mediator, e.g. photons or scalars, must be produced at a higher rate than gravitons. This notion is independent of BHphysics.
420
420

420

However, pair production is an important aspect of BHevaporation.

The proposal of pair production is stronger than the convex hull conjecture, as in the pair production proposal, actual particles are produced.
The inequality they derive is invariant under holomorphic transformations and valid for
n
complex moduli:

\frac{g{i\bar{j}}}{n}\absolutevalue{\left(\partial_im^2\right)\left(\partial_{\Bar{j}}m^2\right)-m^2\left(\partial_i\partial_{\Bar{j}}m^2\right)}\geq\frac{m^{4}}{M_{\textnormal{P}}^{2}}.

(525)

Does the WGCapply to all spin-states?

We discuss the different possible spin-states individually.

Spin-0

Palti 2020b
presents the magnetic WGC(
eq.

461
) for spin-0 states as

m_{\textnormal{t}}\sim\absolutevalue{\partial_\phi m_\textnormal{t}}M_{\textnormal{P}}

(526)

with
m_{\textnormal{t}}
the mass scale of an infinite tower of states, which is motivated by the EPand the DC.

Spin-1/2

Explicitly considering the spin,
Urbano 2018
shows that the WGCalso holds for spin-1/2 Dirac fermions.

Palti 2020b
presents the magnetic WGC(
eq.

461
) for spin-1/2 states as

m_{\textnormal{t}}\sim YM_{\textnormal{P}}

(527)

with
Y
is a renormalisable Yukawa coupling.
421
421

421

Gravitinos and Goldstinos do not have a renormalisable Yukawa coupling
Palti 2020b
.

Spin-1

Palti 2020b
presents the magnetic WGC(
eq.

461
) for spin-1 states as

m_{\textnormal{t}}\sim gM_{\textnormal{P}}.

(528)

Spin-2

Klaewer et al. 2019
present the magnetic WGC(
eq.

461
) for spin-2 states as

m_{\textnormal{t}}\sim m_{2}M_{\textnormal{P}}/M_{w},

(529)

with
m_{\textnormal{t}}
the cutoff of the mass term of the Stückelberg gauge field, and

M_{w}\leq M_{\textnormal{P}}
the interaction mass scale.
422
422

422

If there are several spin-2 fields, the cutoff is ambiguous unless the field with the weakest interaction scale is taken (that leads to the largest
M_{w}
)
Kundu et al. 2023
. Assuming
M_{w}\sim M_{\textnormal{P}}
implies
M_{\textnormal{t}}\sim m_{2}
, such that there is no parametric gap between the spin-2 state and an infinite tower of states
Kundu et al. 2023
.
Kundu et al. 2023
generally put theories with a massive spin-2 particle separated from an infinite tower into the swampland.

This bound is even applicable to massive gravitons, where the cutoff is of the order of the graviton mass
m_{\textnormal{t}}\sim m_{\textnormal{g}}
.
The conjecture rules out a continuous limit, in which QGcan be taken into a theory with two massless spin-2 fields
Klaewer et al. 2019
. For that case, i.e. for the graviton mass approaching 0, an infinite tower of (spin-2 or higher-spin) states is expected
Bachas 2019
.

De Luca et al. 2021
find the conjecture to hold in AdSvacua if the cosmological constant is much smaller than
m_{2}
, but not otherwise.
The conjecture is examined, and its assumptions tested by
de Rham et al. 2019
:

•

To derive the bound, it is assumed that the scaling between the helicity modes and the sources has a smooth massless limit. This is questioned by the observation that the interactions scale with negative powers of the spin-2–mass-term
van Dam and Veltman 1970
;
Vainshtein 1972
.

•

The identification
g\sim m_{2}/M_{w}
becomes meaningless in the
m_{2}\rightarrow 0
limit, as the interaction term blows up for fixed
M_{w}
.

•

Klaewer et al. 2019
did not consider the possibility of a mass gap between the light spin-2 states and the UVtower of states. Having no mass gap between the mass of the spin-2 particle of the EFTand the infinite tower that completes the UVtheory, means that the EFTis not under perturbative control.

•

The conjecture might be in tension with the EP.

•

The spin-2 WGCis invoked to avoid global symmetries, but the helicity-1 mode of the graviton is not a genuine vector field with a global U(1) charge. The U(1) symmetry proposed by
Klaewer et al. 2019
vanishes in unitary gauge, ergo, there is no global symmetry.

•

A counterexample to the spin-2 WGCis found in diffeomorphism-invariant couplings.

•

Counterexamples to the proposed cutoff of the spin-2 WGCare found in interacting spin-2 theories.

•

There are counterexamples to the spin-2 WGCin massive gravity
Bachas and Lavdas 2018a
;
Bachas 2019
;
Bachas and Lavdas 2018b
;
Bachas and Estes 2011
.

de Rham et al. 2019
conclude that the conjecture as presented by
Klaewer et al. 2019
is invalid. However, they stress that this does not rule out a WGCfor spin-2 states entirely:
For local and Lorentz-invariant massive spin-2 fields, they propose

m_{\textnormal{t}}\lesssim\left(m_{2}^{2}M_{w}\right)^{1/3}.

(530)

Furthermore, positivity bounds on the S-matrix yield a bound for the coupling:
g\lesssim\left(m/M_{w}\right)^{1/4}\ll 1

de Rham et al. 2018
.

Spin-J

Sammani and Saidi 2024
present a form of the WGCfor higher-spin states that matches the form of
eq.

460
.
A generalisation for higher-spin fields is also presented by
Porrati and Rahman 2009
.

Kaplan and Kundu 2021
present a necessary condition for particles with spin
J\geq 3
in 4d spacetime:

m_{\text{gr}}\lesssim\begin{cases}m_{J}\left(\frac{\absolutevalue{g_J}M_{\textnormal{P}}}{m_{J}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2\left(J-2\right)}}&\absolutevalue{g_J}\gtrsim\frac{m_{J}}{M_{\textnormal{P}}}\\
m_{J}&\absolutevalue{g_J}\lesssim\frac{m_{J}}{M_{\textnormal{P}}},\end{cases}

(531)

where
m_{J}
is the mass of an elementary particle
X_{J}
of spin
J\geq 3
,
m_{\text{gr}}
is the mass of a higher-spin particle in the gravity sector (which implies that the particle
X_{J}
of mass
m_{J}
can be one of a non-gravitational QFT). The conjecture is that the particle
X_{J}
can only couple to gravity, if there is a higher-spin state in the gravitational sector that satisfies the above bound. If we take
m_{\text{gr}}
as a bound
\Lambda_{\text{gr}}
, which we integrate out, then we obtain a low-energy QFT, which obviously breaks down for
\absolutevalue{g_J}\lesssim\frac{m_{J}}{M_{\textnormal{P}}}
, as at this point non-gravitational interactions are weaker than gravitational interactions, and it was not valid to integrate out / decouple gravity in this regime. Above
\Lambda_{\text{gr}}
, stringy effects appear, ergo, this scale can also be interpreted as the string scale.

Does the WGCalso apply to multi-particle states?

In a theory with
N
charges, each particle state is associated with a vector
\vec{z}=\frac{1}{m}\left(q_{1},\dots,q_{N}\right)
.
It is not sufficient that one species
i
with
\absolutevalue{\Vec{z_i}}>1
exists, as orthogonal states could still form stable BHs
Cheung and Remmen 2014a
.
It is also not sufficient to have a species
i
for each U(1) individually with
\absolutevalue{\Vec{z_i}}>1
, as combined states could exist that would form stable BHs
Cheung and Remmen 2014a
;
Heidenreich et al. 2015
.
Moreover, it is also not sufficient that every KKmode on its own is superextremal
Harlow et al. 2023
, as you might be able to create multi-particle states that form stable BHs, as in
fig.

6
.

Figure 6:
Reducing a higher-dimensional parent theory onto a circle
S^{1}
leads to superextremal KK-modes. If the circle is too small (right figure), intermediate states can violate the convex hull conjecture. Figure adapted from
Harlow et al. 2023
.

In a theory with
N
charges and different couplings
g_{i}
, there is a charge vector
\vec{q}=\left(q_{1}g_{1},...,q_{N}g_{N}\right)
which yields an effective charge of
q_{\text{eff}}=\sqrt{q_{1}^{2}/g_{1}^{2}+\dots+q_{N}^{2}/g_{N}^{2}}\leq q_{1}/g_{1}+\dots+q_{N}/g_{N}

Heidenreich et al. 2015
;
van Beest et al. 2022
;
Palti 2020a
.
To ascertain that extremal BHscan decay, the convex hull of the charge-to-mass ratio vector

\vec{z}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\vec{q}\cdot M_{\textnormal{P}}/m

(532)

has to include the unit ball, i.e.

\absolutevalue{\Vec{z}}>1

(533)

is required
Palti 2019
;
Cheung and Remmen 2014a
;
Saraswat 2017
;
Palti 2020a
;
van Beest et al. 2022
.
423
423

423

With
N
identical charges, this gives the bound
m\leq q/\sqrt{N}

Saraswat 2017
. From the species scale (
eqs.

276
and
II.14
) we know that
\delta M_{\textnormal{P}}^{2}\sim N\Lambda^{2}
. It follows that for large
N
, the WGCbound is independent of
N
, since the contributions just cancel, as was already noted by
Cheung and Remmen 2014a
.

This is known as the
convex hull conjecture
.

The magnetic version of the convex hull conjecture demands that a monopole charged under all symmetry groups is not a BH, i.e.
\Lambda\lesssim M_{\textnormal{P}}/\sqrt{\frac{1}{g_{1}^{2}}+\dots+\frac{1}{g_{N}^{2}}}

Saraswat 2017
.
424
424

424

For identical gauge couplings, this yields
\Lambda\lesssim M_{\textnormal{P}}\frac{g}{\sqrt{N}}

Saraswat 2017
. The latter cutoff was already conjectured early on by
Huang 2008b
.

What is the Lattice WGC?

The motivation for the stronger form of the WGCcomes from the observation that the WGCcan fail after compactification if there are different charges. The proposed remedy is that the lightest state in any charge-direction is superextremal
Heidenreich et al. 2016a
.
This condition implies the convex hull conjecture, and an infinite number of charged particles with potentially super-Planckian masses — which can be interpreted as extremal BHs, if the higher-order corrections to the extremality bound reduce the mass of extremal BHs
Heidenreich et al. 2016a
(which is likely the case, as we have reasoned in
section

II.19.1
).

A more formal definition of the LWGCis the following:

For every point
\vec{q}
on the charge lattice, there is a particle of charge
q
with charge-to-mass ratio at least as large as that of a large, semi-classical, non-rotating extremal black hole with charge
\vec{q}_{\text{BH}}\propto\vec{q}

Heidenreich et al. 2016a
.

This means that there is a particle lighter than
qM_{\textnormal{P}}
, a particle lighter than
2qM_{\textnormal{P}}
, a particle lighter than
3qM_{\textnormal{P}}
, a particle lighter …
Heidenreich et al. 2016b
.

This conjecture does not hold, though, as there are counterexamples to this conjecture
Heidenreich et al. 2015
;
Harlow et al. 2023
;
Heidenreich et al. 2017
;
Heidenreich et al. 2018b
.

What is the Tower WGC?

The convex hull conjecture might be violated after circle compactification (onto a small enough circle)
425
425

425

Cota et al. 2024
question the necessity of a tower of states below a certain compactification size. They argue that a theory remains consistent if it is compactified onto a circle below a certain threshold, even without the appearance of a tower of states. An exception to this exception is the tower of states that appears — as requested by the EP — in the weak-coupling limit of the heterotic string
Cota et al. 2024
.

Harlow et al. 2023
;
Heidenreich et al. 2016a
;
Cota et al. 2022
.
426
426

426

This form of dimensional reduction is not per se a counterexample to the WGCbecause it first needs to be checked if the higher-dimensional parent theory is a valid theory of QGat all
Harlow et al. 2023
, as an infinite number of charged particles indicates that the theory is only valid up to a cutoff scale, which itself indicates the appearance of new particles, e.g. massive KKmodes or strings, which might satisfy the WGC, i.e. they allow BHsto decay
Heidenreich et al. 2016a
.

To avoid this violation, an infinite tower of WGCsatisfying states is necessary.
427
427

427

The tower is predicted to start from a mass scale
\Lambda

Cota et al. 2023
: if the tower starts below the cutoff scale, the species scale (
section

II.14
) is lower, due to the additional species present. A mass scale below the cutoff scale was compatible with the mild form of the WGCitself, i.e. no internal inconsistency would arise; however, there might be a tension with stronger versions, as for higher masses in the tower, superextremal BHswere to be expected.

The existence of such a tower is predicted by the TWGC.
428
428

428

An infinite tower of states beyond the cutoff scale is also predicted by the DCand corresponds well to an expectation from duality: Far away from a theory at
\phi_{1}
in moduli space, an infinite tower of heavy particles arises, with a mass
m\sim e^{\absolutevalue{\mathfrak{a}}d(\phi_{1},\phi_{2})}
— where we use the same symbols as in
section

II.5
with
d(\phi_{1},\phi_{2})
the geodesic distance in moduli space
\mathcal{M}
between
\phi_{1}
and
\phi_{2}
— and
\absolutevalue{\mathfrak{a}}\geq 1/\sqrt{d-2}

Etheredge 2023
.
That at least one of the towers that emerge is one of superextremal states can be motivated from entropy arguments
Cota et al. 2022
;
Arkani-Hamed et al. 2007b
. Furthermore, the EPpredicts the appearance of a charged tower in the asymptotic limit of vanishing gauge coupling
Cota et al. 2022
;
Palti 2019
;
Harlow 2016
;
Heidenreich et al. 2018a
;
Grimm et al. 2018
.

A formal definition of the TWGCreads as follows:

For every site in the charge lattice,
\vec{q}\in\Gamma
, there exists a positive integer
n
such that there is a superextremal particle of charge
n\vec{q}

Heidenreich et al. 2019
;
Harlow et al. 2023
.

The TWGCis stronger than the convex hull conjecture, as additional constraints are required, but weaker than the sLWGC, as the tower can have gaps, i.e. it is not required that the (sub-)lattice is filled
Andriolo et al. 2018
.

The tower can be narrow and sharply defined at weak coupling, but its meaning becomes blurry for strong coupling
Heidenreich et al. 2019
. It can be satisfied by unstable resonances
Enriquez-Rojo and Plauschinn 2020
,
but not by multi-particle states
Heidenreich et al. 2019
;
Harlow et al. 2023
. Unstable resonances do not correspond to states in Hilbert space, but to localised peaks in the S-matrix of a scattering process: for weak coupling this means that the lifetime of the particle is long and the peak itself is localised at a narrow energy scale, which corresponds to the mass of the unstable particle; for strong coupling the peak is spread out, which makes it difficult to define a mass and therefore also challenging to define the meaning of the TWGC
Harlow et al. 2023
.
429
429

429

The mass of a resonance, i.e. the location of a pole in the complex energy plane of the S-matrix, is a complex quantity, which introduces an ambiguity in the interpretation of the conjecture
Heidenreich et al. 2017
.

If it was an infinite tower of stable states, one of the arguments in favour of the WGCpresented by
Arkani-Hamed et al. 2007b
would break down.
430
430

430

Heavy towers can be unstable, and the validity of the tower scalar WGCis still under debate
Etheredge 2023
.

From the claim
m/q<1
, they deduce that the number of exactly stable particles in QGin asymptotically flat space is finite (except for BPSstates, but those are bound), which implies that even for neutral particles the number of massless degrees of freedom is finite. We wonder if an infinite tower of stable states would imply infinite degrees of freedom for some particles.

A potential
431
431

431

The full spectrum was not computed, which might remedy the observed disagreement with the conjecture.

counterexample to the TWGCcomes from 4d F-theory, where an infinite tower of states appears that does not satisfy the bounds presented by the TWGC
Heidenreich et al. 2019
;
Lee et al. 2019c
. The culprit is the dimensionality: The TWGCin
D
dimension is closely related to the WGCin
D-1
dimensions, and it is not clear if the WGCshould hold at all in
d<4

Heidenreich et al. 2019
, as there are no asymptotically flat BHs
Harlow et al. 2023
.
432
432

432

See also
footnote

439
about dimensionality.

What is the sub-Lattice WGC?

The sLWGCis a stronger conjecture than the TWGC, as it requires a superextremal particle at each site of a full-dimensional sub-lattice of the charge lattice
Hayashi et al. 2023
;
Lee et al. 2018
;
Heidenreich et al. 2017
, which means that the integer
n
from the TWGCis independent of
\vec{q}

Heidenreich et al. 2019
;
Harlow et al. 2023
.
This reflects the principle of completeness
Harlow 2016
;
Lee et al. 2018
;
Klaewer and Palti 2017
;
Enriquez-Rojo and Plauschinn 2020
(see
section

II.3
) and implies the TWGC
Alim et al. 2021
.
A formal definition of the sLWGCis the following:

There exists a positive integer
n
such that for any site in the charge lattice,
\vec{q}\in\Gamma
, there is a superextremal particle of charge
n\vec{q}

Heidenreich et al. 2019
;
Harlow et al. 2023
.

If a theory saturates a sLWGCbound, gravity and the gauge theory become strongly coupled at the same parametric energy scale, i.e. when the charged particles become increasingly broad, the density of states of different charges must behave nicely, such that gravity and electromagnetism become strong at the same scale — this implies that a particle exists which satisfies the WGCand it suggests a unification of forces
Heidenreich et al. 2018b
. This explains how a gauge theory can emerge: the low-energy coupling is small, because of the UVdynamics of heavy particles
Heidenreich et al. 2018b
.
The sLWGCpredicts the existence of an energy scale
\Lambda_{\text{gauge}}<\Lambda_{\text{QG}}
at which the gauge coupling becomes strong — the mass of every charged and weakly coupled particle is bounded by

m<\Lambda_{\text{gauge}}\lesssim e^{2}\expectationvalue{q^2}_{\Lambda_{\text{gauge}}}M_{\textnormal{P}}^{D-2}

(534)

where the mass of every particle is bounded by the
average
charge of the particles, i.e. particles lighter than
\Lambda_{\text{gauge}}
are on average superextremal
Heidenreich et al. 2018b
.

There is a loophole in this variation of the WGC: since the lattice can be made arbitrarily sparse, the constraining power of this form of the WGCcan be arbitrarily diluted, as the charges for arbitrarily sparse lattices become arbitrarily massive
Heckman et al. 2024b
;
Arkani-Hamed et al. 2007b
;
Harlow et al. 2023
;
Brown et al. 2016
;
Montero et al. 2015
;
Rudelius 2015b
.
433
433

433

Harlow et al. 2023
state that it’s plausible that the coarseness is always of
\order{1}
; they did not encounter any cases with
k>3
, but also mention that it’s an open question how exactly the coarseness of the sub-lattice manifests itself in the IRphysics.

Hebecker et al. 2019b
find that (mis-)aligned winding modes can produce settings with arbitrarily large coarseness, which would parametrically violate the sLWGC.

Hebecker et al. 2019c
present a setup with axions in a warped throat, where the sub-lattice of states can be made parametrically coarse.
A very sparse sub-lattice might also be realised through Higgsing, with superextremal particles with masses even above the magnetic WGC
Harlow et al. 2023
. However,
Heidenreich et al. 2017
state that the sLWGCis not robust under Higgsing, which leaves two options:
First, the WGC — in general, and not only the sLWGC — is not suitable to constrain IRphysics because if the conjecture is not robust under Higgsing, it means that the full, un-Higgsed charge lattice needs to be known, i.e. the UVphysics need to be known.
Second, the sLWGCconstrains any IRphases of a theory because there is no sharp distinction between a Higgsed gauge group and a massive U(1), i.e. the gauge group is well-defined in the IR, whereas it is not entirely clear what
full charge lattice
actually means.

Heidenreich et al. 2017
;
Saraswat 2017
suggest that the coarseness
k
changes the cutoff scale to
\Lambda\lesssim gkM_{\textnormal{P}}
.

Casas et al. 2024c
study
gonions
434
434

434

See
footnote

188
.

Aldazabal et al. 2001
in a type IIA setting, and find that the towers of gonions do not satisfy the TWGC/sLWGC — but do also not violate the convex hull conjecture under compactification —, as all the elements of the tower have the same charge. In a setting of one dominant gonion tower with two decompactified dimensions, they find the gonion mass
m_{\textnormal{g}}
to scale like
m_{\textnormal{g}}\sim g^{2}M_{\textnormal{P}}
, such that the WGCis not saturated, as the mass is suppressed by
g^{2}
and not just
g
, i.e. the gonion masses are not extremal.

A potential direction to attack the sLWGCmight be through (quasi-)modular elliptic genera, as they contain gaps in the charge spectrum
Lee et al. 2019c
.
435
435

435

The work by
Lee et al. 2019c
is not a robust counterexample to the sLWGC, as the results are only valid in the weak coupling limit.

The briefly mentioned 4d F-theory counterexample to the TWGCwould also be a counterexample to the sLWGC. It appears that the possibility of large logarithmic terms in loop diagrams require a modification of the sLWGCin 4 dimensions, compared to higher-dimensional theories
Heidenreich and Lotito 2024
;
Heidenreich et al. 2017
;
Heidenreich et al. 2018b
;
Lee et al. 2019c
;
Klaewer et al. 2021
.

Does the WGCapply to strings and branes as well?

Equations

460
and
461
can be generalised for arbitrary
p
-form fields in any dimension
d
.
436
436

436

p
-form gauge fields bring charges to
p-1
-dimensional objects (
p-1
-branes), with 0-branes being point like particles and 1-branes being 1-dimensional strings
Rudelius 2023c
.

The electric WGC,
eq.

460
, reads as:

\frac{p\left(d-p-2\right)}{d-2}\mathcal{T}^{2}\leq g^{2}q^{2}M_{\textnormal{P}}^{d-2}

(535)

with
\mathcal{T}
the tension
437
437

437

The tension has units of mass per length
Rudelius 2023c
.

of the state
Bonnefoy et al. 2021
, which reduces to the mass for a
1
-form field.
438
438

438

The WGCdoes
not
weaken in higher-dimensional settings.
Furthermore, for
p=0
and
p=d-2
, no charged black object exists
Heidenreich et al. 2016a
.

The magnetic WGC,
eq.

461
, can be expressed for a
(d-p-3)
-brane charged under a
p
-form gauge field:

\Lambda\lesssim\left(g^{2}M_{\textnormal{P}}^{d-2}\right)^{1/2p};

(536)

the tension is approximately

\mathcal{T}\sim\Lambda^{p}/g^{2}\gtrsim\mathcal{T}_{\text{BB}}\sim M_{\textnormal{P}}^{d-2}r_{\heartsuit}^{p},

(537)

where
\mathcal{T}_{\text{BB}}
is the tension of a black brane with Schwarzschild radius
r_{\heartsuit}

Harlow et al. 2023
;
Hebecker et al. 2017c
.

Further Comments

We mostly focussed on mass limits, but the mass-to-
charge
ratio has two components.
Imposing an upper bound on the charge has not been extensively explored in string theory
Harlow et al. 2023
, but some considerations and implications are presented by
Ibanez and Montero 2018
.

The WGCshould hold everywhere in physical space
Klaewer and Palti 2017
: To say that gravity is the weakest force is a local statement. Demanding that BHscan decay means that a particle can escape to infinity, i.e. to the horizon. It is natural to expect that the conjecture holds everywhere in between as well.
Studying the WGCin a non-local context (in Lee–Wick Quantum Electrodynamics (QED)
Lee and Wick 1969
;
Lee and Wick 1970
;
Grinstein et al. 2008
),
Abu-Ajamieh and Chattopadhyay 2024
find that in the regime of non-locality, the WGCbounds become even stronger, as the electric force gets modified at high energies.
A similar finding is presented by
Abu-Ajamieh et al. 2024c
:
The non-local electric force is given by

F(r)=-\frac{q}{4\pi r^{2}}\erf\left(\frac{r\Lambda_{\text{nl}}}{2}\right)+\frac{q\Lambda_{\text{nl}}}{4\pi^{3/2}r}\exp\left(-\frac{r^{2}\Lambda_{\text{nl}}^{2}}{4}\right),

(538)

with
\Lambda_{\text{nl}}
the energy scale of non-locality.
A modified, non-local WGCfollows from requiring that gravity is weaker than the non-local electric force:

\displaystyle m

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{2}qM_{\textnormal{P}}\sqrt{\erf\left(\frac{r\Lambda_{\text{nl}}}{2}\right)-\frac{r\Lambda_{\text{nl}}}{\sqrt{\pi}}\exp\left(-\frac{r^{2}\Lambda_{\text{nl}}^{2}}{4}\right)}

(539)

In the low-energy limit
r\Lambda_{\text{nl}}\gg 1
, the non-local WGCagrees with the WGC.
In the high-energy limit, the non-local version is more stringent, as the non-local factor becomes smaller.

The WGCholds under dimensional reduction
Rudelius 2021a
.

If we want to be able to measure the change in charge of a BH,
\delta Q=qg
, when throwing in an additional particle,
\delta Q
needs to be larger than the change in quantum fluctuations due to this additional particle,
\delta\sqrt{\expectationvalue{Q^2}}\sim Ggm/\delta t

Schadow 2018
. This leads to the following inequality, which motivates the WGC:

\displaystyle\delta\sqrt{\expectationvalue{Q^2}}

\displaystyle\sim Ggm/\delta t

(540)

\displaystyle<Ggm/t_{\textnormal{P}}

\displaystyle=gm/M_{\textnormal{P}}

(541)

\displaystyle<qg

(542)

\displaystyle\Rightarrow m/M_{\textnormal{P}}

\displaystyle<q.

(543)

Exploring a new angle of implications of the WGC,
Freivogel et al. 2020
formulate the
Bound State Conjecture
:

In a theory where the heaviest stable particle has mass
m
, it is impossible to construct adiabatically a black hole that is parametrically smaller than the black hole that can be built from free scalars of the same mass
m
.

Gauge forces should not allow for stable bound states that are smaller than a gravitationally bound state would permit, i.e. attractive gauge forces cannot form stars or other objects that decay to collapse into BHsthat are arbitrarily small. This also means that we cannot use an efficient, adiabatic IRprocess that gathers enough particles to create a UVexcitation. The conjecture does not rule out bound states per se, it rather stresses that gauge forces cannot parametrically increase the binding force. BHswith
r\lesssim 1/m
can only form via Hawking radiation (and we would assume processes like Schwinger pair production, even though the latter is not explicitly stated by the authors). The conjecture is weaker than the RFCor the scalar WGC, as the bound state conjecture does not rule out bound states entirely, but limits them. Attractive forces are allowed, but cannot be parametrically stronger than gravity. However, it is a stronger conjecture regarding other aspects, namely, that it also holds in the absence of gravity. The bound is explicitly independent of
M_{\textnormal{P}}
. It is also independent of the spacetime dimension
d
.
